# 0024. Three independent learning products: Individual courses, Learning Paths and day-wise Journeys, each with its own enrolment, official attempt, progress, rewards and reports on one module library

Status: Proposed. The owner answered every decision D0 to D17 on 2026-11-12 and then said "lift the hold and start phase 0"; that sentence started this record and accepted nothing. On 2026-11-13 the owner answered the eight items that needed an explicit word and asked for the conflicts, the final timeline and the release dependencies before implementation; that decided the eight items and did not accept the record either. Later on 2026-11-13 the owner answered the seven items that then needed an explicit word (A36, A32, A34, A33, R16, R13 with R14, R21) and made a restore rehearsal mandatory before the production sitting; five are decided, A34 is agreed "subject to verification" and open for two values and one confirmation, and A33 needs one clarification (section "Decisions requested"). The record is still not accepted. Nothing here is built, applied or deployed. No code, migration or test is written before the owner accepts this record.
Date: 2026-11-12
Deciders: owner; architect

This record supersedes the ADR 0023 draft (never accepted; filed beside this record as `0023-learning-context.md`). Numbers: migrations `0107_learning_contexts.sql` (core), `0108_drop_context_derive.sql`, `0109_product_reporting.sql` (0101 and 0102 stay reserved for P5); lifecycle specs 68 onward (ADR 0022 reserves 66 and 67; 66 is on `main`). No branch claims any of these numbers (checked 2026-11-12). Each number is confirmed against `main` when its pull request opens.

### The owner's requirement

The owner's answer of 2026-11-11, verbatim:

> A Learning Path is a separate learning product, not simply a collection of individual courses.
>
> If a learner has already completed a course individually through the Library, that completion must NOT count towards the same course when it is included in a Learning Path. The learner must complete it again within the Learning Path.
>
> Therefore, Individual Course Progress and Learning Path Progress must be tracked independently, even when both use the same course content.
>
> The one-official-attempt rule should also be maintained separately for each learning context.
>
> Most importantly, the Continue button, chapter lock/unlock logic, Learning Path progress, dashboards and reports must all follow the same path-specific completion rule.
>
> Once a learner completes the official attempt launched from the Learning Path, the next chapter should unlock permanently and remain accessible when they return to the Learning Path.

The plan widened this, with the owner's agreement, to three products: the Individual course, each Learning Path, and each day of a learner's Journey.

The CRM ID requirement (owner, 2026-11-12, in substance): three separate product ids, `course_id` (MOD code), `path_id` (PTH code) and `journey_id` (JUR code) with `enrollment_id` per run. Yoddha must be able to (1) open one learner and see all their learning across the three products, with enrolment, progress, completion and assessment scores, and (2) fetch one product's data by its own id.

Also recorded, not decisions: deleting a path or journey program that has learner attempts is refused (archive instead, 409); the ADR 0023 draft is filed as superseded.

The binding policies keep their content and change only their scope: one official attempt, now per learner per context; unlimited review-only revision visits, counted, never scored, no practice mode; an extra official attempt only by an administrator's grant, the history kept; tries only as the author configured; a final fail never becomes a pass (ADR 0017, per attempt row).

It is not part of this record's phases: a small change to `scripts/lifecycle-nightly.sh` in a pull request of its own. The build step is run again once, and only when its output shows the font download failing; any other build error, and a second failure of any kind, fails the night as today; the run's summary says that a retry happened, and the first attempt's output is kept in the log. **Corrected by the check of the round-5 fold (2026-11-13): that trigger was narrower than the owner's words and was not marked as a reading.** The owner named "Google Fonts/network-related" failures. The retry is made when the build log shows a transient network failure: the font download failing, or another network error in the build (a timeout, a DNS failure, a reset or refused connection). The exact patterns are the executor's and are a reading of the owner's words (reading R24). A compile, type or module error is never a trigger, and when the log shows one beside a network error the night fails with no retry, so a genuine failure is never retried away. Under the script's public-log rule (`scripts/lifecycle-nightly.sh:26-35`) the first attempt's build log stays in the run's output folder as a file of its own and is never uploaded; what is printed is the masked excerpt of at most 40 lines, as for any build failure (`:104-110`), and one line saying that the build was retried once and why | Recorded here only |

What the B2 reading means is in step 83: with every version kept, no backup copy is ever deleted, so the 90-day ceiling is not met for backup copies today.

### Words

- **Product**: the Individual course, a Learning Path, or a day-wise Journey.
- **Context**: where one attempt belongs. One of `individual`, `path:<learning_path_id>`, `journey:<journey_enrollment_id>:<day id>`, or a preview key (`preview`, or `preview:<test run id>` with the day id for a journey test run; step 5). Written when the attempt row is inserted and never changed. The day in a journey key is the day's identity, not its number (section U; reading R16).
- **Preview data**: every row a Preview launch or a test run writes (step 80). It is its own context, counts nowhere official, and is deleted on request and by age (section S).
- **Test run**: a staff member's walk through a whole path or journey in Preview, recorded only as preview attempts under one `preview_runs` row, apart from the activity days marked done inside a journey run (decision A41, from phase G; section T).
- **Official attempt, revision visit, granted retake**: ADR 0014's terms, read per (learner, course, context).
- **Official rule**: within one (learner, course, context), the rows that count are those that are not abandoned, not revision rows and not previews. One rule, used by SQL and TypeScript alike (step 9).
- **Done**: the context's official result satisfies the resolved `pass_required` (step 10). For a path step, done means `passed_ever` (step 44).
- **Step**: a `learning_path_courses` row. **Day**: one entry of a frozen `journey_versions.days`, identified across versions by its day id (`journey_versions.day_ids`, step 91); its number is its position in one version.
- **Recorded enrolment**: a row in `course_assignments`, `learning_path_assignments` or `journey_enrollments`. Under D15 a self-enrolment is such a row; nothing is implied from an attempt.

Production runs this code and has no learners and no courses, so nothing has gone wrong on real data. Staging holds about 630 test attempts.

### Why one record

The ADR 0023 draft is right about Learning Paths and leaves Journeys, rewards, previews, the webhook and the emails on the shared result. Extending it would keep two rules alive. This record keeps what the draft got right (per-context official attempt, visits and grants; one pure result function with a guard test; the open-attempt index split; paste-first with an either-state check) and replaces the rest. Section "Alternatives considered" lists what changes.

## Decision

Goal: every learner record carries one fixed product context; "official" is decided once per context by one rule; one database function records completion side effects; each product reads only its own records through one progress rule; a read-only Overview shows the three side by side.

Out of scope: any change to the content engines (KIVO, SCORM, third-party cmi5); SCORM 2004; per-product attempt tables; yearly run instances (D5); a per-consumer webhook address; lifting the one-attempt policy clamp; the Guardian track (ADR 0022) and P5; any rewrite or move of a learner row; any deletion of a learner row other than preview data, which the owner asked to have deleted (R7, section S).

### A. The model

1. **Principles.**
   1. One content library, one attempt table, one context per attempt row, fixed at insert.
   2. "Official" is derived, never stored. It is computed by one SQL view family and one TypeScript function, with one SQL helper, and a parity test keeps them equal.
   3. One database function writes completion side effects (points, journey day credit, path completion, the email claim). It fails closed and a sweep retries it.
   4. Each product has one progress rule, in one SQL object with one TypeScript twin. No reader of one product reads another product's rows. Three deliberate exceptions: rule R-3 of the statements route (a statement naming another attempt of the same learner on the same course is answered and dropped, `statements:132-166`, LIVE); the Overview's unique-learner count; and the paid points key per course, which the owner chose under D8.
   5. History is never rewritten (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 3). No row moves between contexts. Foreign keys refuse a delete that would erase a context. The 0107 backfill writes only new columns: a label derived from each row's own columns and two "already handled" markers. No existing column value on any learner row changes. One departure, asked for by the owner under R7 and named in section S: preview data, and only preview data written by a Preview launch or a test run, is deleted on request and by age. No official row is ever deleted, and the purge proves it on every run (step 81).
   6. Enrolment stays in the three tables that exist today. A self-enrolment is a recorded row in the same table (D15).
   7. A preview is its own context and counts nowhere. Preview progress is derived from preview attempt rows only (one exception from phase G: an activity day marked done on the test run's own row, decision A41); a preview writes no row in any enrolment, progress, completion, points, badge, notice or outbox table (step 80).

2. **The model.**

   | Product | Recorded enrolment | Context on each attempt | Progress rule (SQL, TypeScript twin) | Rewards |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | Individual course | `course_assignments` (user, team, org, group; `release_at`, `due_at`; `source` `admin` or `self`) expanded by `v_course_enrollments_expanded`, direct arms only | `individual` | `v_individual_enrollment_status`; `contextResult({kind:'individual'})` | paid once per course; a 0-point event row for this context (D8) |
   | Learning Path | `learning_path_assignments` (`source` `admin` or `self`) expanded by `v_path_enrollments_expanded`, with the step list `learning_path_courses` | `path:<path id>` | `v_path_step_status`, `v_path_progress`, the stored `learning_path_completions` row; `pathProgress()` | the same paid keys; a 0-point event row per path context |
   | Day-wise Journey | `journey_enrollments` (`source` `admin`, `audience` or `self`) pinned to a `journey_versions` row; the deadline read live from `journey_programs.deadline_days` (D17) | `journey:<enrolment id>:<day id>` | `journey_day_progress` keyed by day id, `journey_enrollment_state()`, `v_journey_progress`; `computeJourneyState()` with the failed state | no points; the badge per enrolment (D9) |
   | Preview and test runs | none; a test run has one `preview_runs` row (ids and dates only) | `preview`, or `preview:<run id>` with the day id for a journey run | none official; excluded everywhere. Preview Reports derive a run's progress from its preview attempts with the same pure functions (sections S, T) | none |

3. **New files and folders** (structural; recorded here as `CLAUDE.md` requires): the folder `lib/paths/` with `progress.ts` (`pathProgress()`); `lib/attempts/launch-context.ts` (`resolveLaunchContext()`); `lib/scoring/context.ts` (`contextResult()`, pure) and `lib/scoring/context-read.ts` (`readContextResult()`, server only); `app/[org]/(admin)/overview/` (the Overview page); `app/api/journey/self-enroll/route.ts` (Start journey); `app/api/journey/learners/export/route.ts` (the journey CSV). Added on 2026-11-13 for sections S and T: the folder `lib/preview/` (`runs.ts`, the test-run reads and the pure run state; `purge.ts`, the caller of the purge function); `app/[org]/(admin)/preview-reports/` (Preview Reports and the test-run pages); `app/api/preview/runs/route.ts` (start a test run, move its simulated date) and `app/api/preview/purge/route.ts` (manual delete). No new library.

### B. Migration 0107, `0107_learning_contexts.sql` (the core)

Hand-pasted in the SQL editor, staging first. One transaction, `lock_timeout = '5s'`, idempotent, ending in `notify pgrst, 'reload schema'`. Every new relation carries RLS, a policy and literal grants in the file; every function carries a literal `revoke execute ... from public, anon, authenticated` and `grant execute ... to service_role` unless a step says otherwise. Every function that 0107, 0108 or 0109 creates or recreates carries `set search_path = pg_catalog, public` (the form of `0100:258`) and names its relations with their schema; `scripts/check-migration-grants.mjs` does not check this, so the privilege-matrix after-check does (step 19). `node scripts/check-migration-grants.mjs` must pass.

**Creation order inside the file (binding).** Steps 4 to 19 below are numbered by subject, not by position in the file. Creating a view binds the relations it reads at once, and a `language sql` function is checked against its objects when it is created; a view or such a function placed before something it reads stops the paste with 42P01. A plpgsql body is only parsed at creation and resolves its objects when it first runs. The file is therefore written in this order:

   1. The header, the prerequisite DO block and the before-checks (step 4).
   2. Columns, run-once backfills, generated columns and CHECKs on `course_attempts` (steps 5 and 7), in this order, because a backfill that reads a generated column stops the paste with 42703 if the column is not there yet: `context_kind` with its backfill, `not null` and CHECK; then the generated `context_key`; then the shape CHECK; then the generated `settle_sig`, which has no backfill of its own; then `side_effects_sig` and `side_effects_at` with their backfill, which reads `settle_sig`; then `webhook_skip_reason` with its backfill. Then the other changed tables (step 8), each generated `context_key` after the column it is computed from; the `revision_visits` and `journey_day_progress` backfills read `course_attempts.context_key` and `context_kind`, so they come after those exist and are filled. Then `context_derive_log`; the two trigger functions (plpgsql) and their triggers, created only after every backfill of this item has run, because the immutability trigger would refuse the `context_kind` backfill itself; the foreign keys; the indexes, each new unique index before the old one is dropped.
   3. The new tables with their RLS, policy and grants: `learning_path_completions` (step 14) with its trigger function `learning_path_completions_append_only()` and its trigger, then `completion_notices` (step 15).
   4. The views, in this order: `v_context_attempt_scoring`; `v_context_attempt_summary`; the two old-name views (`create or replace`, columns unchanged); `v_course_enrollments_expanded` (step 18); `v_course_enrollment_terms` and `v_path_enrollment_terms` (step 18); `v_path_step_status`; `v_path_progress` (step 9). The two path views come last because they read the terms view and `learning_path_completions`.
   5. The `language sql` functions, each after what it reads: `official_attempt_in_context` (after the scoring view), `instance_pass_required`, `journey_credit_state` (step 13), `journey_reminder_counts` (after `journey_credit_state`).
   6. The plpgsql functions, in any order among themselves, because each names objects of this file or another function of it: `consume_grant`, both forms of `record_revision_visit`, `journey_complete_enrollments_core` with its two wrappers, `journey_record_completion`, `record_learning_activity`, `sweep_completion_side_effects`, the two refresh functions, the XP core, its wrapper and `gamification_close_month`, `journey_auto_enroll`, and `launch_context` (which reads the path views). The functions of step 13 are plpgsql for this reason: they write `completion_notices`. No function of this file that names a later object may be `language sql`.
   7. Restated grants, the after-checks (step 19) and `notify pgrst`.

   **Objects added on 2026-11-13 (sections S and U) and where they sit in this order.** In item 2, before the generated `context_key`, because the key reads them: `journey_days.removed_at` and the relaxed `day_number`; `journey_versions.day_ids` and `day_ids_source` with their backfill; `preview_runs` (a table, created here because an attempt column references it; its RLS, policy and grants with it); `course_attempts.journey_day_id` with its backfill; `course_attempts.preview_kind` with its backfill, and `preview_run_id`. Then `context_key`, then the shape CHECK. With the other changed tables of item 2: `attempt_requests.journey_day_id`; `journey_day_progress.day_id` with its backfill, the new unique key created before the old one is dropped. In item 3: `preview_settings` and `preview_purge_log`. In item 6 (plpgsql): `journey_save_days`, `journey_repin_enrollments`, `purge_preview_data`, and the paste-window trigger function that fills `journey_versions.day_ids`.

   **Objects added by the independent review of round 4, and where they sit.** In item 2, before the shape CHECK, beside `journey_day_id` (the CHECK names it; corrected in round 5, the text said "before the immutability trigger", which would have allowed it after the CHECK): `course_attempts.journey_version_id` (no backfill; step 5). With `preview_runs`: its column `done_activity_day_ids` (step 79). In item 3: the one `preview_settings` row, inserted in the run-once block that creates the table, and `preview_purge_log.excluded` (step 79). In item 5, after `instance_pass_required`: `journey_attempt_satisfies` (step 13). In item 6: the day-id form of `consume_grant` beside the old form, which becomes a wrapper (step 10). Each is in the privilege matrix of step 19, the reverse script of step 61 and the rehearsal of step 63.

   **Objects added in round 5 (2026-11-13), and where they sit.** In item 2, with the indexes: the foreign-key indexes of step 7 on the changed tables (seven as first written; seventeen in all after the check of the fold: seven on `course_attempts`, `attempt_requests` and `journey_day_progress` here, and ten on `preview_runs`, `learning_path_completions`, `completion_notices` and `preview_purge_log`, each created with its table, in items 2 and 3). In item 3, with `preview_purge_log`: its trigger function `preview_purge_log_append_only()` (plpgsql, the form of `learning_path_completions_append_only()`), created before its trigger; with `preview_settings`: the columns `backup_days` and `purge_legacy` and the CHECK that ties `live_days` to `backup_days` (step 79). No new table, view or migration file. `journey_attempt_satisfies` gains an argument and `purge_preview_data` an arm (steps 13, 81); the old form of `consume_grant` locks the grant before the enrolment (step 10).

   The PGlite rehearsal (step 63) runs the file top to bottom on a database at 0106 where none of 0107's objects exist yet (every scenario applies the migration chain first), so a wrong order fails there before any paste.

4. **Header and prerequisite block.**
   - The never-paste-again list. After 0107 none of these is pasted again, because 0107 supersedes an object each defines: 0034 (the attempt foreign key); 0035 and 0056 (the two refresh functions, step 16); 0053 (`gamification_record_activity` at `:311`, `gamification_close_month` at `:886`; a re-paste overwrites the wrapper, `0058:202-206`); 0058 (the wrapper `:216`, `journey_record_completion` `:272`, the journey foreign key); 0062 and 0083 (the two indexes); 0073 (the XP core `:555`, `gamification_close_month` `:824`); 0081 (`:87`); 0084 (the XP core); 0088 (`:50`) and 0089 (`journey_record_completion`); 0096 (`journey_auto_enroll`); 0103 (`consume_grant`, `record_revision_visit`, the two scoring views; 0103 also refuses to run without the 0062 index, `0103:226-231`); 0105 (the course enrolment view). Earlier definers of the same objects are covered too: 0031 (the enrolment view `:89`, the summary `:146`), 0082 (`:28`), 0063 (`journey_auto_enroll` `:72`; a re-paste would make audience enrolments read `admin`), and every file that defines the template check: 0014, 0015, 0016, 0025, 0059 and 0065 itself (`0065:33-53`, the latest definer; a re-paste would drop `journey_completion` from the check, or fail once such a template is stored). The same list is the header of the file.
   - A DO block that requires the objects of 0100, 0103, 0104, 0105 and 0106 and refuses otherwise. For every object 0107 replaces it accepts either state, so the file's second paste passes.
   - **Backfills run once.** Every backfill of this file (steps 5, 7 and 8) runs only inside the DO block that adds its column: the block tests whether the column exists; if not, it adds the column and runs the update; if it does, it does nothing. A second paste therefore never touches a value the new code wrote. Without this guard a re-paste after phase 3 would mark rows whose writer call failed as handled, and would mark held SCORM rows as never sending. The after-checks that count backfilled rows apply to the first paste only (step 19).
   - Before-checks. Q1 rows by tag shape and `is_preview`. **Q2 refuses the paste** if any of these is above 0: rows carrying both a path and a journey tag that are not previews; journey-tagged rows with a null `journey_day`; rows with `journey_day` set and no `journey_enrollment_id` (orphans of deleted programs). Q2 also counts, without refusing, preview rows that carry a tag (step 6) and abandoned rows that are completed or passed (expected 0, ADR 0010 step 7). Q3 open rows per (learner, version). Q4 the report matview numbers. Q5 the two foreign keys' delete rule. Q6 `xp_events` counts by rule. Q7 `journey_day_progress` rows by whether their attempt is a journey row. Added on 2026-11-13, counts only unless marked. **Q8 refuses the paste** if any is above 0, because a day identity would be invented: version days with no `journey_days` row for (program, day number); journey attempts or progress rows whose (program, day number) has no `journey_days` row. Q8 refuses on the first paste only, tested as the backfills test it (`journey_day_progress.day_id` does not exist yet): once the new editor has removed or moved a day, older versions' numbers and attempts' launch-time numbers rightly match no active `journey_days` row, so on a later paste Q8 is a count and refuses nothing. Q9, drift from earlier publishes to all (the only evidence left is the course on the credit row, `0089:118`): progress rows whose `course_id` differs from the course their enrolment's pinned version holds on that day number; progress rows on day numbers that are not required days of the pinned version; in-progress journey attempts whose day is not the open day of the pinned version or holds another course; active enrolments whose credits are not an unbroken run from the first course day; active enrolments whose pinned version differs, day number by day number, from the program's current version or draft, split by whether the first difference is at or below the learner's furthest credited or started day (this sizes step 94's gate). Q10, rows flagged `is_preview` today (they become `legacy_role`, step 82): how many, how many are completed, how many have `launch_mode = 'Normal'` (flagged by the 0100 backfill, `0100:456-467`), how many carry a result (completed, passed, failed or scored), split by launch mode (added by the check of the round-5 fold: a result in Browse mode is the class the owner is told about under A33, step 82), how many are the target of a used grant (`attempt_requests.used_attempt_id`), and how many `xp_events`, `journey_day_progress` and `lrs_forward_outbox` rows point at one; also how many are completed rows of a person who holds no other official result on that course (after 0107 that person's result is hidden and their next Start is a first official attempt, step 82) and how many are still open (they are never resumed by Start, step 82). On production every count of Q8 to Q10 is expected to read 0; a Q10 count above 0 there goes to the owner before the paste (step 74).

5. **`course_attempts`: the context.**
   - `context_kind text`, added nullable, backfilled, then `not null`, with `check (context_kind in ('individual','path','journey','preview'))`. Backfill order: `is_preview` → `preview`; else `journey_enrollment_id is not null` → `journey`; else `learning_path_id is not null` → `path`; else `individual`.
   - `journey_day_id uuid` (new, nullable, `references journey_days(id) on delete no action`): the identity of the journey day the attempt belongs to (section U). Backfill (once): for journey rows, the `journey_days` id of (the enrolment's program, `journey_day`); exact, because until the new editor ships a day row's id is tied to its number (step 90). `journey_day` stays as the day's number when the attempt was launched, for display and the tenant LRS; it is no longer part of the key.
   - `journey_version_id uuid` (new, nullable, `references journey_versions(id) on delete no action`; added by the independent review of round 4 for reading R14): the journey version the enrolment was pinned to when the attempt was launched. Written at insert by the launch and by `consume_grant` from phase 2 on; never backfilled, so older rows and rows from the paste-window launch code read null and are judged by the pinned version alone. It is not part of the key; the shape CHECK only keeps it null outside the journey kind and does not require it inside it; it is in the immutability trigger's list (step 7). It lets the credit rule say that a publish never makes a started day harder (step 13).
   - `preview_kind text` and `preview_run_id uuid` (new, nullable; step 79).
   - `context_key text generated always as (case context_kind when 'path' then 'path:' || learning_path_id::text when 'journey' then 'journey:' || journey_enrollment_id::text || ':' || journey_day_id::text when 'preview' then 'preview' || coalesce(':' || preview_run_id::text, '') || coalesce(':' || journey_day_id::text, '') else context_kind end) stored`. Changed on 2026-11-13: the journey key names the day id (A27), and a test run's previews have their own key (R7, A21). A `legacy_role` preview keeps the plain key `preview` (the CHECK below keeps its `journey_day_id` null).
   - `course_attempts_context_shape_chk`: `individual` → every tag and `journey_day_id` null and `not is_preview`; `path` → `learning_path_id` set, the journey columns null, `not is_preview`; `journey` → `journey_enrollment_id`, `journey_day` and `journey_day_id` set, `learning_path_id` null, `not is_preview`; `preview` → `is_preview` and `preview_kind` set; for the kinds `course`, `path_run` and `journey_run` the three official tags (`learning_path_id`, `journey_enrollment_id`, `journey_day`) are null, `preview_run_id` is set exactly for the two run kinds, and `journey_day_id` is set exactly for `journey_run`; for `legacy_role`, `preview_run_id` and `journey_day_id` are null (the `journey_day_id` backfill skips preview rows) and the old tags are left as they are (step 6). Outside `preview`, `preview_kind` and `preview_run_id` are null. Outside `journey`, `journey_version_id` is null (a journey test run's version is on its `preview_runs` row); inside `journey` it may be null (older rows). `is_preview` is `boolean not null default false` (`0100:420`), so no `coalesce` is needed.
   - The column comment on `is_preview` (`0100:441-442`, "a launch by super_owner, admin or data_analyst") is restated: "true = a preview launch; never a learner's result, and excluded from every official rule (ADR 0024). Which launches are previews is decided by the launch code; `preview_kind` says which kind." The owner answered R6 on 2026-11-13, so from phase 2 a row is a preview only when it was started with Preview or from a test run; rows flagged by role before that keep the flag and read `legacy_role`.

6. **Preview rows and product references (rewritten on 2026-11-13 for R7 and A21).** Today a staff launch with `?lp=` or from a journey writes the tag and `is_preview` on one row (`launch:1017` with `:1029`). Nulling those tags would be an update of existing attempt columns, so it is not done: such rows are `legacy_role`, the CHECK admits their tags, and their key is `preview` whatever the tags hold. A new preview never carries an official tag: no `learning_path_id`, no `journey_enrollment_id`, no `journey_day`; `attempt-insert-sites.test.ts` asserts it. That keeps every reader that filters attempts by a path or enrolment tag from ever meeting a preview, even one that forgot the official rule. The owner's R7 asks for path and journey test progress, so a test run's preview does carry a product reference, but in preview-only columns: `preview_run_id` names the `preview_runs` row, which names the path, or the journey program and version (step 79), and a journey run's row names its day by `journey_day_id`. A test run has no enrolment, so it cannot and does not point at one. Consequence, stated: a path or journey enrolment referenced by a `legacy_role` row's old tag cannot be deleted (it is archived instead), which matches the rule for learner rows; a path or program referenced only by test runs can be deleted once the route has purged those runs (step 85).

7. **`course_attempts`: triggers, keys, markers, indexes.**
   - Trigger `course_attempts_context_immutable`, `before update`, for every role including the service role (D6): raises when `(context_kind, learning_path_id, journey_enrollment_id, journey_day, journey_day_id, journey_version_id, is_preview, preview_kind, preview_run_id)` of the new row is distinct from the old (the last three, `journey_day_id` and `journey_version_id` added on 2026-11-13). It is `before update` only: it does not stop a delete, and the only code that deletes an attempt row directly is the purge function of step 81, which can select preview rows only. Attempt rows also leave by cascade, as today: with their organisation, with their user, and with a course or version whose only attempts are previews (`0100:546-575` refuses the delete while any other attempt exists; step 85 says how the routes meet that cascade). It is the first trigger on this table, so there is no firing-order question. It refuses three writes that exist today, each on purpose: the resume re-tag (`launch:873-881`, fail-soft at `:883`), the untagged-credit re-tag (`0089:110-112`, removed by step 13), and the grant-retake update that writes `is_preview = true` for a `data_analyst` (`launch:946-957`, fail-soft at `:953-956`; the retake stays a real attempt, which is what D7 wants).
   - Trigger `course_attempts_context_derive`, `before insert`, only when `new.context_kind is null`: `preview` if `is_preview`; else `journey` if the journey tag is set, nulling a path tag that arrives with it (a choice made at insert, not a rewrite); else `path`; else `individual`. For a journey row it also fills `journey_day_id` from the enrolment's pinned version (`day_ids` of `journey_day`, step 91) and raises when there is none, because the old launch code names only the number and the shape CHECK needs the id; for a preview row it writes `preview_kind = 'legacy_role'`, because the old launch code flags previews by role. It exists for the paste window only and 0108 drops it. Each firing is recorded in `context_derive_log`, a one-row table (`id boolean primary key default true check (id)`, `last_fired_at timestamptz`, `fired_count bigint not null default 0`; RLS on with an explicit no-access policy; `revoke all ... from anon, authenticated`; `grant select, insert, update ... to service_role`; the trigger function is `security definer`). It holds a time and a count, nothing about a learner. It is the data source of 0108's before-check (step 20), and 0108 drops it.
   - Foreign keys `learning_path_id` and `journey_enrollment_id` are re-created `on delete no action` (today `set null`). `no action` is checked at the end of the statement, so the organisation and user cascades still work and a lone delete of a path or enrolment with attempts fails with 23503.
   - `side_effects_sig text` and `side_effects_at timestamptz`: the settled state the completion writer last handled (step 12). `settle_sig text generated always as (completion_status || '|' || success_status || '|' || coalesce(score::text, '')) stored`. `settle_sig` is added before `side_effects_sig` (the creation order, item 2), because the backfill reads it. Backfill (once, step 4): on every row already completed or passed, `side_effects_sig = settle_sig` and `side_effects_at = coalesce(completed_at, started_at)`, so the sweep never reaches history.
   - `webhook_skip_reason text` with `check (webhook_skip_reason in ('pre_0107','not_official','out_of_scope'))`: a terminal marker for rows that will never send a completion webhook (step 31). Backfill (once, step 4): `pre_0107` on every completed SCORM 1.2 row whose `completion_webhook_at` is null (their message went out inline, unlatched). No send time is invented.
   - Indexes: `course_attempts_one_in_progress_ctx_idx unique (user_id, course_version_id, context_key) where status = 'in_progress'`, created before `course_attempts_one_in_progress_idx` (`0062:52-54`) is dropped; `(user_id, course_version_id, context_key)`; `(organization_id, context_kind, completed_at)`; `(context_key, user_id) where context_kind <> 'individual'`. Added in round 5, because each of these columns is a `no action` foreign key whose parent row can be deleted (a journey program's cascade, a purged test run), and without an index every such delete scans the child table (the house rule of `0100:473-480`, followed for `revision_visits` at `0103:326`): partial indexes on `course_attempts (journey_version_id)`, `(preview_run_id)` and `(journey_day_id)`, each `where` the column is not null; `attempt_requests (journey_day_id)` the same way; and `preview_runs (learning_path_id)`, `(journey_program_id)` and `(journey_version_id)`. **Ten more, and the rule's boundary (check of the round-5 fold, 2026-11-13).** The seven were not all of them: the check found four more columns of the same kind, and a full pass over every foreign key 0107 adds found six more. The rule is now stated so that the count is not reopened: every foreign-key column that 0107 adds has an index that leads with that column, unless a unique key or an existing index already does. That gives seventeen. The ten added here: `attempt_requests (learning_path_id)` and `(journey_enrollment_id)`, each partial `where` the column is not null (the table's three indexes lead with the organisation or the learner, `0083:37-47`, so a path delete or an enrolment's cascade would scan it); `journey_day_progress (day_id)`, a plain index because the column is `not null` (the only key on it, `unique (enrollment_id, day_id)`, leads with the enrolment); `completion_notices (attempt_id)`, partial, because every purge and cascade of an attempt sets it null; and the organisation and user cascades of the new tables, which the staging CI purge runs (the purge `0100:477-479` names as the reason for the house rule): `preview_runs (organization_id)` and `(user_id)`, `learning_path_completions (organization_id)` and `(user_id)`, `completion_notices (organization_id)` and `preview_purge_log (organization_id)`. Already led by a key or an index, so not counted: `learning_path_completions.path_id` and `completion_notices.user_id` (their unique keys), and the two re-created keys `course_attempts.learning_path_id` (`0037:33`) and `journey_enrollment_id` (`0058:193`). Outside the rule because this record declares no foreign key on them: `journey_day_progress.recorded_by`, `preview_purge_log.actor_user_id` and `preview_settings.updated_by`, which hold an id only. Columns that earlier migrations added are not in the count either. The version stays in the open-row key, so language-slot routing keeps working (`launch:475-488`, `:757-790`).

8. **Grants, visits and the other changed tables.**
   - `attempt_requests`: `context_kind text not null default 'individual'` (`individual`, `path`, `journey`), `learning_path_id` and `journey_enrollment_id` (both `on delete no action`), `journey_day`, `journey_day_id` (`references journey_days(id) on delete no action`; set with a journey context, section U), the same shape CHECK without the preview arm, a generated `context_key` with the same journey formula as the attempt's (the day id). `attempt_requests_one_open_ctx_idx unique (user_id, course_id, context_key)` with the 0083 predicate (pending, or approved and unused) is created before `attempt_requests_one_open_idx` (`0083:45-47`) is dropped. Existing rows read `individual`: no writer could set a context. The table's own-rows select policy and grants are restated.
   - `revision_visits`: `context_key text not null default 'individual'`, backfilled from the visit's attempt where it exists (`0103:315`), after `course_attempts.context_key` exists (the creation order, item 2); a visit whose attempt row is gone stays `individual` (disclosed). Deny-all policy and grants unchanged and restated.
   - `xp_events`: nullable `context_kind`, `context_key`; `event_only boolean not null default false`; index `(organization_id, context_key, created_at desc)`. Old rows stay null (the table is append-only, `0053:134-146`). `xp_events_rule_check` (`0084:46-50`) is untouched.
   - `learning_path_courses.pass_required boolean` (null = inherit the course).
   - `journey_days.pass_required boolean` (null = inherit). The freeze into `journey_versions.days` is TypeScript (step 46).
   - `journey_days`, `journey_versions` and `journey_day_progress` gain the day identity of section U (step 91): `journey_days.removed_at` and a nullable `day_number`; `journey_versions.day_ids` and `day_ids_source`; `journey_day_progress.day_id` with `unique (enrollment_id, day_id)` in place of `unique (enrollment_id, day_number)` (`0058:173`).
   - `journey_enrollments.source text not null default 'admin' check (source in ('admin','audience','self'))`. **No `deadline_days` column** (D17). `journey_auto_enroll` is recreated from `0096:158-186` writing `source = 'audience'`.
   - `journey_programs.visibility text not null default 'private' check (visibility in ('private','org_public'))`, the shape courses and paths have had since `0033:24-30`.
   - `course_assignments.source` and `learning_path_assignments.source`: `text not null default 'admin' check (source in ('admin','self'))`.
   - `journey_day_progress`: `credit_kind text not null default 'attempt' check (credit_kind in ('attempt','legacy_cross_context','self','admin'))`, `recorded_by uuid`, `late boolean not null default false`. Backfill: rows whose `attempt_id` is null or points at a row that is not a journey row become `legacy_cross_context`. They are kept and still count as credited (step 45).
   - `org_api_keys`: `contract_version smallint not null default 1 check (contract_version in (1,2))`, `allow_contract_override boolean not null default false`. **Existing keys read 1 and new keys read 2 (decision A28):** inside the run-once DO block that adds the column (step 4), the column is added with `default 1`, which labels every existing key 1, and the next statement is `alter table public.org_api_keys alter column contract_version set default 2`. The only route that creates a key writes with the user's session and does not name the column (`app/api/org/api-keys/route.ts:68-76`), and the insert grant below leaves the column out, so every key created after the paste takes the default, 2. No route change and no SQL by the owner is needed to give a consumer a version 2 key: a new key is issued. A second paste does not reach the block, so it changes no key. Until phase 9a deploys, no code reads the column, so a key created between the 0107 paste and the 9a deploy answers in today's shape and changes to version 2 when 9a ships. On production both happen in one sitting (step 74). On staging the window is weeks: a test consumer that must stay on version 1 keeps its existing key, or its new key is set to 1 by SQL there (test data); disclosed. After-checks: on the first paste every existing key reads 1; the column default reads 2; in the rehearsal, a key inserted as `authenticated` through the granted columns reads 2. Apart from that default, the two columns are written by the service role only. The table's policy is "for all" to super owners with no literal grants (`0071:15-36`), so `authenticated` holds table-level privileges from the project defaults, and the settings route writes the table with the user's session (`app/api/org/api-keys/route.ts:68-76`, `:99-104`). A column-level revoke does nothing while the role holds the privilege on the whole table, so 0107 runs `revoke insert, update on public.org_api_keys from authenticated` and grants back only what the route writes: `grant insert (organization_id, name, key_prefix, key_hash, is_active, created_by) on public.org_api_keys to authenticated` and `grant update (is_active) on public.org_api_keys to authenticated`. `last_used_at` is written by the service client (`lib/integrations/auth.ts`). Select and delete are untouched. After-check: as `authenticated`, an update or insert naming `contract_version` raises 42501, and the route's insert and its deactivate still succeed.
   - `user_badges.period`: its column comment (`0053:227`, "'YYYY-MM' for monthly badges; NULL = lifetime") is restated to add "'enr:<enrolment id>' for a journey badge (ADR 0024)" (step 13).
   - `org_integration_settings.webhook_contexts text not null default 'individual' check (webhook_contexts in ('individual','all'))`.
   - `notification_templates_event_type_check` is re-asserted from its latest form (`0065:33-52`) with `journey_completion` added.

9. **The official rule in SQL.**
   - New views `v_context_attempt_scoring` and `v_context_attempt_summary`, built from the LIVE bodies (`0103:430-536`, `0103:547-610`) with these changes. (a) Every partition, `distinct on` and group key gains `context_key`, and the scoring view's final join to its `official` CTE and the summary's left join to the scoring view are on `(course_id, user_id, context_key)` (today `0103:529` and `:603-604` join on course and learner only; left as they are, base rows would fan out across contexts). (b) The official rule (`not is_revision and context_kind <> 'preview' and status <> 'abandoned'`) is the filter of the scoring view's `completed` CTE (`0103:447-450`), the place where the official attempt is chosen. (c) The summary's `base` CTE filters only `not is_revision and context_kind <> 'preview'`. It keeps abandoned rows, as today, so the 0099 columns keep their meaning: `attempt_count`, `latest_completion` and `total_time_seconds` over every row, and `live_attempt_count`, `latest_live_completion` and `latest_live_success` over rows that are not abandoned (`0103:564-570`; 0 or null means only reset attempts). (d) The summary appends `context_kind`, `context_key`, `learning_path_id`, `journey_enrollment_id`, `journey_day` and `journey_day_id`. `journey_day` is the number at launch, for display only; every per-day reader (the CRM `days[]` score, journey admin, `journey_score`) joins on `context_key` or `journey_day_id`, never on the number. `official_attempt_id` is already its column 19 (`0103:597`) and is not appended again. `security_invoker = on` in the definition and restated; `grant select ... to authenticated, service_role`.
   - `v_course_attempt_scoring` and `v_course_attempt_summary` **keep their names, columns, column order and (course, learner) grain** and become the Individual context: the same 0103 bodies with `context_kind = 'individual' and status <> 'abandoned'` added to the scoring view's `completed` CTE, and `context_kind = 'individual'` alone added to the summary's `base` CTE (abandoned rows stay there, for the reason in (c)). Option and grants restated (`0103:532`, `:536`, `:606`, `:610`). This is why no page shows mixed numbers between the 0107 and 0109 pastes: the six LIVE matviews that join the summary on (course, learner) keep one row per learner. Their meaning in that window is stated in section K.
   - `official_attempt_in_context(p_user uuid, p_course uuid, p_context_key text) returns uuid`, `stable`, `language sql`, created after the scoring view, reading `v_context_attempt_scoring`. It replaces the ad-hoc chains in `consume_grant`, `gamification_close_month` and the XP ordinal. Service role only; views join `official_attempt_id` set-based and never call it per row.
   - `instance_pass_required(p_course uuid, p_context_key text) returns boolean`, `stable`, `language sql` (it reads tables only): the path step's `pass_required`; else the day's value in the enrolment's pinned `journey_versions.days`; else `courses.pass_required`. Service role only, by the rule of this section's header: it is called by `consume_grant`, the writer and `journey_record_completion`, never by a view. Views that `authenticated` can read resolve the rule by join (`coalesce(learning_path_courses.pass_required, courses.pass_required)`, and the day's value for journeys), because a learner's read of a view that called the function would fail with 42501.
   - **The path rule, in 0107** (it was planned for 0109; the writer of step 14 needs it from the first paste, and one rule must not exist twice). `v_path_step_status` (path, learner, course): `v_path_enrollments_expanded` × `learning_path_courses`, left-joined to `v_context_attempt_summary` on `context_key = 'path:' || path_id`, with `organization_id`, `passed_ever`, `latest_result`, `latest_score`, `done` (step 44) and the resolved `pass_required` by join. `passed_ever` is computed set-based from the path's own attempt rows under the official rule (some official attempt or consumed grant retake satisfied the resolved rule). `v_path_progress` (path, learner): `organization_id`, `steps_total`, `steps_done`, `next_step`, `path_score` (the mean of the latest official scores of scored steps), `completed` (a `learning_path_completions` row exists), `status`, `due_at`, `enrolled_at`, `started_at`, `completed_at`, `overdue`. Both are `with (security_invoker = on)`, restated by `alter view`, with `revoke all ... from anon` and `grant select ... to authenticated, service_role` (the form of `0105:691-697`). They read only objects of 0107 or earlier: the summary, the path enrolment view, the step list, the terms view of step 18 and `learning_path_completions` of step 14. The last two have higher step numbers but are created earlier in the file: the creation order at the head of this section puts the table, then the terms views, then these two views, then `launch_context`.
   - The policy function `effective_attempt_policy` (`0103:359-416`) is unchanged: it answers constants for every course, so every context has the same policy.

10. **`consume_grant`, recreated from `0103:721-905` (grants `:907-908`), same signature.** Carried over unchanged: the 22023 consistency check (`:760-768`), the lock order with the grant first (`:747-754`), `stale` and `stands` with their note suffixes (`:844-873`), `override_confirmed` (`:863`). Changed: the grant row must match the context computed from the three tags passed in (today the lock matches only id, learner, course and status), else `unavailable` with nothing written; `retake_live`, the chain, `stands` and the abandon are filtered by `context_key`; the `stands` test reads `instance_pass_required` in place of `courses.pass_required` (`:803-806`); the insert names `context_kind` (`:889-894`), because 0108 removes the derive trigger. For a journey launch the new code calls a second form that adds `p_journey_day_id uuid` **with no default** (a default would make the old call ambiguous for PostgREST, as in step 11): the launch passes the day id it resolved, the function locks the enrolment row after the grant, refuses with `unavailable` and nothing written when that id differs from the grant's `journey_day_id`, and writes `journey_day_id` and `journey_version_id` (the pinned version read from the locked row) on the retake (A27). A day number resolved inside the function, outside the enrolment lock, could name another day after a re-pin that runs between the launch's check and this call. The old form keeps its signature for the paste-window launch code as a wrapper: it resolves the number through the pinned version under the same lock and calls the new form; 0108's preconditions end its use, and it stays as a wrapper. **The two signatures, written out (round 5).** Old, unchanged (`0103:721-731`): `consume_grant(p_grant_id uuid, p_user_id uuid, p_course_id uuid, p_version_id uuid, p_org_id uuid, p_learning_path_id uuid, p_journey_enrollment_id uuid, p_journey_day integer) returns table (outcome text, attempt_id uuid, abandoned_ids uuid[])`. New: the same eight arguments followed by `p_journey_day_id uuid`, nine in all, no default on any, the same return type. **Lock order in both: the grant, then the enrolment.** The wrapper's first statement is the grant lock itself (the `for update` select of `0103:748-754`); only then does it lock the enrolment row and resolve the number; the nine-argument form it calls takes the same two locks in the same order, which the transaction already holds. As first written the wrapper took the enrolment lock first, the reverse of the new form, and two launches of one learner could have deadlocked (found by the review of round 5). `journey_record_completion` and the re-pin lock the enrolment and no grant, so no cycle exists. The `stands` test for a journey row uses the lenient rule of step 13 (the rule of the version the attempt was launched under, or the pinned version's). Grants restated on both forms. A preview never reaches either (step 37).

11. **`record_revision_visit`.** A seven-argument form adds `p_context_key text` **with no default** (a default would make the six-name call ambiguous for PostgREST). Its advisory lock and 10-second dedupe are keyed (learner, course, context) (`0103:670-676` today per learner and course). The six-argument form (`0103:656-683`) stays as a wrapper passing `'individual'`. Grants restated on both.

12. **`record_learning_activity(p_attempt_id uuid) returns jsonb`: the one side-effect writer.** `security definer`, service role only.
    1. Load the row. Answer `{skipped: reason}` for a preview, a revision row or an abandoned row.
    2. **Activity branch**, on every call for a row that is not a journey row: the daily points and streak, exactly as the core does today (`0084:121-133`, `:189-200`). Today the routes call the functions only when the run reads official (`commit:364-374`, `statements:322-335`); the writer keeps that meaning by running the branch only when the row is the context's official candidate (the context has no official attempt, or this row is it or a live granted retake).
    3. **Completion branch**, when the row is complete or passed and `side_effects_sig is distinct from settle_sig`: `official = (id = official_attempt_in_context(...))`. If official, in this order: the reward candidates (section F); for a journey row, `journey_record_completion`; for a path row, the path completion check (step 14); the completion-notice claim (step 15). Then set `side_effects_sig = settle_sig`, `side_effects_at = now()` and, when the row is not official, `webhook_skip_reason = 'not_official'`. Every inner write is idempotent, so the branch can run again when the stored result changes. That is required: cmi5 sends `completed` and then `passed` in separate requests (`lib/xapi/process-statement.ts:137-160`), and a SCORM row under a non-final verdict can go from failed to passed (`commit:186-196`, `:280-303`). A one-time stamp would lose the pass marker, the score bonuses, a `pass_required` day credit, a `pass_required` path step and the "passed where required" email.
    4. Answer `{activity, official, attempt_number, context: {kind, learning_path_id, journey_enrollment_id, journey_day}, done, passed_ever, xp, journey_credited, journey_completed, path_completed, notices: [...]}`. `attempt_number` counts official attempts within the context. `passed_ever` is set for a path row only (the step's value from `v_path_step_status`) and is null otherwise. `path_completed`, `journey_completed` and each entry of `notices` are true or present only when this call made the insert or the transition. Each entry of `notices` is `{id, product_kind, instance_id, event_type}`: `id` is the `completion_notices` row the call inserted, and the route stamps `sent_at` on that id after the send (step 32).
    5. Any error raises and nothing is written. The route logs the attempt id, keeps the learner's save, and fires no webhook and sends no email.

13. **`journey_record_completion`, recreated from `0089:17-141`; its grants, last stated at `0058:381-382`, are restated.** New: it requires `context_kind = 'journey'` and `not is_revision` (the body reads neither today; the routes gated it); it reads the resolved `pass_required` (today the course's, `0089:46-51`); it credits (enrolment, day id) when the attempt's `journey_day_id` is a day of the enrolment's pinned version, whatever course that day holds now, because the launch already proved the day held the attempt's course when the learner started it (the argument of A30) and the owner's A27 answer forbids losing started work to a later course swap; the round-3 text's course check and its `day_course_mismatch` answer are withdrawn. When the day is no longer in the pinned version (removed, or cleared to a rest day), the credit row is still written, as history, and counts towards nothing (reading R13); the answer says `day_removed: true`. The insert sets `day_id`, `day_number` (the day's number in the pinned version at credit time, or the attempt's `journey_day` for a removed day), `credit_kind = 'attempt'` and `late` (credited after that day's counted date); its conflict target is `(enrollment_id, day_id)`. The enrolment must be owned by the attempt's learner and be active (`0089:53-63`), with one addition by the independent review of round 4: for a `completed` enrolment the function still writes the credit row, as history, and changes nothing else (no second completion, badge or claim). That is the case of a publish that completes an enrolment while a learner holds an open attempt on a day the new version no longer requires: the learner may finish it (step 22) and the result is kept, as the owner's second A27 sentence asks (reading R13). A `reset` enrolment is refused as today. **Which pass rule judges a started attempt (reading R14, default changed by the independent review).** The attempt is credited when its result satisfies the day's rule in the enrolment's pinned version, or the day's rule in the version the attempt was launched under (`journey_version_id`, step 5; null means the pinned version only). So a publish that makes a day's rule stricter never fails work started before it, and one that relaxes it helps at once. `instance_pass_required` is unchanged and keeps answering the pinned version's rule; the lenient test is one more `language sql` helper of 0107, `journey_attempt_satisfies(p_attempt_id uuid, p_target_rule boolean) returns boolean` (stable, service role only, in the creation order's item 5, the privilege matrix and the reverse script). Changed in round 5: the helper takes the target's rule as an argument, the resolved `pass_required` of the attempt's day in the version the caller judges against, and answers true when the attempt's result satisfies that rule or the rule of the day in the version the attempt was launched under. It reads the launched version itself and never the target, so it can judge a draft that has no version row. It has three callers, not two: this function and `consume_grant`'s `stands` test, which pass the pinned version's rule, and `journey_repin_enrollments`, which passes the rule of the target it was given, a version row in a real run or `p_draft` in a dry run, when it writes the credits of a relaxed rule (steps 94, 95). The dry run and the real run therefore judge by the same code and the same input, which is what step 63's equality of their counts needs (the first text said "by nothing else" and gave the helper no way to read a draft; found by the review of round 5). Every other reader reads the credit row. `attempt-insert-sites.test.ts` asserts that a journey insert in `app/` and `lib/` names `journey_version_id` (a fixture under `tests/` or `scripts/` may leave it null, which is the legal "pinned version only" value; step 23), so the rule cannot silently fall back to the pinned version for new rows. Removed: the untagged branch (`0089:64-77`) and the re-tag (`:110-112`). The function's first statement locks the enrolment row (`select ... from journey_enrollments where id = ... for update`) and reads the status, the owner and the pinned version from that locked row, so a re-pin running at the same time cannot change the version between the checks and the write, and two last-day credits of one enrolment run one after the other and the second one's check sees the first one's row. Not added, on purpose (decision A30): a start-date floor and a calendar ceiling on the tagged branch, which has neither today (`0089:53-63`; only the removed untagged branch had them, `:90-108`). The attempt's context is checked against the open day before the attempt row exists (step 22) and can never change (step 7), so a journey row proves its day was open when the learner started it; a check repeated at completion would refuse a credit the learner earned whenever the save, a late `passed` or the retry sweep lands after an admin moved the start date. The sweep re-runs this function, so it applies the same checks as the first call: active enrolment, the learner's own, the day's identity, the resolved pass rule.

    **One journey completion rule (decision A27 as the owner answered it on 2026-11-13; the design is section U).** A day is identified by its day id, never by its number. A journey enrolment is complete when every required day of its pinned version holds a `journey_day_progress` row for that day id, and the version has at least one required day. A required day is a day of the pinned version at or below `days_total` that carries a course (from phase G, also a required activity day). The open day is the required day with the lowest number in the pinned version and no credit row. This is a check per day identity, and it replaces the count of progress rows used today in three places (`0089:121-128`, `count >= required`; the publish route `:153-157`; `computeJourneyState`, `lib/journey/journey.ts:247` and `:251`, which opens `courseDays[completedCount]`). The rule is one SQL statement, `journey_credit_state(p_ids uuid[]) returns table (enrollment_id uuid, required_days int, credited_days int, credited int[], credited_day_ids uuid[], next_day int, next_day_id uuid, resumable_day_ids uuid[])` (`stable`, `language sql`, service role only): `credited_day_ids` lists the required days of the pinned version that hold a credit row and `credited` their numbers in that version, `credited_days` is their number, `next_day` and `next_day_id` name the lowest-numbered required day without one (null when none is left), and `resumable_day_ids` lists the days whose context holds an open official attempt of this enrolment, whether or not the day is still in the pinned version (a started day stays resumable, step 93). Every SQL reader uses it: the completion functions below, `launch_context` (step 18), `journey_reminder_counts`, and 0109's `journey_enrollment_state` (step 21). `journey_reminder_counts` (`0089:147-169`, grants `:171-172`) keeps `enrollment_id`, `done` and `done_today`, with `done` now being `credited_days`, and gains a fourth column, `credited int[]`, which the nudges cron passes to the TypeScript twin (`app/api/cron/journey-nudges/route.ts:178-203` passes `done` as the count today). A new output column changes the function's return type, so 0107 drops and creates it, with the grants restated; the old cron code reads the three columns it knows from the same answer. The TypeScript twin is `computeJourneyState` (step 45).

    Why by day identity, and not by the count or by the day number. The count is right only while credits form an unbroken run from day 1, which holds until a publish with `apply_to=all` re-pins an enrolment to a version whose course days moved (kept under A11). After such a re-pin today, the count can open a day whose module the learner already completed under another number. Under the one-attempt rule that launch is a revision visit, and neither write route credits a revision visit (`commit:364-374`), so the learner is stuck until an admin's grant; the count can also skip a day the learner never took. The round-3 text of this step replaced the count with a credit per day number. The owner rejected that on 2026-11-13, and rightly: when a publish inserts or moves a day, a credit stays on its number while the module moves, so a completed module is asked for again and a new module is skipped, and an open attempt on a moved day is orphaned, because rows cannot be re-tagged (D6, principle 5). A credit per day identity cannot do either: the credit and the open attempt name the day itself, and the day keeps its id when the admin moves it (section U). It is still D1's grain, a context per enrolment and day, and a course on two days still gives two results. With no re-pin all three rules give the same numbers. Every edge the owner's four sentences cover is settled in step 95. `legacy_cross_context` credits count by their day id like any other.

    Completion moves into `journey_complete_enrollments_core(p_ids uuid[], p_origin text) returns setof uuid` (plpgsql, `security definer`; `revoke execute ... from public, anon, authenticated`, `grant execute ... to service_role`): it locks the named enrolments that are active, in id order (`for update`), reads `journey_credit_state` for them in a statement that starts after the locks are held, and for each one with `required_days > 0` and `next_day is null` runs `update journey_enrollments set status = 'completed', completed_at = now() where id = ... and status = 'active'`. `completed_at` is named because every surface that prints a journey completion date reads it (the journey page and its selection order, the certificate, the CRM `completed_at`), and both writers set it today (`0089:129`; the publish route). For each enrolment that update changed, and for no other, the core itself then inserts the `yoddha` badge with `period = 'enr:' || enrollment_id` and the program's name in `metadata` (`user_badges` already keys on `coalesce(period,'')`, `0053:233-234`; old lifetime rows are untouched) and takes the journey claim in `completion_notices` (step 15), writing `p_origin` into the claim's `origin`. The badge insert and the claim are statements of the core, in the core's transaction, and of nothing else, so every caller shares one latch. Two thin wrappers inherit all of it, so the rule exists once. `journey_complete_enrollment(p_enrollment_id uuid) returns boolean` passes one id with the origin `learner`; it answers true only when the core's update changed that row (today every concurrent caller that reaches `0089:128` reads `yoddha_unlocked: true`), and the writer reads the claim the core made from the same transaction. `journey_complete_enrollments(p_program_id uuid) returns setof uuid` passes every active enrolment of one program with the origin `publish` and returns the enrolment ids the core completed. The origin is recorded so that a claim made by an admin's publish can be told from a learner's claim whose send failed (decision A20's refusal branch); a null `attempt_id` could not serve as that mark, because the column is `on delete set null` and phase G's activity credits complete an enrolment with no attempt. All three functions are in the privilege matrix of step 19 and in the reverse script's drop list (step 61). The publish route reaches the set-based form through `journey_repin_enrollments` (step 94), which re-pins and completes in one transaction. These are the only writers of a journey completion.

14. **`learning_path_completions`** (new table): `id uuid primary key`, `organization_id` (cascade), `path_id` (`on delete no action`), `user_id` (cascade), `completed_at`, `path_score numeric`, `steps jsonb` (step, course, attempt id, score; ids and scores only), `unique (path_id, user_id)`, and an append-only trigger in the `xp_events` form (`0053:134-146`): the plpgsql trigger function `learning_path_completions_append_only()` and the trigger `learning_path_completions_no_mutation`, `before update or delete`, which lets the organisation and user cascades through as the 0053 function does. The trigger function has its execute revoked from `public, anon, authenticated` like every function of this file. RLS on; a select policy for the learner's own rows and `is_org_admin`; no insert, update or delete policy. The grants are written out in the file: `revoke all on public.learning_path_completions from public, anon, authenticated;` and `grant select on public.learning_path_completions to authenticated, service_role;`. `authenticated` needs the select: `v_path_progress` is `security_invoker` and reads this table, so without it a learner's read of the path view would raise 42501, and the rehearsal's grants seed runs before 0107 and does not cover a table 0107 creates. No API role is granted insert: the row is inserted only by the `security definer` writer of step 12, as the table's owner. The writer inserts it `on conflict do nothing` when every step of the path is done for this learner in `path:P`, read from `v_path_step_status` of step 9 (the one SQL definition of step 44's rule; the writer holds no second copy), and reports `path_completed` only when the insert happened.

15. **`completion_notices`** (new table; the D16 latch). `notification_log` cannot serve: it has no unique key and no course or attempt column, and it is written after the send (`0014:48-65`; `lib/notifications/send.ts:291-330`). `xp_events` cannot serve either: an organisation with gamification off writes none (`0084:107-109`). Columns: `id uuid primary key`, `organization_id` (cascade), `user_id` (cascade), `product_kind text check (product_kind in ('individual','path','journey'))`, `instance_id uuid` (the course, the path, the journey enrolment), `attempt_id uuid` (`on delete set null`), `event_type text`, `origin text not null default 'learner' check (origin in ('learner','publish'))` (who made the claim: the completion writer on a learner's own completion, or an admin's publish to all enrolled learners, step 13), `claimed_at timestamptz not null default now()`, `sent_at timestamptz`, `unique (user_id, product_kind, instance_id)`. `organization_id`, `user_id`, `product_kind`, `instance_id` and `event_type` are `not null`: a null in a key column would never conflict, and the latch would not hold. RLS on with an explicit no-access policy (the form of `0103:336` and `0094:17`); service role only. The grants are written out in the file, as the CI check requires: `revoke all on public.completion_notices from public, anon, authenticated;` and `grant select, update on public.completion_notices to service_role;`. Select and update are what the routes need to stamp `sent_at` on the id the writer returned. Insert is not granted to any API role: only the `security definer` functions of steps 12 and 13 take a claim, as the table's owner. The writer claims by `insert ... on conflict do nothing`, inside its own transaction, and returns a notice only when the insert happened:
    - Individual: when the Individual official attempt is done under the resolved rule (completed, or passed where a pass is required). Once per learner and course: a granted retake that completes again sends nothing.
    - Path: in the same call that inserts `learning_path_completions`.
    - Journey: inside `journey_complete_enrollments_core` (step 13), for each enrolment its update completed, in the same transaction, so the writer and the publish route, which reach the core through its two wrappers, cannot claim twice. Once per enrolment, so a re-enrolment can earn its own. The claim's `origin` is `learner` from the single wrapper and `publish` from the set-based one; Individual and path claims are always `learner`.

16. **The retry sweep, scheduled from 0107.** `sweep_completion_side_effects(p_limit int) returns jsonb` re-runs the completion branch for rows that are complete, not revision, not preview, not abandoned, with `side_effects_sig is distinct from settle_sig`, older than two minutes. Each row runs in its own exception block, so one failing row cannot stop the batch; failures are counted in the answer. `refresh_gamification_views()` (from `0056:179-204`, grants `:205-206`) and `refresh_report_views()` (from `0035:81-124`, privileges `0049:54, 59`) are recreated in 0107 to call it inside their own exception block and to add a `completion_sweep` entry to their answer. `refresh_report_views()`, the nightly one on both environments, also calls the retention purge of step 81 in its own exception block and adds a `preview_purge` entry (counts only). The technical detail scheduled the sweep only from 0109, which would have left a fail-closed writer with no retry for weeks. Production runs the first function every 15 minutes (`.github/workflows/cron.yml:28`); staging runs only the second, nightly at 03:30 UTC by pg_cron, and specs call the sweep directly. After-checks that count the refresh answers' entries (the 0103 and 0105 forms expect six and two) get new expectations.

17. **The XP core and wrapper** (`gamification_record_activity` from `0058:216-246`, grants `:247-250`; `gamification_record_activity_core` from `0084:55-349`, grants `:350-351`) and `gamification_close_month` (from the whole of `0073:824-1022`, grants `:1023-1024`, never from 0053) are recreated as section F says.

18. **Enrolment views.**
    - `v_course_enrollments_expanded` (from `0105:613-697`): the four direct arms with their release gates stay; the path arm (`:657-670`) and the hold-back (`:672-689`) go (D2). A self-enrolment is a `user` row, so the view needs no new arm. Two columns, `security_invoker`, grants as 0105.
    - `v_path_enrollments_expanded` (`0105:545-587`) is unchanged: a self-enrolment is a `user` row there too.
    - `v_course_enrollment_terms` (course, learner) and `v_path_enrollment_terms` (path, learner): `due_at` and `enrolled_at`. One due-date rule for the whole LMS: the learner's own (`user`) row's `due_at` when it has one, else the earliest `due_at` among the other assignments that reach the learner. `enrolled_at` is `min(assigned_at)` (`0005:40`, `0008:41`; `learning_path_assignments` has no `created_at`). A self-enrolled row has no due date, so it is never overdue. Both views carry `organization_id`, are `with (security_invoker = on)` with the option restated by `alter view`, and carry `revoke all ... from anon` and `grant select ... to authenticated, service_role`, as `0105:691-697` does. Without the option a view runs with its owner's rights, and a learner could read every organisation's assignment dates through the Data API; with a grant to the service role only, the learner-facing views that join them would fail with 42501. They are in the privilege matrix of step 19.
    - `launch_context(p_user uuid, p_org uuid, p_course uuid, p_path uuid, p_enrollment uuid, p_day_id uuid, p_day int) returns jsonb` (the day is named by its id; `p_day` serves an old link that carries only a number and is resolved through the pinned version, step 93): `stable`, plpgsql, `security definer`, service role only; created last among the objects of this file, after the path views. One call returns the facts `resolveLaunchContext` needs (step 22): active membership; the course's visibility and the learner's released direct enrolment; for a path, its organisation, active flag, visibility and `sequence_mode` (`learning_paths.sequence_mode`, `0034:34`), the learner's recorded enrolment, and the step list with `step_number` (`learning_path_courses.step_number`, `0008:23`; there is no column named `sequence`), `release_at` and `done` from `v_path_step_status`; for a journey, the enrolment's owner, status, program and `start_date`, the pinned version's `unlock_mode`, `count_sundays` and `days_total`, the organisation's timezone (`gamification_settings.timezone`, default `Asia/Kolkata`, as `0089:92-95` reads it), the day's id, its number and course in the pinned version (or that it is no longer in it), that day's credit, and `required_days`, `credited_days`, `next_day`, `next_day_id` and `resumable_day_ids` from `journey_credit_state` (step 13), which together are what "this day is open, credited or resumable" needs; and the paths and journey days in which the learner holds the course. It returns facts only and decides nothing: the pure resolver stays the one decision and computes today's date and the calendar ceiling from these facts with the existing helpers (`lib/journey/journey.ts:132-166`). The SQL facts are tested in the rehearsal (step 63), and a unit test feeds the resolver the function's answer shape on fixture rows. It exists for the subrequest budget (step 70). The page passes the session's user id and asserts the organisation, as for every service-role read.

19. **After-checks A1 to A24.** Added on 2026-11-13: A19, every journey attempt has a `journey_day_id`, every `journey_day_progress` row a `day_id`, every `journey_versions` row a `day_ids` entry for each of its days, and, on the first paste only, counts by day id equal the counts by (program, day number) taken in Q8 and Q9 (after the new editor has moved or removed a day the two no longer agree, by design); A20, `unique (enrollment_id, day_id)` present and the old key absent; A21, every `is_preview` row reads `preview_kind = 'legacy_role'` on the first paste, no other row has a `preview_kind`, and the count equals Q10; A22, `purge_preview_data` in dry-run mode over every organisation answers with every new-kind count 0 and `excluded` 0 on the first paste (no row of a new kind exists yet) and writes no log row; under A33 option (b) it also prints the three by-role counts (would be deleted, kept, not yet aged), which need not be 0 and must add up to Q10's total (corrected by the check of the round-5 fold: the text said "every count 0", which staging's aged by-role rows would have failed); A23, `preview_runs`, `preview_settings` and `preview_purge_log` have RLS on with a policy, and as `authenticated` or `anon` a read of each raises 42501, and `preview_settings` holds exactly one row, with `live_days = 30` on the first paste (step 79) and, since round 5, `backup_days` null and `purge_legacy` false, and the append-only trigger of `preview_purge_log` and the seventeen foreign-key indexes of step 7 are present (seven before the check of the fold); A24, `journey_save_days`, `journey_repin_enrollments` and both forms of `consume_grant` in the privilege matrix. The privilege matrix also asserts, for every function these files create or recreate, that `pg_proc.proconfig` holds a `search_path` entry (the header of this section). A17: on the first paste every existing `org_api_keys` row reads `contract_version = 1`, and the column default reads 2 (step 8). A18: `journey_credit_state` answers for every active enrolment, and no active enrolment has `required_days > 0` with `next_day` null (such a row would be a completion the paste missed; expected 0, counted and reported if not). A1 to A16: The new indexes present and the two old ones absent; both foreign keys `no action`; both triggers present; the recreated function bodies name `context_key`; the privilege matrix with a real 42501 as `anon`, covering every new relation and function, the two terms views and the two path views; both refresh functions answer with every `error` null and a `completion_sweep` entry; counts by `context_kind` equal Q1; Q2 unchanged; on the first paste only, zero completed rows with `side_effects_sig` distinct from `settle_sig` (on a later paste such rows are the sweep's own candidates and are only counted); the two old-name views keep their column lists; `v_course_attempt_summary` has one row per (course, learner); `v_context_attempt_summary` has one row per (course, learner, `context_key`); as `authenticated`, a write naming `org_api_keys.contract_version` raises 42501; as `authenticated`, a read of the terms views and the path views for another organisation returns 0 rows; `context_derive_log` exists with RLS on; the RLS audit's rule holds (every new table with `organization_id` has RLS on and at least one policy).

### C. Migrations 0108 and 0109

20. **`0108_drop_context_derive.sql`.** Drops `course_attempts_context_derive`, its function and `context_derive_log`. Before-check, from data: the file refuses to run when `context_derive_log.last_fired_at` is within the last 24 hours, and prints `fired_count`. A firing means some insert still names no `context_kind`: old launch code on one of the two staging deployments, or a test fixture. After-check: the trigger and the table are gone and a tagless insert fails `not null`. Preconditions of the paste, recorded with it: phase 2 runs on both staging deployments (the Workers staging and Cloud Run, whose deploy is the owner's); `attempt-insert-sites.test.ts` is green on `main` over `app/`, `lib/`, `tests/` and `scripts/` (step 23); the nightly list and the pull-request e2e job have each passed once on a build whose fixtures name `context_kind` (step 64). Added on 2026-11-13: (a) every open pull-request branch contains phase 2 and the fixtures commit, because each branch's e2e job runs its own code against the staging database (`.github/workflows/pr-checks.yml:78-91`), so a stale branch fires the derive trigger and restarts the 24 hours, and goes red after the paste; (b) 0108 also drops the paste-window trigger that fills `journey_versions.day_ids` and sets that column `not null` (step 91), with a before-check that no version lacks it. Without the nightly and e2e preconditions, the paste would turn the nightly and every pull request's e2e job red: several fixtures insert attempts directly with the service role, and the e2e job runs against the staging database.

21. **`0109_product_reporting.sql`.** Header: after 0109 never paste 0031, 0035, 0049, 0053 (`refresh_gamification_views` `:730`, `mv_leaderboard` `:629`), 0056, 0059, 0073, 0087, 0090, 0099 or 0106 again, **and never 0107**: both files recreate the two refresh functions, so a re-paste of 0107 after 0109 would strip the new matviews and the snapshot from them. Contents:
