    - **Progress views.** `v_individual_enrollment_status` (course, learner): the direct enrolment view joined to the Individual summary and the terms view; `organization_id`, `status`, `passed`, `official_score`, `started_at`, `completed_at`, `due_at`, `overdue`; `security_invoker`, restated, `revoke all ... from anon`, `grant select ... to authenticated, service_role`; `pass_required` read by join, never through `instance_pass_required`. `v_path_step_status` and `v_path_progress` are not created here: they are in 0107 (step 9), and 0109 leaves them alone.
    - `journey_enrollment_state(p_enrollment_id uuid)` (stable, service role only): the SQL twin of `computeJourneyState` (`lib/journey/journey.ts:212-283`) with `blocked_failed`, `late_days`, `journey_score`. Finished, the credited count and the open day come from `journey_credit_state` of 0107 (step 13), the rule by day identity; this function holds no count of its own. Behind copies the TypeScript rule exactly: `max(0, pendingDays - 1)`, where `pendingDays` is the number of released course days that hold no credit (step 45; today `releasedMissions - completedCount`, the same number while credits run unbroken from day 1), 0 in progress mode, rest days skipped (`journey.ts:257-278`). The deadline is the twin of `dateOfDay(start_date, program.deadline_days, version.count_sundays)` (`journey.ts:169-182`), reading `journey_programs.deadline_days` live. `v_journey_progress` over it, one row per enrolment with `status <> 'reset'`, carrying `organization_id`; service role only, because an enrolment id must not open another member's state.
    - **Overview objects.** `v_product_enrollments` (product, instance, learner; a union of the three, service role only); `mv_product_dashboard` (the same grain, for paged drill-down); `mv_product_dashboard_agg` (organisation × product × instance, product roll-ups and one `all` row; counts per status, `overdue`, `unique_learners`); `mv_journey_enrollment_status` (program, version); `overview_unique_learners(p_org uuid) returns int`; `product_metrics_daily` (primary key `(organization_id, snapshot_date, product_kind, instance_id)` with `instance_id uuid not null default '00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000'`; the journey instance is the program; append-only; RLS on with an explicit no-access policy; service role only); `report_refresh_log` (`view_name`, `refreshed_at`, `duration_ms`, `rows`, `error`; RLS on with a no-access policy; service role only); `snapshot_product_metrics()` (idempotent per day).
    - **Recreated report matviews**, each with its unique index, `revoke select ... from anon, authenticated` and `grant select ... to service_role`: `mv_course_enrollment_status` (`0099:203-223`) and `mv_course_performance` (`0090:194-228`) over the Individual objects; `mv_path_enrollment_status` (`0099:234-272`) and `mv_path_performance` (`0090:231-278`) built on `v_path_step_status` and `v_path_progress` of 0107, keeping their column names and unique indexes: a step's done is the view's `done` (`passed_ever`, step 44), and the matviews hold no `pass_required` branch of their own (0099's branch at `:246-250` goes; it would be a second path rule that ignores `passed_ever`). The live-attempt column is read from `v_context_attempt_summary` on the same path key; `mv_course_interaction_breakdown` (`0031:338-376`) and `mv_course_interaction_top_wrong` (`0035:21-74`) with `context_kind` added to the grain and unique index and the official rule applied (their privileges live only in `0049:23-29` and are restated); `mv_leaderboard` (`0073:430-508`) and `mv_learner_metrics` (`0073:510-546`) with `avg_score` over Individual and path contexts, journeys excluded, and `done` from the summaries. New: `mv_product_leaderboard` (organisation, kind in individual or path, instance, learner), completions and average score from `v_context_attempt_summary`, refreshed nightly.
    - `attention_overdue_count` (from `0087:17-59`): three set-based arms (Individual, path, journey) counting distinct learners, done read from each product's rule, with the Custom Group arm the function lacks today.
    - **Feedback (D12).** `v_course_feedback_responses` (from `0106:252-306`) decides `is_latest` per (course, learner, question, `context_key`) and appends `context_kind`, `context_key`, `learning_path_id`, `journey_program_id`. `course_feedback_summary` gains a three-argument form `(p_course_id uuid, p_context_kind text, p_instance_id uuid)`; the one-argument function (`0106:327-405`) is kept as a wrapper for the Individual product, so `lib/interactions/feedback.ts:433` keeps working in either deploy order. Grants restated (`0106:305-306`, `:404-405`).
    - `journey_day_funnel_svc(p_program_id uuid, p_org uuid)`: a service-role sibling of `journey_day_funnel` (`0059:61-82`, which raises for analysts and for the service role), excluding reset enrolments; the caller asserts the organisation. It groups by day id and labels each day with its number in the program's current version (today's function groups by `day_number` across versions, `0059:72-78`, which mixes different days once a publish has moved them); credits on days no longer in the current version are reported in one separate count.
    - Both refresh functions are recreated again with the new matviews in their lists; `refresh_report_views()` also runs `snapshot_product_metrics()` in its own exception block and keeps 0107's call of the preview purge (step 81) with its `preview_purge` entry; each view's result is written to `report_refresh_log`.
    - After-checks: both refreshes with every `error` null, the 15-minute set and the nightly set timed separately against stated budgets; the privilege matrix; per-product counts equal counts by context on the seed.

### D. Launch and runtime

22. **`resolveLaunchContext(input)` in `lib/attempts/launch-context.ts`** (pure, unit-tested; no `next/*` or Supabase import). The page gives it strict reads made **before** `evaluate()` (today the path context is computed at `launch:819`, after the course-level evaluation at `:317-358`). Every read is error-checked, and a failed read refuses the launch with the retry notice; it never opens a step.

    | Launch link | Checks | Result |
    |---|---|---|
    | both `?lp=` and `?journey=` | none | refused, `context_conflict` |
    | `?mode=preview` | `canViewReports(role)` (`lib/auth/permissions.ts:14-16`); the course is in the organisation | `preview`: `is_preview = true`, `preview_kind = 'course'`, no official tag written, never settled by the writer. The launch mode is `Normal` (decision A32, answered by the owner on 2026-11-13; the Browse alternative is not built) |
    | `?mode=preview&run=R` (and `&day=<day id>` for a journey run) | `canViewReports(role)`; test run R is in the organisation and is the caller's own; the course is a step of R's path, or the day of R's pinned version holds it; the step or day is open in R by the same rule a learner meets, read over R's preview rows and R's simulated date (section T) | `preview:R`, or `preview:R:<day id>`: `preview_kind` `path_run` or `journey_run`, `preview_run_id = R`, no official tag, no Continue |
    | `?lp=P` | P is in the organisation and active; the learner has a recorded path enrolment, or P is "Everyone in this org" and the learner is an active member (step 24 enrols first); the course is a step; the step is released; in strict order every earlier step is done in P by `pathProgress()` | `path:P`, or `dashboard?locked=` / `?upcoming=` |
    | `?journey=E&day=<day id>` (a number is accepted from an old link and resolved through the pinned version) | E is the learner's and in the organisation; E is active, or completed (review, and the resume of an attempt that was open when a publish completed E; never a new official attempt); the program is active; then one of: the day is the open day of the pinned version and holds this course (active E only); or the day is credited and this course is the course on its credit row (review); or the day's context holds an open official attempt of this course (resume, even when the day has moved, holds another course now or was removed; step 93) | `journey:E:<day id>` |
    | no product parameter | a released direct assignment reaches the learner, or the course is "Everyone in this org" and the learner is an active member (step 24 enrols first) | `individual` |
    | no product parameter, not individually entitled | exactly one path or journey day holds the course for this learner → redirect into it; several → the course page's product chooser (built in phase 6; until then the dashboard, whose path and journey cards open their own product); none → refused as today | redirect |

    The facts for these checks come from one `launch_context` call (step 18) and the attempts read; a failed call refuses the launch. Which twin decides the lock: at launch the strict lock reads each earlier step's `done` from `launch_context`, that is from the SQL rule `v_path_step_status`, and the pure resolver applies the order to it. `pathProgress()`, the TypeScript twin, serves the pages that already hold the attempt rows (the path page, the dashboard tile, Continue) and is not called by the launch. `context-parity.test.ts` keeps the two equal, so "by `pathProgress()`" in the table above means the path rule, whichever twin reads it. The release gate and the strict lock apply only to the path named in `?lp=` (today every enforced path that holds the course gates the launch, `launch:667-749`; D2 drops that hold-back). The "Revisit your missions" link (`app/[org]/(learner)/journey/page.tsx:278-284`) gains `&day=`, so a review is tagged to its day. A credited day whose course a later publish swapped (changed by the independent review of round 4): the review opens the course on the credit row, the one the learner completed, as a revision visit of that attempt. The day's new course is not offered to a learner who completed the day, because the owner's third A27 sentence applies an update "only to learners who have not yet completed the affected day"; the round-4 text's optional official attempt of the new course is withdrawn, so a credited day never holds official results for two courses. On every surface a credited day shows the course, MOD code, status and score of the attempt on its credit row (steps 49, 55), and `journey_score` reads the same row. While a day's context holds an open official attempt of the course the learner started, the launch of that day resumes it and does not open the day's new course beside it (step 93).

23. **The launch page.**
    - `evaluate()` selects `context_kind, context_key, is_preview` and scores only in-context rows through `contextResult`. The `canManage` short-circuit (`launch:318`) is removed. These reads each gain the context filter: `lookupRow` (`:834-851`), the flagged close (`:978-994`), the stale-revision close (`:792-810`), the 23505 re-read (`:1067-1078`), slot routing (`:465-517`), the grandfather read (`:528-552`), `tokenExpiryTargets` (`:936-939`), `continueReady` (`:1243-1246`), and the grant snapshot (`readGrants` `:299-303`, `grantSnapshotFor` `lib/scoring/attempt-kind.ts:293-337`), so a path grant can never be consumed by an Individual launch.
    - The resume re-tag (`:870-884`) is deleted. The insert (`:1017-1026`) and its retry (`:1049-1055`) name `context_kind` with the tags; `attempt-insert-sites.test.ts` asserts that every `course_attempts` insert in `app/`, `lib/`, `tests/` and `scripts/` names it, that a journey insert names `journey_day_id`, that a journey insert in `app/` and `lib/` also names `journey_version_id` (step 13; not required of fixtures, where null is legal), and that a preview insert names `preview_kind` and no official tag (the test fixtures join the scan in the pull request of step 64 that changes them). The revision seed and `LMS.OfficialResult` come from the context's official attempt. `recordRevisionVisit` calls the seven-argument function, and its plain-insert fallback (`:1192-1199`) names `context_key`.
    - **Start and Preview (D7 and the owner's R6 answer of 2026-11-13).** `launchModeFor` (`lib/alds/launch.ts:59`) takes the mode from `?mode=preview`, not the role. Without the mode every role is a learner with real rows: "their attempt and score should be recorded exactly like a normal learner's attempt", and "For testing purposes, they must select **Preview mode**" (the owner, for `super_owner`, `admin` and `data_analyst`). This replaces the owner's rule of 2026-11-06 (ADR 0006 note (r)), as the owner said. A Preview insert writes `preview_kind` (`course`, or a run kind) and, under decision A32, `launch_mode = 'Normal'`, so the module behaves as it does for a learner and its score reaches Preview Reports (step 78). Role checks that change to read the mode or the attempt's context: `launch:242` (the access bypass passes `canViewReports(role) && mode === 'preview'`, which narrows it for admins and gives analysts a preview they lack today); `lib/auth/course-access.ts:59`; `launch:670-671` (the release-gate skip, preview only); `launch:1169`, `:1245`, `:1312`, `:1351`; the course page `app/[org]/(learner)/courses/[courseId]/page.tsx:97`, `:110-115`, `:126`, and `:289` (the "Request another attempt" button follows the attempt's context, so an admin's real attempt can request one); `continue:84-85` and `continueTarget`'s `isStaff` (`lib/learner/continue.ts:100`, `:127`), which are deleted, not re-keyed: a preview carries no `?lp=` (next bullet), the continue link exists only on a path launch (`launch:1227-1230`), so a preview never reaches the continue address, and the preview term of `continueReady` goes with them. This removes "Continue shows on a preview launched from a path", which the owner approved as ADR 0019 decision 3; the owner agreed on 2026-11-13 ("Preview mode should not have a Continue button", A21), and a test run's launch has none either (section T); `lib/attempts/restart.ts:18-22` with the restart route's count (`.../versions/[versionId]/restart/route.ts:191`); `lib/courses/learner-history.ts:14`, `:49`. These three (the restart helper, the restart route's count and the history check) tell a preview by `context_kind = 'preview'`, not by the name `is_preview`: the tier guard forbids that name under `lib/attempts/` and the other grading folders (`tests/unit/alds-tier-read-sites.test.ts:129-150`, `:171`), and the database ties the two together (step 5). On a database without 0107 the read falls back to no preview split (step 72's fallback-safe readers). `resolveLaunchContext` (in `lib/attempts/`) answers the kind and the preview kind as its own result fields; only the launch page and `lib/alds/launch.ts` name the columns `is_preview`, `launch_mode`, `preview_kind` and `preview_run_id` on the insert (step 62 states the one guard amendment). **Access to a started or credited journey day (added by the independent review of round 4).** The access check that runs before the resolver (`launch:237-249`, `lib/auth/course-access.ts:245-267`) grants a journey entitlement only when the pinned version holds the course at or below the calendar ceiling, so today it would refuse exactly the launches steps 22 and 93 admit: a started day that was removed, swapped or moved past the ceiling, and the review of a credited day whose course was swapped. For a `?journey=` launch the journey branch of `learnerCanAccessCourse` therefore also grants access when an active or completed enrolment of the learner holds an open official journey attempt of the course (`context_kind = 'journey'`, in progress, not a revision row) or a credit row for the course, whatever the pinned version and the ceiling; the resolver still decides which of the two it is. `launch-context.test.ts` gains the removed, swapped, moved-past-the-ceiling and completed-enrolment resume cases and the swapped-day review, and a unit test of the access check covers the same five.
    - **Buttons.** Start or Resume (a real attempt): the learner course page, each path step, each journey day, dashboard cards, Continue. A launch by someone not enrolled is refused with "You're not enrolled in this course" and, for report-viewing roles, a "Preview instead" button. Preview (not an official record): the learner course page, path step and journey day for report-viewing roles; the admin Library course page, the path editor and the journey program editor for admins; the course report pages for analysts (D10). A step or day preview opens the course outside the path or journey: its link carries `?mode=preview` and no `?lp=` or `?journey=`, so it has no Continue (the owner's A21 answer). To walk a whole path or journey without official records, the path editor, the journey program editor and Preview Reports carry a "Test run" button (section T). The launch bar shows "Preview: not an official record"; the plan's wording "Preview: not recorded" no longer fits, because the owner's R7 keeps preview results for Preview Reports for a limited time (reading R18). Dashboard cards and Continue never preview.

24. **Recorded enrolment on Start (D15) for courses and paths.** When step 22 finds no enrolment and the product is "Everyone in this org", the launch page inserts the enrolment before any attempt is read or written: a `course_assignments` or `learning_path_assignments` row with `assignee_type = 'user'`, `source = 'self'`, `assigned_by` the learner, no due date. It is a plain insert, and a 23505 answer is read as "already enrolled". It is not an upsert: the unique user indexes are partial (`0005:50-52`, `0008:51-53`, `where assignee_type = 'user'`), and an upsert through supabase-js cannot name a partial index as its conflict target, so every self-enrolling Start would be refused with 42P10. This is the house pattern of `app/api/assignments/route.ts:271-275`. The write uses the service client the page already holds (`launch:280-284`), because all three enrolment tables are admin-write under RLS (`0005:81-85`, `0008:115-119`, `0058:157-160`); it runs only after `requireOrgAccess`, an active-membership check and the visibility read, and it names the organisation. Eligible member means `organization_members.status = 'active'`. The page then re-reads the enrolment. An admin who later assigns a self-enrolled learner converts the row (`source = 'admin'`, `assigned_by`, the admin's dates): `app/api/assignments/route.ts:271-285`, `lib/assignments/direct.ts:63-77` and `app/api/learning-path-assignments/route.ts:183-193` (which swallows the 23505 today) change to do so.

25. **Next's fetch memoization (binding on this section).** Next 15.5 dedupes identical GET fetches within one server render. Any server-render read that repeats after a write, a race or in a loop must carry `.abortSignal(new AbortController().signal)`. Pull request 243 fixed the continue wait (`continue:156`), `readMy` (`launch:332-338`), `lookupRow` (`launch:845`) and `grantSnapshotFor` (`attempt-kind.ts:312`); production v1.4.0 still carries the defect. Every new re-read carries the signal: the enrolment re-read of step 24, any read `resolveLaunchContext` repeats after the entitlement redirect or a rerun, the per-context grant snapshot, and every read in `readContextResult`, including the in-context attempts read that replaces `readMy`. That last one matters because `evaluate()` runs twice in one render after `consume_grant` answers `used`, `stale`, `retake_live` or `stands` (`launch:959-968`); without the signal the second run would score the first run's rows, the defect pull request 243 fixed. An `.rpc()` call is a POST and is not deduped, so `launch_context` may be called again after the self-enrolment write; it must not be called with `{ get: true }`. Route handlers have no such cache, so the writer call in the two completion routes needs none. Two tests on spec 68: a write mid-render with the fresh answer asserted, and the `evaluate` rerun after a `consume_grant` answer of `used`.

26. **Runtime: no engine change.** The registration stays the attempt id (`launch:1392-1403`; `lib/alds/launch.ts:134-135`), and state, statements and SCORM data are already stored per attempt, so one row per context separates bookmarks, state, statements and scores with no change to any package. LaunchData gains three additive extensions, `context/kind`, `context/id`, `context/day`. The tenant LRS feed numbers attempts per context (`lib/lrs/context.ts:99-120`) and bumps `PROFILE_VERSION` (`lib/lrs/profile.ts:25`). Statements already sent are never renumbered.

27. **Continue and the status route.** The continue page reads through `readContextResult` in the `lp` path's context (today every context, `continue:148-161`), and each wait read keeps its own abort signal. A stored result that is not the latest pass but whose step is `passed_ever` continues to the next step instead of `?failed=1` (D14; `lib/learner/continue.ts:129`). `app/api/attempts/[attemptId]/result-stored/route.ts:62-125` scores only the attempt's own context (today the whole course, previews included). The language switch (`app/api/courses/[courseId]/language-preference/route.ts:131-247`) counts and abandons open rows only within one context. Its body (`{ orgSlug, language, restart_if_in_progress? }` today, `:18-22`) gains an optional `context: { kind, path_id | journey_enrollment_id + day_id }` (the day by its id, section U), default `individual`, validated with the entitlement part of `resolveLaunchContext` as step 37 does; a `preview` context is refused. Both callers send it: `launch-language-picker.tsx:50` sends the launch's own context, and `change-language-menu.tsx:193` on the course page sends `individual`. The saved preference stays one per learner and course. An open attempt of another product on the old language is left alone and is met at that product's next launch by ADR 0004 step 9, read per context (rule (2): the launch resumes that attempt's version). The cmi5 hold of ADR 0014 step 18 (`requires_finish`) is judged over the same in-context rows. Both records carry a dated note (step 77). The owner agreed on 2026-11-13 (A24) and asked how the previous attempt and its history are kept.

    **What a language switch keeps (the answer to the owner's A24 question; read in the code at `main` 1aeb339, LIVE, and unchanged by this step except for its scope).** No official result is deleted or overwritten by a language switch. (1) The route selects only open attempts that are not revision rows and not granted retakes (`language-preference/route.ts:131-173`). A completed, passed or failed attempt is never selected, so an official result cannot be touched; an open attempt cannot hold an official result, because every completing write sets a terminal status (`0099:228-231`). (2) Closing writes one thing, `status = 'abandoned'`, on the learner's own rows that are still in progress (`:238-253`). The row stays, with its progress data, score fields, statements and State documents. Nothing is deleted. Later writes to it are refused with 410, and it is never official and never in progress (ADR 0004 step 11). (3) After a completed official attempt, a switch closes nothing: the next launch in the new language is a revision visit seeded from the official attempt, written only to the revision row (`launch:1100-1146`). (4) A granted retake in progress is never closed. (5) What is overwritten: the saved preference, one row per learner and course, which keeps no history of earlier choices. (6) What the learner loses: the chance to continue the closed attempt. From this step on that is one product's open attempt, not every product's. On SCORM and plain xAPI that includes a partly taken in-module assessment (its answers stay on the closed row and never count); the hold of ADR 0014 step 18 protects cmi5 only, as today. (7) What is not stored today: why and when a row was closed. No such column exists, although the route's comment expects one (`:233-235`).

    **Added for that answer (decision A37).** (a) The route writes an audit row through the existing `auditLog` helper, as the per-version restart route does (`.../restart/route.ts:235-249`): action `course.language_switched`, the course, the old and new language, the context, and the ids of the attempts it closed; ids only. History then says which attempts a switch closed and when, without a schema change. (b) Rule (1)'s redirect (`launch:503-507`) goes to the launch page's own picker with the launch's context kept (`?lp=` or `?journey=&day=`, plus `?switch=<lang>`), not to the course page, whose menu sends `individual`; read per context, the old target would confirm a switch for the wrong product and loop. (c) A path step and a journey day whose course has two or more active packages show "Change language", which opens the same picker in that context; without it a learner with a saved preference could never switch an open path or journey attempt, because the picker shows only when no preference exists (`launch:385-431`). (d) The course page menu's `inProgress` prop is built from Individual rows only (`courses/[courseId]/page.tsx:620-634` reads every context today), and its confirm copy says what happens: an open attempt in this product is closed and restarts in the new language; a completed result is kept and later visits are revision visits (today's copy promises a reset in every case, `change-language-menu.tsx:302-308`). (e) The admin's per-version restart (ADR 0008) stays version-wide across every context, as step 38 says. It closes open preview rows of that version too, and for those it sends no "abandoned" statement to a tenant LRS (step 80).

### E. Completion: the writer's answer, the webhook, the emails

28. **Both routes call one function.** `commit:349-378` and `statements:306-339` replace `classifyAttempt`, `gamification_record_activity` and `journey_record_completion` with one call of `record_learning_activity(attempt_id)` per commit or statement batch. On an error or an unreadable answer the route treats the attempt as not official (today it reads official). `classifyAttempt` is retired in its four callers; a thin `classifyAttemptInContext` over `official_attempt_in_context` serves the hold sweep (`app/api/cron/gamification-refresh/route.ts:52`) and the Attention summary (`lib/attention/collect.ts:419`).

29. **The xAPI route keeps ADR 0015 and ADR 0017.** `official` from the writer's answer replaces `officialRun` as the input of `decideCompletionWebhook`; the decision itself (`fire`, `refire`, `hold`, `none`; `statements:340-355`; `lib/integrations/webhook-hold.ts:129-155`) is unchanged. The `LMS.OfficialResult` write of ADR 0017 step 19 (`statements:361-399`) takes the same `official`, so it follows the context's official attempt.

30. **The SCORM webhook joins the latch.** The inline send (`commit:425-455`) is deleted; the route calls `fireCompletionWebhookForAttempt` (`lib/integrations/webhook.ts:188-201`) on the writer's answer. SCORM messages are then latched like cmi5 ones.

31. **Webhook scope and the terminal marker.** The payload gains `context {kind, learning_path_id, journey_program_id, journey_enrollment_id, journey_day_id, journey_day}` and `context_attempt_number` (`journey_day` is the day's number when the attempt was launched; `journey_day_id` is its identity). A path-context message, which is sent only under the `all` scope, also carries `passed_ever` (boolean, from the writer's answer), so a consumer can tell a failed retake after an earlier pass from a step never passed; other messages do not carry the field. `org_integration_settings.webhook_contexts`: `individual` (the default: one `course_completed` per Individual official completion; `attempt_number` keeps its meaning) or `all` (one per product context; a non-Individual message sends `attempt_number: null` with the context, so it cannot be read as an Individual retake). Both settings reads (`webhook.ts:143-147`, `:231-235`) select the column with the missing-column fallback. When the scope excludes an official completion the route sets `webhook_skip_reason = 'out_of_scope'`; a later change of scope sends nothing for rows already marked. The hold sweep (`webhook-hold.ts:228-237`) selects `completion_webhook_at is null and webhook_skip_reason is null and side_effects_at is not null` for every manifest type (the `cmi5, xapi` bound at `:237` goes; the `pre_0107` marker of step 7 makes that safe). The sweep's date floor stays: `completed_at >= WEBHOOK_HOLD_SWEEP_FROM` (`webhook-hold.ts:166`, `:236`) is kept in the new select and in the fallback. The marker covers only SCORM rows; cmi5 and xAPI rows completed before the floor with a null latch carry no marker, and without the floor the sweep would send their messages months late. The staging probe's `fromIso` option is unchanged. On a database without 0107 the sweep's select falls back to today's select, type bound included, so the cron stays green there (principle 6, step 72). Without the marker the sweep, which scans three rows and fires two per run, newest first (`:187-189`), would fill with rows that can never fire. The sweep writes the marker itself when it finds a candidate that is not official or is outside the scope, so complete rows from before the paste that were never official leave the scan the first time they are seen. Nothing calls `/api/cron/gamification-refresh` on staging (staging has no cron; its pg_cron job runs the SQL refresh, not the route), so this TypeScript sweep never runs there by itself: spec 68's sweep and marker cases call the route directly with the staging cron secret (step 64).

32. **Emails (D16), sent from `notices` in the writer's answer by both routes.**
    - `asset_completion`: for an `individual` notice only. cmi5 and xAPI courses gain the email they lack today.
    - `path_completion`: for a `path` notice only. The in-route path computation (`commit:475-628`) is deleted, so group-assigned and self-enrolled learners now get it and a retake no longer re-sends it.
    - `journey_completion`: a new template, for a `journey` notice only. It touches the template check (step 8), `lib/notifications/types.ts:1-16`, the defaults in `lib/notifications/templates.ts`, the template route's allow-list (`app/api/notifications/templates/route.ts:4-15`) and the settings lists.
    - No email for a path step, a journey day, a revision visit or a preview.
    - After the send the route stamps `completion_notices.sent_at`. Emails are not swept: a failed send leaves the claim in place, is logged in `notification_log` as today and shows in the Attention Center's failed-email item. A completion repaired later by the retry sweep sends no email. Both are disclosed.
    - A journey completed by an admin's publish with `apply_to=all` (step 47) takes its claim and sends no email: the publish request cannot afford the sends on the Workers plan (step 70), and D16's build notes rule out a sweep. The owner agreed on 2026-11-13 (A20): "No completion email should be triggered solely because of the Admin's publish action." How that completion shows on the dashboard and in journey reports is step 49.

### F. Rewards (D8, D9)

33. **Keys in `gamification_record_activity_core`.**

    | Rule | Paid key (unchanged) | Event row (new, `xp = 0`, `event_only = true`) |
    |---|---|---|
    | `daily_activity`, streaks | `daily:<org>:<user>:<day>`; per streak run | none |
    | `course_completed` | `complete:<u>:<c>`: once per course, the first official completion in any context that is not a journey | `complete:<u>:<c>:<context_key>` |
    | `course_passed` | `passed:<u>:<c>` (a 0-point marker today, `0084:217-230`) | `passed:<u>:<c>:<context_key>` |
    | `perfect_score`, `high_score` | `perfect:` / `high:<u>:<c>`: once per course and tier, so a later context or a granted retake can add a tier not yet earned, never pay one twice | `...:<context_key>` |

    No new rule value. Event rows reuse their rule and are written outside the `v_amt > 0` branches (`0084:191`, `:205`). They follow the same anti-gaming gate as the paid `complete` candidate (`0084:150-155`). `v_attempt_no` and `v_extra_grants` count within the row's `context_key` through the official rule (today across the course with no revision or abandoned filter, `0084:167-181`), so each context's first official attempt is attempt 1 and can earn a bonus. Every new row writes `context_kind` and `context_key`.

34. **Journeys and previews.** The wrapper returns early when `context_kind = 'journey'`, and also when `context_kind = 'preview'` (added on 2026-11-13): between phases 2 and 3 on staging the old routes still call the wrapper directly (step 60), and a preview must write no points, daily activity or streak in that window either (the owner's R7). The membership exemption (`0058:233-243`) is removed: being on a journey no longer cancels points, daily activity or the streak for that course elsewhere. The badge is per enrolment (step 13).

35. **What reads what.** Counters and badges keep reading the paid keys. Per-product boards and reports read `v_context_attempt_summary`, not the event rows, because an organisation with gamification off writes no `xp_events` at all (`0084:107-109`). The tenant LRS points source (`lib/lrs/sweep.ts:557-600`) skips `event_only` rows, so the 0-point rows do not become statements. `gamification_close_month`'s `highest_scorer` chain (`0073:905-939`) reads the per-context summary over Individual and path contexts. `mv_leaderboard.badges_count` counts every badge row (`0073:454-458`), so a learner who completes two enrolments shows two journey badges; stated. The learner profile prints `user_badges.period` as text (`app/[org]/(learner)/profile/page.tsx:239`), so it changes to show the period only when it matches `^\d{4}-\d{2}$` and otherwise the program name from the badge's `metadata`; without that every journey badge would show the raw `enr:<uuid>` to the learner. A unit test covers both shapes.

36. **Why nothing pays twice.** `dedupe_key` has one unique index (`0053:113`); the paid key has no context in it; a second insert from any context, route, late save or sweep is `on conflict do nothing`. A row cannot carry two contexts (the CHECK) or change context (the trigger). Journey rows return before the core. History is stamped as handled.

### G. Grants, requests and revision visits

37. **Requests and grants carry a context.** `POST /api/attempt-requests` (`app/api/attempt-requests/route.ts:127`), approve (`[id]/route.ts:191`), bulk (`bulk/route.ts:209`, `:215`) and the ticket path into `grantExtraAttempts` (`lib/attempts/grant.ts:34`, inserts `:157`, `:166`) take `{kind, path_id | journey_enrollment_id + day_id}` (the day by its id; a request or grant on a journey day stays with that day when a publish moves it), validate it with the entitlement part of `resolveLaunchContext`, and decide through `readContextResult` in that context. A request in the `preview` context is refused. Individual is the default, so today's callers keep working. The grant readers in `lib/scoring/attempt-kind.ts` (`:293`, `:339`, `:402`, `:453`, `:479`, `:508`, `:548`, `:578`) and the continue page's grant read (`continue:112-118`) each take a context.
38. **Forced restart.** The re-issue (`lib/attempts/force-restart.ts:586-646`, inserts `:613`, `:623`) writes the new grant in the reset row's own context, and `approvePendingRequest` (`:528-576`) judges `stands` and matches the pending request in that context. The selection of rows to restart is unchanged: content is the same in every context. Preview rows are among them; `enqueueAbandonedStatements` (`lib/lrs/abandoned.ts:34-60`), which every abandon site calls, leaves them out of the tenant LRS feed (step 80, decision A35).
39. **Surfaces.** The grants queue shows a context chip. The request button appears on the course page (Individual), on a path step whose latest result failed, and on a failed journey day, each in its own context.
40. **Deletes.** `DELETE /api/learning-paths/[id]` gains `requireOrgAdminApi` and an organisation filter, answers 409 "This Learning Path has learner attempts. Deactivate it instead." on 23503, and 404 when no row was deleted. `DELETE /api/journey/program` answers 409 the same way; its comment at `:356-358` is rewritten. Each of the two routes, and the course delete route (`app/api/courses/[courseId]/route.ts:166-230`), first counts the attempts that block the delete, by step 85's rule for each product (for a course, its attempts that are not previews; for a path or a program, every attempt that carries its tag, a `legacy_role` row included), and answers 409 with nothing deleted when there is one; only then does it purge the product's preview data and delete, in step 85's order and with its refusals (step 85 is the one statement of the rule; built in this phase, priced in step 72). `course_ratings.path_id` stays `on delete set null` (`0031:39`): a rating is not an attempt, and no app code writes that table. Specs that delete paths directly (`tests/bot/specs/20-admin-writes.spec.ts:55`, `tests/bot/lifecycle/42-qr-deeplink.spec.ts:143`) change their teardown.

### H. One progress rule per product, and the learner pages

41. **`contextResult(i)` in `lib/scoring/context.ts`** is the only caller of `computeScoring` (`lib/scoring/policy.ts:306-412`). Input: the learner's attempts on one course (any context; the function filters by the official rule), the grant rows, the policy, the resolved `pass_required`, the context. Output: the in-context rows, `scoring`, `status`, `stored`, `done`, `liveRetake`. A row without `context_key` throws, so no reader is context-blind by accident. `readContextResult` in `context-read.ts` does the strict reads and answers `{ok: false}` on any error. `tests/unit/revision-listing.test.ts` is turned around in the ADR 0023 draft's form: a dated allow-list of today's direct `computeScoring` callers that each pull request shrinks and the last asserts empty.
42. **Individual.** The course page shows the Individual result and, beneath it, one line per path or journey in which the learner holds the course, linking to that product. The Library and dashboard cards read the Individual context only, open-row progress included.
43. **Path: `pathProgress()` in `lib/paths/progress.ts`**, the twin of `v_path_step_status` and `v_path_progress`. It replaces all five "step done" rules of the Context (row 7): the launch lock, the path page, the dashboard tile, the email rule (now the stored completion) and the path matview. The tenant LRS path statements (`lib/lrs/sweep.ts:361-457`) read `learning_path_completions` and the step view in place of raw path rows.
44. **Passed earlier (D14).** Field name, the same everywhere: **`passed_ever`** in SQL and JSON, `passedEver` in TypeScript; shown to people as "passed earlier".
    - `passed_ever`: some official attempt or granted retake of this learner on this step in `path:P` satisfied the step's resolved rule (passed where a pass is required, completed otherwise).
    - `latest_result`, `latest_score`: the standing official result of the context (ADR 0014's chain: a granted retake's result replaces the earlier one).
    - `done = passed_ever`. The strict lock, Continue, `steps_done`, the path completion insert and the dashboard tile read `done`. Reports, the CSV, the CRM `steps[]` and the path page show `latest_result` and `latest_score` with `passed_ever` beside them, so nothing is hidden.
    - A step counts as failed on reports and the Overview only when it is not `passed_ever`, its latest result failed and no granted retake is live.
    - The rule holds inside one path. It does not apply to the Individual product or to journey days, where the standing result decides.
45. **Journey.** `computeJourneyState` gains `blocked_failed`: the current mission's official result failed under the resolved rule and no unused journey grant exists. The journey page shows the failed day with its request button. `legacy_cross_context` credits stay credited for the learner (the history is theirs); the journey funnel and `mv_journey_enrollment_status` report them in a separate count; the LRS journey source (`lib/lrs/sweep.ts:459-511`) does not send them as attempt-backed days. A day's credit, once written, stands; after a re-pin an uncredited day needs an attempt on the day's current course.

    **The TypeScript twin of the day-identity rule (decision A27, step 13, section U).** `computeJourneyState` stops taking `completedCount` and takes `creditedDays: number[]`: the numbers, in the pinned version, of the days whose id holds a progress row. One helper in `lib/journey/`, `creditedDaysFor(version, progressRows)`, makes that list from the rows' `day_id` and the version's `day_ids`; a credit whose day is not in the pinned version maps to no number and counts towards nothing. No caller maps by `day_number`. `parseVersionDays` and `JourneyDayEntry` gain `dayId`. The twin derives: `completedCount` = the number of `courseDays` that are in `creditedDays`; `finished` = every course day is credited (today `completedCount >= missionsTotal`, `lib/journey/journey.ts:247`); `currentDay` = the lowest course day not credited (today `courseDays[completedCount]`, `:251`); `resumableDays` = the days whose context holds an open official attempt, passed in by the caller from its attempts read; calendar-mode pending and Behind count only released course days that are not credited. The parameter changes its name and type on purpose, so a caller that still passes a row count fails the type check. The journey page opens the lowest uncredited day and marks each day credited by its own row, not by position. Every reader of `journey_day_progress` that passes a row count or `progress.length` today changes in the same pull request (phase 2), because the twin's signature changes for all of them at once: the journey page (`app/[org]/(learner)/journey/page.tsx:154`, `:209`), the dashboard (`.../dashboard/page.tsx:215`), the launch page (`launch:188`), `lib/auth/course-access.ts:258`, the journey admin page and its client (`app/[org]/(admin)/journey-admin/page.tsx:77`), Learner Analytics (`app/[org]/(admin)/analytics/page.tsx:510`), the Manager Report Card (`lib/manager/insights.ts:372`, `lib/manager/actions.ts`), the two CRM feeds (`lib/integrations/progress.ts:533`, `app/api/integrations/learner-summary/route.ts:370`), the LRS journey source (`lib/lrs/sweep.ts:462`) and the nudges cron (`app/api/cron/journey-nudges/route.ts:178-203`, which passes the `credited` array of `journey_reminder_counts`; on a database without 0107 the column is absent and the cron falls back to the first `done` course days, which is today's reading). The publish route's own count (`app/api/journey/program/route.ts:153-157`) is deleted by step 47. The Manager Report Card's own status rule (row completed or count-finished, `lib/manager/insights.ts:405`) reads the twin's `finished`, so it cannot say completed on a rule of its own. `legacy_cross_context` credits count in all of these by their day id, as the learner's history; only the funnel, the matview and the LRS source set them apart, as said above. A source-scan test, `journey-credit-sites.test.ts`, asserts that no file outside `lib/journey/` derives a journey count from the number of progress rows.
46. **Per-step and per-day `pass_required`.** The step editor's PATCH (`app/api/learning-paths/[id]/courses/route.ts:20-58`) accepts `pass_required`. For days, three TypeScript places carry the key or it is dropped: the publish (`app/api/journey/program/route.ts:70-86`, `:102-117`), the curriculum PUT (`:606-668`), and `parseVersionDays` with `JourneyDayEntry` (`lib/journey/journey.ts:73-105`). Both editors warn when a module repeats (counts only). The journey editor's operations change with the day identity (step 92): rows carry their id, a move changes a day's number and not its content, and insert and remove exist as operations of their own; the curriculum PUT saves through `journey_save_days` in place of the upsert on the number (`route.ts:663-665`), which is a change to that route's behaviour and is recorded here as `CLAUDE.md` requires.
47. **The publish route.** With `apply_to=all` (`app/api/journey/program/route.ts:127-172`) it still re-pins active enrolments; completed and reset enrolments are never re-pinned, as today (`:139`). Its separate re-pin update (`:135-140`) and its per-enrolment loop (`:152-171`: a count read, an update and a badge insert for each enrolment) are deleted; it makes one call of `journey_repin_enrollments` naming the version it has just inserted (step 94), which re-pins and completes in one transaction, and reports its counts, `completed_now` among them. Before the publish the editor calls the same function as a dry run and shows the counts in the confirmation. A dry run names no version, because none exists yet: the route passes the would-be version (`days`, `day_ids`, `days_total`), built from the draft by the same TypeScript helper in `lib/journey/` that the publish uses to freeze a version, so the dry run compares each enrolment with what the publish will pin and not with the current version (which would always show 0). The version insert, the pointer update and the re-pin stay three requests, as today; if the re-pin fails after the pointer moved, the publish stands for new learners and the admin repeats "apply to all", which is safe because the function is idempotent for the program's current version. It sends no email (step 32, decision A20): one email costs 5 to 6 subrequests, so about seven completions in one publish would pass the 50-subrequest limit and stop the request after the version was already re-pinned. The response reports, as counts only: `repinned`; `kept_on_version` (enrolments left on their version by step 94's gate); `completed_now`; `sent_back` (enrolments whose open day moved to an earlier number, because a day was inserted or moved before their furthest day); `started_days_kept` (open attempts on a day that moved, changed course or was removed, all still resumable); `credits_now_history` (credits on days the new version no longer requires); `credited_by_rule_change` (step 95). The round-3 counts `failed_days_moved` and `days_reopened` are withdrawn: under the day-identity rule a moved day keeps its context and its credit, so neither case exists. The editor's "apply to all enrolled learners" confirmation says, before the publish, what the owner's A27 answer promises and what the counts show: nobody repeats a day they completed, nobody loses a day they started, and learners who have not completed a changed day get the new version of it. A journey version is frozen per publish, not per learner, and the record says so wherever it says "pinned". The loop's replacement is built in phase 2, with the TypeScript twin, not in phase 3: otherwise the route would still complete by count for one phase while the database and the journey page check per day. The route sent no email before and sends none after, so nothing of A20 is built by this. On a database without 0107 the call answers PGRST202; the route then completes nothing, reports `completed_now: 0` and logs it. This call is therefore a write path that is not safe in both deploy orders, the third one named under decision A19 (step 72), and it has no fallback under A19's recommendation: the paste-first rule covers it, because a database without 0107 that runs phase 2 code has no enrolment a learner could have credited. The owner allowed the departure on 2026-11-13 (A19), so no fallback is built.
48. **Journeys for everyone (D15).** `VisibilityRadio` (`app/[org]/(admin)/library/[courseId]/details-form.tsx:322-352`) gains a third `assetKind` and is used in the journey program editor. A new learner section, on the dashboard and the journey page, lists active programs with `visibility = 'org_public'` on which the learner has no active or completed enrolment. "Start journey" posts to `app/api/journey/self-enroll/route.ts`, which checks active membership, the program and its visibility, then inserts the enrolment with the service role: `source = 'self'`, `enrolled_by` the learner, `start_date` today, the program's current version. The insert is a plain insert and a 23505 from the partial one-active index (`0058:148-149`) is read as "already enrolled"; it is not an upsert, for the reason in step 24. The three existing writers name their source and keep their own skip rules: the admin POST (`app/api/journey/enrollments/route.ts:90-101`, `admin`; it skips an active enrolment only, so an admin can still enrol a learner again after a completion), `sync_audience` (`app/api/journey/program/route.ts:249-272`, `audience`; it skips active and completed) and the `journey_auto_enroll` trigger (`audience`). Self-start follows the `sync_audience` rule.

### I. Reports, feedback and the Overview

49. **One loader per product**, each reading its product's rule: `loadIndividualStatus` (today `loadEnrolledCourseStatus`, `lib/scoring/status.ts:245-302`), `loadPathStatus`, `loadJourneyStatus`. From the first pull request the readers tolerate the absent columns and apply the Individual filter when present.

    | Report | After |
    |---|---|
    | Course Reports, Library card, learners table, CSV | The Individual product. A product selector shows the same course "in path P" or "in journey J", read from that product's own views. The CSV gains `context_kind` and `context_name`. The "via: path" label goes (`app/[org]/(admin)/library/[courseId]/learners/page.tsx:325`; the CSV route `:245`) |
    | Revisits (the course Reports tile, the learners table, the learner course page, Learner Analytics) | Visits of the product on show: the Individual product reads `revision_visits` with `context_key = 'individual'`, and the product selector reads the chosen path's or journey's visits. Today every read is per course and learner with no context (`lib/attempts/revision-visits.ts:99`, `:179-181`; `.../reports/page.tsx:160`; `.../learners/page.tsx:580`; `analytics/page.tsx:823`; the course page `:230`). The CRM course rows' `revision_visits` (`lib/integrations/progress.ts:702`; `learner-summary/route.ts:296`) become the Individual count when the row is the Individual product (strict version 1 and version 2); a step's or day's count is its own |
    | Path Reports, Learners, index, CSV | One read of the path rule: latest score and `passed_ever` per step, `path_score` per learner. Custom Group assignments shown |
    | Journey admin | Journey-context attempts only (today grouped by learner and course in any context, `app/[org]/(admin)/journey-admin/page.tsx:215-236`); blocked-failed, late days, `journey_score`; a learners CSV; the page split into a read page and its write forms |
    | A journey completed by an admin's publish (the owner's A20 answer) | It is the same row state as a learner's own completion (`status = 'completed'`, `completed_at`, the badge, the claim with `origin = 'publish'`), so every surface shows it the same way: the journey page shows the completed view with the date, and the certificate is available; journey admin's table and Reports tab, Learner Analytics, the Manager Report Card, the Overview and both CRM feeds read "completed" from the row; reminders stop. The day figures beside it agree, because the publish completes an enrolment only when `journey_credit_state` says no required day is left, the same rule those surfaces derive their day counts from (step 45). The day funnel does not move: a publish adds no day credit. The learner dashboard today reads active enrolments only (`dashboard/page.tsx:155-166`), so any completion, a learner's own included, shows there only as the journey banner going away. Added (decision A38): the dashboard shows a "Journey completed" card with the program name, the date and a link to the journey page and certificate, for the learner's latest completed enrolment, read in the same query with `status in ('active','completed')` |
    | Learner Analytics, Organisation Reports | Three maps, one per product; a product dimension on every number and one "all products, unique learners" line |
    | Attention Center | Three overdue arms counting learners; pending requests with their context; completions whose side effects have waited over an hour |
    | Reminders cron | The Individual rule only (`app/api/cron/reminders/route.ts:189-222`). The cron emails every assigned learner who has not completed (`:18-23`), and a self-enrolment is a `user` assignment row, so without a rule a member who only pressed Start would begin to get `asset_reminder` emails. Recommended, decision A22: the cron skips rows with `source = 'self'` |
    | Manager Report Card | Course lines Individual; path lines from `v_path_progress`; the journey lens gains blocked-failed; `report_card_cache` gains a `schema_version` so old entries are not served |
    | Raw attempt readers in TypeScript (added by the independent review of round 4; built in phase 2, with Preview by mode) | Two official surfaces read `course_attempts` rows directly and skip only revision and abandoned rows: Learner Analytics' attempt tally and last activity (`app/[org]/(admin)/analytics/page.tsx:1008-1022`) and the Report Card's engagement "last active" (`lib/manager/insights.ts:507-515`). Each drops rows with `context_kind = 'preview'`, as does every other `.from("course_attempts")` read on an admin, manager, attention or CRM surface that does not go through `contextResult`. A staff member's previews then move no number and no date in an official report (the owner's R7). The source scan of step 62 asserts it. These readers, and `lib/lrs/abandoned.ts` (step 80), stay deploy-safe in both orders: on 42703, a database without 0107, each falls back to today's select with no preview filter (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` principle 6; no new-kind preview can exist there) |
    | A journey day after a publish swapped its course | A credited day shows the course, MOD code, status and score of the attempt on its credit row; an uncredited day shows the pinned version's course (step 22) |
    | Question analytics (D12) | Combined across the three products with a product filter (all, or one). Previews, revision visits and abandoned attempts excluded. With "all", distinct learners are counted over the filter, never summed across products |

50. **Learner feedback (D12).** "Latest" is per learner, course, question and product context. The course Reports section shows the Individual product by default, with the product selector. Path Reports and journey admin each gain a Learner Feedback section for their own context; ADR 0021 put those pages out of scope, so this is new work, priced in phase 7. ADR 0021 decision A.1 stands unchanged: feedback, names and comments are for `canManage` roles only. Readers to change: `readFeedbackSummary`, `readFeedbackComments`, `pageFeedbackRows` (`lib/interactions/feedback.ts:427`, `:453`, `:483`) and the CSV route.
51. **Analysts (D10).** `data_analyst` is RLS-plain. The Overview and the read parts of the three product report pages read with the service role after `canViewReports`, and every read names `organization_id = org.id`, because RLS no longer backs it up. The progress views carry `organization_id` for this purpose (`v_individual_enrollment_status`, `v_path_step_status`, `v_path_progress`, `v_journey_progress` and the two terms views; steps 9, 18, 21). This matters for the course loader, whose attempts read has no organisation filter today (`lib/scoring/status.ts:290`) and whose invoker view reads an incomplete set on an analyst session (`:218-222`). Gates that widen from `canManage` to `canViewReports` for reading only: course Reports (`.../reports/page.tsx:77`), course learners (`:131`) and its CSV (`:64`), path Reports (`:95`), path Learners (`:84`) and export (`:41`), the journey admin read page (`:38`). Never widened: the Learner Feedback section and its CSV (`feedback-section.tsx:46`, `app/api/courses/[courseId]/feedback/export/route.ts:50`), and every write form.
52. **The Overview** (`app/[org]/(admin)/overview/`, behind the flag `PRODUCT_OVERVIEW`; super_owner, admin, data_analyst). It reads three sources only: `mv_product_dashboard_agg`, `product_metrics_daily` and `overview_unique_learners`. It writes nothing; a unit test asserts that the module imports no writer and calls no writing function. (The two RLS audits in this repository check the database, not code, so they cannot assert that.)

    | Metric | Definition |
    |---|---|
    | Enrolled | Recorded enrolments of the product. Individual: per course. Path: per path. Journey: per enrolment that is not reset. A member of an "Everyone" product who never pressed Start is not enrolled |
    | Not started | Enrolled, with no attempt in that product |
    | In progress | Started, no final standing result, not failed. A live granted retake counts as in progress |
    | Completed | Individual: the official result is done. Path: the `learning_path_completions` row exists. Journey: the enrolment is completed |
    | Failed | Individual: the standing result failed and no retake is live. Path: a step is failed (step 44) and the path is not completed. Journey: blocked, failed |
    | Overdue | Not completed and past due: the due date of step 18 (Individual, path), or the journey's live deadline (D17) |
    | Behind (journey) | The `computeJourneyState` rule (step 21) |
    | Unique learners | Each person once across the three products; per-product learner counts are never summed |
    | Trends | Daily snapshots per product and in total; until the first snapshot, completions per week from `completed_at` |

    Not started, in progress, completed and failed split every enrolment exactly once. Every tile links to that product's own report. The matviews refresh nightly at 03:30 UTC with the existing report refresh (production `cron.yml:24`; staging pg_cron), which also writes the snapshot; the page shows the last refresh time from `report_refresh_log`. A journey deadline edit shows on the Overview after the next refresh; journey admin and reminders use it at once (D17). No new `/api/cron/*` route is added. The refresh route answers `ok: true` whenever the function returns (`app/api/cron/refresh-report-views/route.ts:39-54`), so it changes to report a failed view, snapshot or sweep entry in its heartbeat detail.

### J. CRM and Yoddha (D11 and the CRM ID requirement)

53. **A contract version per API key.** `org_api_keys.contract_version`: 1 for existing keys, 2 for new keys, by the column default set in 0107 (step 8, decision A28); the key route is not changed. `?contract=` is honoured only when the key's `allow_contract_override` is true. `authenticateApiKey` (`lib/integrations/auth.ts:36-80`) reads the column with the missing-column fallback.
54. **Version 1 is strict.** It keeps today's fields and reads the Individual product only. `courses[]` holds one row per course the learner holds individually. A learner who holds a course only through a path or journey has no Individual result there. Option (b), showing another product's status on that row, is ruled out. If Yoddha cannot move to version 2 before production's first learner, option (c) returns to the owner as a decision; this record does not build it.
55. **Additions, per endpoint** (additive in versions 1 and 2 unless marked). The two endpoints differ today and keep their own key names:

    | Field | `progress` (`lib/integrations/progress.ts`) | `learner-summary` |
    |---|---|---|
    | Path object | `learning_paths[]` gains `steps[]`, `path_score`, `context` | `paths[]` gains `steps[]`, `path_score`, `context` |
    | Each step | `course_id`, `code` (MOD), `status`, `score`, `passed`, `passed_ever`, `attempts`, `completed_at`, `revision_visits`, `target`. A step carries no `context` of its own: every step of a path shares the path object's `context` (`{kind: 'path', path_id}`) and inherits it | the same |
    | Journey object | `journeys[]` gains `context` (`{kind: 'journey', journey_id, enrollment_id}`), `journey_score`, `journey_status` (blocked-failed included); `days[]` gains `day_id`, `course_id`, `code` (MOD), `status`, `score`, `passed`, `late`, `target` beside today's `day`, `completed` and `completed_at`. Each day carries its own `context` (`{kind: 'journey', journey_id, enrollment_id, day_id, day}`), because each day is its own context. `day_id` is the day's stable identity; `day` is its number in the learner's pinned version and can change when an admin republishes, so a consumer that stores per-day records keys them on `day_id` (the addendum says so). A day's credit is looked up by `day_id`. For a credited day, `course_id`, `code`, `status`, `score` and `passed` come from the attempt on the credit row, so a day whose course a later publish swapped still reports what the learner completed; for an uncredited day they come from the pinned version's course and the day's context | `journeys[]` gains `enrollment_id`, `context`, `days[]` (the same shape, each with its `context`), `journey_score`, `journey_status`; its one count request per enrolment (`route.ts:369-372`) becomes one batched read |
    | Course rows | `context: {kind: 'individual'}` (version 2) | the same |
    | `summary` | per product, plus unique learners (version 2) | the same |
    | Launch targets | carry `?lp=` or `?journey=&day=` | the same |

    The feeds' `context` uses the feeds' own key names (`path_id`, `journey_id`, `enrollment_id`, `day`), which Yoddha already keys on. The webhook's `context` (step 31) keeps the attempt row's column names. The difference is deliberate, and the addendum states both shapes side by side. So every record carries a context, its own or its parent's (the owner's CRM ID requirement, item 2): path and journey objects and journey days in both versions, steps through their path, course rows in version 2.

    **Who is listed, and unreleased steps (decision A29).** Both feeds expand enrolment in TypeScript today (`lib/integrations/progress.ts:421-479`; `app/api/integrations/learner-summary/route.ts:70-150`) and list what is assigned whether or not it can be opened yet: every step of an assigned path, released or not (`docs/CRM_INTEGRATION.md:531-540`, kept on purpose by ADR 0020 decision 2). This record keeps that contract in both versions. The feeds keep their own expansion over the three enrolment tables, with the group arm they already have, and do not move onto the report views, whose release gates would silently drop rows a consumer receives today. `steps[]` lists every step of the path in step order. So that a consumer can tell assigned from openable, each step, and each course row in version 2, gains `released` (boolean) and `release_at` (additive). A feed can therefore list a learner or a step the LMS reports do not count yet; the addendum repeats the existing paragraph of `docs/CRM_INTEGRATION.md` and adds the two fields. What does change is the course list: a course reaches `courses[]` only through a recorded direct assignment, never through a path (step 54).

56. **Product-wise access.** `progress?path_id=` is new: an id or a PTH code (`resolveIdsOrCodes` already accepts `learning_paths`, `lib/reference-codes.ts:18`, `:94`), mirroring `journey_id`. It is additive and available in version 1 and in version 2. It returns only learners with a recorded enrolment in those paths and only those paths' records. `course_id` returns the Individual course only, in version 2 and in strict version 1; a course's results inside a path come through `path_id`, inside a journey through `journey_id`. Learner-wise access (use case 1) is `learner-summary?employee_id=` and `progress?employee_id=`, each with the three sections. Yoddha keys journey records on `enrollment_id`: a restart is a new enrolment under the same `journey_id`. The feeds read recorded enrolments, so a self-enrolled learner appears like any other.
57. **Two gates.** Version 1's meaning changes even where its fields do not: a path step stops counting a Library completion, and under the default scope a path or journey completion sends no `course_completed`. (1) The release gate: a dated addendum to the consumer notice of PR 1b, and Yoddha's written agreement, are in hand before the production tag of step 74. (2) The first-learner gate (step 74a): steps 53 to 56 are deployed on production and Yoddha reads version 2 before production has its first learner, as D11 requires. Under strict version 1 a learner who holds a course only through a path reads "not started", which is what D11 was chosen to avoid, so an agreement alone is not enough. If Yoddha cannot read version 2 by then, R4 applies and option (c) goes back to the owner. The plan's timing paragraph had the same gap: it gated only on the addendum and the agreement. The addendum covers: one official attempt per course in each product; journey days credited only from journey launches; a final fail keeps that product's step or day not done, with `passed_ever` explained; SCORM messages now latched and held like cmi5; `attempt_number` null for non-Individual messages under the `all` scope, and `passed_ever` on path messages; the `path_id` filter; `context` on every record. Added by the independent review of round 4, because round 4 changed the contract after this list was written: `day_id` on each day and in its context, with the instruction "key per-day records on `day_id`; `day` may change after a republish" (step 55); `released` and `release_at` on steps and course rows (A29); previews are left out of both feeds and the webhook, which count and send them today (step 80); a staff member's Start is listed as a real learner record (the owner's R6). In a draft sent before acceptance (proposal P5) these four lines are marked pending. `docs/CRM_INTEGRATION.md`, the tech team's guides and the CRM live harnesses (outside this repository) gain version 1 and version 2 runs.

### K. Existing records

58. **No row moves and no existing value changes.** 0107 labels each attempt from its own columns (step 5), marks completed rows as handled (step 7), labels grants as Individual and visits from their attempt (step 8), and labels journey day credits that did not come from a journey attempt (step 8). Production has no learners, so every count there reads 0.

    | Case (staging counts of 2026-11-11) | Count | Rule |
    |---|---|---|
    | Attempt with both tags, not a preview | 0 | Paste refused if any; spec 46's fixture (`tests/bot/lifecycle/46-lrs-real-lrs.spec.ts:132`) is corrected first |
    | `journey_day` set, enrolment deleted | not counted yet | Paste refused. On staging the owner resets them as test data first (the standing direction of 2026-10-18). Production: 0 |
    | Preview row carrying a tag | not counted yet | Labelled `preview`; tags left as they are (step 6) |
    | Path tag cleared by an earlier path delete | cannot be known | Labelled Individual; disclosed |
    | Journey tag on a day that holds another course | 1 | Labelled journey, with that day's id; kept. If it is already completed it is marked handled and nothing is credited; if it is still open and completes later, it credits its day (step 13) |
    | Rows flagged `is_preview` today | not counted yet (Q10) | Labelled `preview` with `preview_kind = 'legacy_role'`; kept; never selected by the purge (step 82) |
    | Untagged completions by learners who hold the course only through a path or journey | 45 | Labelled Individual, as history; the learner's next product launch opens that product's own attempt |
    | Points from path attempts under the per-course key | 51 | Stand: the paid key is still per course |
    | Points rows pointing at deleted attempts | 348 | Stand; no context |
    | Journey day credits not from a journey attempt | 3 | Labelled `legacy_cross_context`; kept and still credited |
    | Lifetime journey badges (`period` null) | all | Untouched |

59. **Between the 0107 and 0109 pastes** (weeks on staging; minutes on production, where both are pasted in one sitting, section P). The old-name summary views are the Individual product, so: course matviews are already right; `mv_path_enrollment_status` and `mv_path_performance` (no app reader, `0049:15`, `0099:197`) count Individual results for path learners and are wrong until 0109; `mv_leaderboard` and `mv_learner_metrics` average Individual scores only until 0109 adds path scores. No application code reads the old-name views directly (the only mentions in `app/` and `lib/` are comments). They reach pages through `mv_course_performance`, which serves the course Reports tiles (`app/[org]/(admin)/library/[courseId]/reports/page.tsx:139`) and the Manager Report Card benchmark (`lib/manager/insights.ts:693`). Both show the Individual product from the next refresh after 0107, the Report Card benchmark included. No learner is counted twice and no refresh fails. This is disclosed on staging, not hidden.
60. **Staging window between the 0107 paste and the phase 2 deploy** (days). The old code's inserts get their kind from the derive trigger. Its resume re-tag is refused by the trigger and fails soft, so a journey learner who resumes an untagged open row completes it as an Individual attempt and the day is not credited; the next launch from the journey page opens a journey attempt and repairs it. An Individual grant consumed from a `?lp=` launch answers `unavailable`. Until phase 3 deploys, the old routes still call the recreated `gamification_record_activity` and `journey_record_completion` directly, so points and day credit work as the new bodies define them, but nothing calls the completion branch of the writer except the sweep: a path completion is stored by the nightly sweep on staging, the sweep claims the notice and no path or journey email is sent for it, and the old SCORM-only course email keeps going out from the old route. In the same window the old TypeScript still opens a journey day by the count while the database completes by day identity (A27); the two differ only for an enrolment re-pinned to a version that moved day numbers. The old editor still moves content between numbered rows and the old publish writes no `day_ids`; the paste-window trigger of step 91 fills them by number and marks the version `slot_backfill`, so step 94's gate covers every version published in the window. Old launch code still flags previews by role; the derive trigger marks them `legacy_role`, and the purge never selects them, unless A33 is answered (b) and the switch is on, when the retention run removes the aged ones that nothing protects (step 82; qualified on 2026-11-13). **Previews between phases 2 and 3.** From phase 2 a Preview is a row of a new kind, but the two write routes are the old ones until phase 3, and they send the SCORM course and path emails and the webhook inline whenever their classification reads official (`commit:386-626`, `:425-455`; `statements:340-353`), which reads no context. So phase 2 itself adds one check to both routes, before any side effect: a row whose `context_kind` is `preview` is never official, and nothing is sent or called for it. Phase 3 replaces the check with the writer's answer, which says the same. Without it a mode-based preview on staging would send an email and a webhook for one phase, and spec 75 could not pass before phase 3. Staging is test data.
61. **Reverse scripts**, staging only, never production with learners: `ops/0107-reverse.sql`, in this order: (1) it refuses if any (learner, version) has two open rows, or if 0109 is still applied; (2) it restores the earlier bodies of every object 0107 recreated, so that nothing still reads a context column: the 0105 enrolment view, the 0103 scoring and summary views, `consume_grant` and `record_revision_visit`, the 0089 `journey_record_completion`, the 0084 XP core, the 0058 wrapper, the 0073 `gamification_close_month`, the 0089 `journey_reminder_counts` (by drop and create, because 0107 changed its return type), the 0096 `journey_auto_enroll`, the 0056 and 0035 refresh functions, and the 0065 template check; (3) it drops the new objects that depend on the context columns, each with `if exists`: the seven-argument `record_revision_visit(uuid, uuid, uuid, uuid, uuid, text, text)` (step 11; it is new, so restoring the 0103 six-argument body in part (2) does not remove it, and it reads `revision_visits.context_key`, which part (4) drops), `launch_context`, `sweep_completion_side_effects`, `record_learning_activity`, `journey_complete_enrollment`, `journey_complete_enrollments`, `journey_complete_enrollments_core`, `journey_credit_state`, `official_attempt_in_context`, `instance_pass_required`, `v_path_progress`, `v_path_step_status`, the two terms views, `v_context_attempt_summary`, `v_context_attempt_scoring`, `completion_notices`, `learning_path_completions`, then its trigger function `learning_path_completions_append_only()` (after the table, because the table's trigger depends on it and dropping the table drops only the trigger), and `context_derive_log`; (4) it drops the two triggers and their two trigger functions, the CHECKs and the context indexes, each with `if exists`, because 0108 may already have dropped the derive trigger, its function and `context_derive_log`; it recreates the 0062 and 0083 indexes, re-creates the two attempt foreign keys `on delete set null` (0034, 0058), and only then drops the generated and added columns; (5) it restores the table-level privileges of `org_api_keys`. Journey completions that the day-identity rule made, and enrolments it left active, are not recomputed by the reverse; staging is test data. Added on 2026-11-13, in the same parts: part (1) also refuses while any preview row of a new kind, any `preview_runs` row or any soft-removed `journey_days` row exists (the owner purges or resets them first; test data); part (3) also drops `journey_save_days`, `journey_repin_enrollments`, `purge_preview_data`, `preview_purge_log`, then its trigger function `preview_purge_log_append_only()` (after the table, as for `learning_path_completions`), and `preview_settings`; it does not drop `preview_runs` (corrected in round 5: the text dropped that table here, while `course_attempts.preview_run_id` still referenced it, which stops the script with 2BP01); part (4) drops `preview_runs` as its last statement, after `preview_run_id` is gone; part (4) also drops the paste-window `day_ids` trigger and its function with `if exists`, re-creates `unique (enrollment_id, day_number)` on `journey_day_progress` before dropping `day_id`, restores `journey_days.day_number` to `not null` and drops `removed_at`, and drops `journey_versions.day_ids` and `day_ids_source`, `course_attempts.journey_day_id`, `preview_kind` and `preview_run_id`, and `attempt_requests.journey_day_id`. Added by the independent review of round 4: part (1) also refuses, and prints the counts so the owner resets those staging rows first, while any (enrolment, day number) holds more than one `journey_day_progress` row (the old key that part (4) re-creates would fail with 23505; two credits of one enrolment can share a number after a move, which is why 0107 dropped it), and while any (learner, course) holds more than one request matching the 0083 predicate (the same failure for the index part (4) re-creates, once a learner has open requests in two contexts); part (2) restores the 0103 body of `consume_grant` and part (3) drops its day-id form and `journey_attempt_satisfies`; part (4) also drops `course_attempts.journey_version_id`. `ops/0109-reverse.sql` restores the 0087, 0099, 0090, 0073, 0031 and 0106 bodies, and restores **0107's** bodies of the two refresh functions, never the 0035 and 0056 ones: those would remove the completion sweep, the only retry of the fail-closed writer. A code rollback needs neither, because readers tolerate the new columns. Statements and points rows written with context keys stay.

### L. Edge cases

| Case | Behaviour | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| Same course on days 3 and 7 of one journey | Two official attempts, two scores, two day credits | `journey:E:<id of day 3>`, `journey:E:<id of day 7>` (D1) |
| Same course in paths P and Q | Two official attempts; P moves nothing in Q; points paid once | `path:P`, `path:Q`; the editor warns |
| Course held individually and in a path | Two statuses on the course page; a Library completion never unlocks a path step | `individual`, `path:P` |
| Retake by grant | Opens only in the grant's product; abandons and replaces only that context's attempt; history kept; in progress while live | Step 10 |
| Failed official attempt | Stands as that product's result. A path step never passed keeps later strict steps locked until a path grant. A failed required journey day blocks the journey until a journey grant | ADR 0017 per row; steps 44, 45 |
| Failed granted retake after an earlier pass (path) | Later steps stay unlocked; path progress and a stored completion are unaffected; the step shows its latest result and "passed earlier" | `passed_ever` (D14) |
| Course updated to a new version | Open attempts restart in every context (ADR 0011); grants re-issued in their own context; finished attempts untouched | Step 38 |
| Future release date | An Individual release gates only the Individual launch; a step release gates only that path step; a journey day opens by its own unlock mode | Step 22 (D2) |
| Missed journey days | Calendar catch-up as today; each caught-up day is its own attempt, marked late | Step 13 |
| Journey deadline edited | Every enrolled learner's deadline and overdue follow at once; snapshots keep earlier days' figures | Live read (D17) |
| Learner in all three products | Three open attempts at once; three bookmarks, results and report rows; one unique learner | The open-row index per context |
| Unenrol, then re-enrol (course or path) | The same history returns; "take again" is a grant | No run instances (D5) |
| Journey reset and re-enrol | A new enrolment and fresh contexts; old attempts stay with the old enrolment and leave every count | New enrolment id |
| Finished journey, learner reviews a day | A revision visit tagged to that day | Step 22 |
| Path edited after enrolment | An added step lowers steps done; a completed path stays completed | The stored completion |
| Journey republished to all enrolled learners (the owner's A27 answer) | A completed day is never asked for again; a started day stays resumable and its completion still credits it; a learner who has not completed a changed day gets the new version of it; no attempt or credit row is changed | Credit and context by day identity (steps 13, 93 to 95) |
| Republish: a day removed (its module cleared, the day removed, or `days_total` cut) | Completed: the credit is kept as history and counts towards nothing; nothing is retaken. Started: the attempt stays resumable from the journey page and can be finished; its result and a credit row are kept as history and count towards nothing (reading R13). Not started: the day is skipped. A shorter version whose required days are all credited completes at the publish; a learner who then still holds an open attempt on a removed day can resume and finish it in the completed enrolment, and its credit is written as history (steps 13, 22) | Step 95 |
| Republish: a day's course swapped | Completed: the credit stands; the day keeps showing the course the learner completed and its review opens that course; the new course is not offered to them. Started on the old course: the learner finishes the course they started, and that credits the day; the new course is not opened beside it (reading R13). Not started: the new course | Step 95 |
| Republish: a day inserted before the learner's current day | Completed days keep their credit under their new numbers.
