The started day, now one number later, stays resumable and credits on completion. The inserted day has no credit, so it becomes the open day and the learner goes back to it; in calendar mode it counts as behind at once (reading R15) | Step 95; `sent_back` |
| Republish: days reordered | Credits and open attempts follow their days. An uncredited day moved before the learner's furthest day becomes the open day | Step 95 |
| Republish: a required flag changed | A rest day that gains a module is a new required day with no credit. A mission day that becomes a rest day is a removed day. A per-day pass rule changed: a credit stands; a learner who started the day before the publish is credited when the result satisfies the rule of the version the attempt was launched under or the new rule, so a rule made stricter does not fail started work (reading R14, asked with R13; step 13); a rule relaxed on a day whose official attempt already satisfies it is credited at the publish. A course's own `pass_required` is not versioned: a change reaches every started and future journey day at once, with no publish (`0089:46-51`; disclosed) | Step 95; `credited_by_rule_change` |
| Republish: a completed enrolment and a new day | Never re-pinned; it stays completed, with its badge and certificate | The re-pin selects active enrolments only (step 47) |
| An attempt started before a republish and finished after it | Credited to its own day whatever the day's number or course is now; kept as history if the day was removed | The day-identity check of step 13 replaces the round-3 day-course check |
| Republish reaching an enrolment pinned to a version published before the new editor | Re-pinned only when the first changed day comes after the learner's furthest credited or started day; otherwise the enrolment stays on its version and is counted. Expected 0 on production | Step 94's gate; `kept_on_version` |
| Two last days of one enrolment are credited at the same moment | One completion, one badge, one claim; neither credit is missed | The enrolment row lock before the credit insert (step 13) |
| Republish moves a failed required day to another number | The day keeps its identity, its context and its failed result; the learner still needs a journey grant. The round-3 consequence, a fresh official attempt without a grant, no longer exists. One case remains: a course swap on a failed day. The new course has its first official attempt in that day's context; it is a different course, not a retake | The context key names the day id (step 5) |
| Preview or test run of a path or journey | Nothing official is written: no enrolment, credit, completion, points, badge, email, webhook, CRM row or tenant LRS statement. The run's progress is shown in Preview Reports from its preview attempts and is deleted on request or by age | Sections S and T |
| "Everyone in this org" product | Any active member finds it and self-enrols by pressing Start; then exactly as an assigned learner | Recorded enrolment, `source = 'self'` (D15) |
| Visibility switched back to Assigned only | Self-enrolled learners keep their enrolment and records; nobody new can self-enrol | The row exists |
| Staff member who also learns | Start is a real attempt; Preview is its own context; the two never collide or resume each other | `preview` in the open-row key (D7) |
| Path or journey program deleted with attempts | Refused, 409; archive instead | Foreign keys `no action`; step 40 |
| Forged `?lp=` or `?journey=` | Refused before any write | Step 22 |
| Completion side effects fail | No points, credit, webhook or email; the save succeeds; the sweep retries and the webhook then fires; no email | Steps 12, 16, 31, 32 |
| Result arrives in two requests (completed, then passed) | The completion branch runs again for the new state; nothing pays twice | `side_effects_sig` |
| Late save on an old completed attempt | No second payment, credit, webhook or email | Markers of step 7; the claim of step 15 |
| Gamification switched off | No points rows at all; emails, path completion and day credit still work | Steps 15, 35 |

63. **PGlite rehearsal** (`scripts/migration-rehearsal/run.mjs`): scenarios `0105-0106-then-0107`, `0106-then-0105-then-0107` (the order production takes if an interim sitting pastes 0106 first), `0107-then-0108`, `0107-0108-then-0109`: four scenarios, entered in `expectations.json`. Files, in the house form of that folder: `grants-0107.sql`, `seed-0107.sql`, `assert-0107.sql`, `assert-0108.sql`, `assert-0109.sql`. **Grants first.** The rehearsal's shim gives the Supabase roles no grants and no default privileges (`scripts/migration-rehearsal/shim.sql:5`), so on it a write as `authenticated` raises 42501 before 0107 as well, and a view read as `authenticated` raises 42501 from the base tables instead of returning rows: both privilege checks below would pass or fail for the wrong reason. `grants-0107.sql` therefore runs before 0107 in all four scenarios, in the form of `asrole-grants.sql`: `grant usage on schema public to anon, authenticated`; `grant select on all tables in schema public to anon, authenticated`; and `grant insert, update, delete on public.org_api_keys to authenticated`, the table-level privileges the project defaults gave that table (step 8). Each privilege assertion then has a control that shows it can fail. Seed: learners I (Individual only), P (path only), B (both), J (a journey with one course on two days), O (an "Everyone" path, self-enrolled), S (a staff member who also learns), a completed row of every kind from before the paste, a completed SCORM row, a tagged preview row, an orphaned journey-day row, a deleted-path row. Assert: the second paste changes nothing, proved on rows the new code could have written: between the first and the second paste the rehearsal inserts a completed row with `side_effects_sig` null (a failed writer call) and a completed SCORM row with `completion_webhook_at` null and `webhook_skip_reason` null (a held webhook), and after the second paste the first is still a sweep candidate and the second carries no marker; the derive trigger's firing is recorded in `context_derive_log`, and 0108 refuses within 24 hours of a firing and passes after it; Q2 refuses the orphan and passes without it; old completed rows carry `side_effects_sig = settle_sig` and SCORM rows the `pre_0107` marker; the CHECK refuses a both-tag insert and a preview without `is_preview`, and admits the old tagged preview; the derive trigger labels a tagless insert, and after 0108 that insert fails; three open rows coexist in three contexts, a preview and a real attempt coexist, and a second open row in one context raises 23505; the immutability trigger raises for the service role; `consume_grant` in `path:P` leaves the Library row open, judges `stands` against P only, and refuses a grant whose context differs from the launch; the six-name `record_revision_visit` call in SQL named notation resolves with both signatures present (PostgREST's own overload choice is not testable here: the rehearsal runs no PostgREST, so the six-name and seven-name `.rpc()` calls are a case of spec 68 on staging); the writer runs the activity branch on an incomplete row, runs the completion branch again when `completed` becomes `passed`, pays nothing twice, skips previews and revision rows and never touches a stamped row; the sweep finds no row from before the paste and survives one raising row; `journey_record_completion` credits only journey rows, credits a started attempt to its own day after a re-pin moved the day or swapped its course, writes a history credit for a removed day, sets `late`; `journey_complete_enrollment` answers true on the first call and false on a second, sequential call (PGlite is one connection, so two concurrent callers are a case of spec 69 on staging); `journey_complete_enrollments` returns each completed enrolment once and takes one claim each; the day-identity rule after a re-pin (A27), on enrolments re-pinned by `journey_repin_enrollments` after the draft was edited through `journey_save_days`, each with a learner who completed days 1 to 3 and holds an open attempt on day 4: (a) a day inserted at number 2: the three credits and the open attempt keep their day ids, `next_day` is the inserted day, the open attempt's day is in `resumable_day_ids`, its completion credits its own day, and no attempt or progress row changed between before and after (compared column by column); (b) two days swapped: credits follow their days and no credited day is `next_day`; (c) the course on day 4 swapped: the open attempt completes and credits day 4, and no second attempt can be opened in that context while it is open; (d) day 4 removed: the open attempt completes, a credit row is written, `credited_days` does not count it, and the enrolment completes when the remaining required days are credited; (e) the new version is shorter and every required day is credited: the function completes it at once with one badge and one claim, `completed_at` set; (f) a completed enrolment is not re-pinned; (g) an enrolment pinned to a `slot_backfill` version with a change at or below its furthest day is kept on its version and counted, and one with the change after it is re-pinned; (h) a per-day pass rule relaxed on a failed day credits it at the publish; `journey_save_days` moves two rows in one call without a unique violation and never leaves an active row without a number; the backfilled `day_ids` of a seeded version equal the `journey_days` ids by (program, number), and Q8 refuses a version day with no `journey_days` row; in all of these, `journey_reminder_counts.done` and `journey_credit_state.credited_days` agree (`assert-0107.sql`), 0109's `journey_enrollment_state` agrees with both (`assert-0109.sql`, since the function does not exist before 0109), and the parity check runs the same fixtures through `computeJourneyState`; the path views and the terms views, read as `authenticated`, return the member's own organisation's rows (the control: more than 0, and no 42501) and 0 rows for another organisation; on `org_api_keys`, as a super owner's `authenticated` session, an update of `name` succeeds before 0107 (the control: the seeded table privilege and the policy are in force) and, after 0107, an update naming `contract_version` or `name` raises 42501, while an insert through the granted columns and an update of `is_active` still succeed; the key inserted that way reads `contract_version = 2` and the seeded key from before the paste reads 1; the file is created in the order at the head of section B (the first paste, on a database at 0106 where none of 0107's objects exist yet, is the test); a claim taken by `journey_complete_enrollments` reads `origin = 'publish'` and a claim taken through the writer reads `origin = 'learner'`; `launch_context` returns, for learners P, O and J, the same facts as direct selects on the seed: `sequence_mode`, each step's `step_number`, `release_at` and `done`, the self-enrolment row, and for J the `start_date`, `unlock_mode`, `count_sundays`, the timezone, `credited_days` and `next_day`, and it returns no enrolment facts for a learner of another organisation; `v_context_attempt_summary` has one row per (course, learner, `context_key`) and keeps a learner whose only rows are reset attempts, with `live_attempt_count` 0; the writer's path completion agrees with `v_path_step_status` on the D14 fixture; one claim per (learner, product, instance) in `completion_notices`, with gamification off as well; points under D8(c): learner B completes C at 0.95 individually, then at 1.0 in P, then at 1.0 in Q → `complete:B:C` and `high:B:C` paid from the Individual row, `perfect:B:C` paid once from the P row, the 0-point event rows for each context, nothing paid from Q; `learning_path_completions` unique per (path, learner), and a failed retake after a pass neither removes nor blocks it; a lone path delete raises 23503 while the organisation and user cascades succeed; both refresh functions answer with every `error` null; the privilege matrix returns 42501 for `anon`; `v_journey_progress` is unreadable by `authenticated`; `v_course_attempt_summary` keeps one row per (course, learner); the parity checks.

    **The purge rehearsal (added on 2026-11-13; the proof that no official row is touched).** The seed gains: a course preview, a path test run and a journey test run of learner S with statements, State documents, launch tokens and learning sessions, some older than the retention and some newer; `legacy_role` rows (a completed one flagged by the 0100 backfill, one that is the target of a used grant, one with an `xp_events` row and a `journey_day_progress` row pointing at it); an `lrs_forward_outbox` row naming a new-kind preview attempt; and every official row kind of the seed above. Assert: (1) a dry run returns the counts and changes nothing (row counts of every table equal before and after; no log row). (2) The retention run deletes exactly the new-kind preview attempts older than `live_days`, their statements, State, tokens, sessions and outbox rows, and every test run whose own `created_at` is older than `live_days`, with all of that run's attempts, fresh ones included; the newer ones stay: an old run with no attempt goes, another tester's new run with no attempt stays, and a new run keeps its attempts. (3) A manual run by scope (one run; one tester; one course; the organisation) deletes exactly that scope. (4) After every run, for every table of the database, the count and a checksum over the primary keys of rows that are not new-kind preview data equal the values taken before the run; this is the case that proves no official row and no `legacy_role` row is touched. (5) When a points row, a day credit, a notice, a path completion, a used grant or a revision visit points at a selected preview attempt (seeded on purpose), that attempt is never deleted: with one such attempt and one clean aged attempt in one organisation, the clean one is deleted, the other stays with every row that points at it, the log row names it under `excluded` with the table, and the answer reports it (step 81). (6) One `preview_purge_log` row per run that deleted or excluded something, holding ids and counts only. (7) As `authenticated` and `anon`, calling the function raises 42501. (8) The organisation and user cascades still delete a tester's test runs. (9) A second paste of 0107 changes no `preview_kind`. Added by the independent review of round 4: (10) with the `preview_settings` row deleted, or its `live_days` null, the retention run raises and the refresh function's `preview_purge` entry carries the error, not a count of 0; after the first paste exactly one row exists with `live_days = 30`. (11) A run with reason `restore` and a null organisation deletes every new-kind preview row and run of every organisation, a manually deleted one that the seed "restored" included, and no `legacy_role` or official row. (12) A run with reason `product_delete` and a course, path or program scope deletes that product's preview data and its outbox rows and writes its log row; a course whose only attempts were previews can then be deleted and leaves no `preview_runs` row without a parent; added by the check of the fold: with one seeded breach on a course, on a path and on a program, the dry run with the product's scope answers `excluded` 1 for each, and the real run leaves the breaching attempt, its run and every row that points at it in place. (13) An outbox row is never written for a preview by the abandon path: not in SQL's reach, so it is a case of `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` and spec 75.

    **Re-pin and day cases added by the independent review of round 4** (`assert-0107.sql`): the dry-run counts for a draft, passed as the would-be version, equal the counts the real run returns after the publish, on the fixtures (a) to (h); a first paste, then a day removed through `journey_save_days`, then a second paste, passes (Q8 only counts, A19's equality is skipped); the new version is shorter, every remaining required day is credited and the learner holds an open attempt on the removed day: the enrolment completes at the re-pin, the open attempt then completes, and `journey_record_completion` writes a history credit on the completed enrolment with no second badge or claim; a day's pass rule made stricter by a publish does not fail an attempt launched under the earlier version (`journey_version_id`), and an attempt with a null `journey_version_id` is judged by the pinned version; `consume_grant` called with a day id that differs from the grant's answers `unavailable` and writes nothing, and its old form still resolves a number; every function of the file has a `search_path` entry in `pg_proc.proconfig`; the reverse script refuses while two credits of one enrolment share a day number.

    **Cases added in round 5 (2026-11-13).** Purge scope: each key of step 81 alone deletes exactly its scope (one attempt; one run; one tester; a tester with one course, path or program); an unknown key, an empty or null scope, a key with a null value, two product keys, `older_than_retention` with another key, a null organisation with reason `manual` or `product_delete`, and reason `product_delete` without exactly one product key each raise 22023 and delete nothing. By-role rows (decision A33, option (b)): with `purge_legacy` false no `legacy_role` row is selected by the retention run, the restore run or any manual scope; with the switch on or off a course delete's call removes that course's by-role rows that nothing protects, lists them in its log row, and reports a kept one so that the route refuses; with it true the retention run deletes an aged `legacy_role` row that nothing protects, with its statements, State, tokens, sessions and outbox rows, and keeps, each seeded on purpose, a row with a result made in Normal mode, a row with a result made in Browse mode (added by the check of the fold: a result keeps a row whatever its mode), the target of a used grant, a row with an `xp_events` row, one with a day credit, one named by a completion record, and one whose completion webhook was stamped; the answer counts them under `legacy_kept` by reason, the heartbeat stays green, and case (4)'s checksum now covers every row that is not new-kind preview data and not a deleted `legacy_role` row; a manual scope and reason `product_delete` on a path or program select no `legacy_role` row with the switch on; reason `restore` with the switch on removes the same aged, unprotected rows and no other `legacy_role` row. Settings: an update that makes `live_days + backup_days + 1` exceed 90 raises 23514; an update that sets `purge_legacy` to null raises 23502 (rewritten by the check of the fold: the case "the purge raises when `purge_legacy` is null" could not be staged, because the column is `not null default false`, step 79). The course delete's second reading (check of the fold): a by-role row of the course that gains a result between the dry run and the real call is reported under `legacy_kept` by the real call and is not deleted by it, which is what lets the route refuse (step 85). The test-run row: `done_activity_day_ids` is empty on every row after the first paste, a path run with a non-empty list is refused by the CHECK, and a journey run's list is gone with the run after its purge. Append-only: an update or delete of a `preview_purge_log` row raises for the service role. Indexes: the seventeen of step 7 exist (seven before the check of the fold). The delete guard: with a session claim that is not the service role, a delete of a `course_packages` row over a version that holds a learner attempt raises `learner_history_exists` through the cascade, and with the service-role claim it does not (the control, and the reason the route must not use that client; step 85). `consume_grant`: the eight-argument form and the nine-argument form, both give the same answers on one journey grant; the lock order itself cannot be shown on one connection, so it is asserted on the wrapper's stored body (its first locking statement names `attempt_requests`), and two parallel launches of one granted journey day are a case of spec 68 on staging. The reverse script runs to its end on a database that holds `preview_runs` and `preview_run_id` (part (4) drops the table last). The re-pin's relaxed-rule credits are equal between a dry run from a draft and the real run, with the draft's rule passed to `journey_attempt_satisfies`.

(Spec 55's two inserts at `:374-376` and `:400-402` are a learner's session and are meant to be refused; they are not fixtures and do not change.) Spec 55 is in the nightly's default list (`scripts/lifecycle-nightly.sh:43`) and lifecycle data lives 48 hours (`:38-40`), so as they stand Q8 would refuse the staging 0107 paste on any day, and from the paste on the derive trigger would raise on spec 55's launch: the nightly would turn red at 0107, not at 0108. After the paste, with the fixtures above: spec 46 names `context_kind`, `journey_day_id` and `day_id`; it need not name `journey_version_id`, which the scan requires only of `app/` and `lib/` (step 23). 65. **Acceptance criteria.**
    1. Learner R (an example learner) holds course C individually, in path P and on day 12 of journey E. He completes C from the Library: Individual status passed; P's step not started; day 12 not credited, with no `journey_day_progress` row.
    2. He completes C from P: a second attempt with `context_key = 'path:P'`; P's step done; the Individual result unchanged by id and value; day 12 still not credited.
    3. He completes C from day 12: a third attempt with `context_key = 'journey:E:<the id of day 12>'`; the day credited from that attempt; the other two results unchanged.
    4. Points: exactly one paid `complete:R:C`; 0-point event rows for `individual` and `path:P`; nothing for the journey.
    5. Each of these shows R once with its own product's status: course Reports, course learners and CSV, path Reports, path Learners and CSV, the path index, journey admin, the journey CSV, Organisation Reports, Learner Analytics, the Manager Report Card, the course page and the dashboard.
    6. The Overview shows 1 unique learner, 3 enrolments, 3 completions, 0 failed, 0 overdue, 0 not started; refreshing changes no product row; an analyst opens all three drill-downs.
    7. A `?lp=Q` launch for a path R is not enrolled in is refused; a plain launch of a course he holds only through P sends him into P.
    8. A path grant opens a retake that abandons only P's open attempt; the Individual result stands; the Overview shows P in progress while the retake is live. If that retake fails after an earlier pass, later steps stay unlocked, the path stays completed if it was, and reports and the CRM show the latest result with `passed_ever` true.
    9. An admin enrolled in E previews C: no day credited, no points, no attempt that counts. Her Start launch then opens a real record. An enrolled analyst's Start launch is a real attempt with a score.
    10. A failed required day 12 shows E as failed on the Overview and in journey admin; a journey grant and a pass credit the day.
    11. Emails: one course email when the Individual course is successfully completed, on SCORM and on cmi5, once per learner and course; one path email when the whole path completes; one journey email when the learner completes the whole journey (the one exception, decision A20: a journey completed by an admin's publish to all enrolled learners sends none); none for steps, days, revision visits, previews or granted retakes that complete again.
    12. CRM version 1 keeps today's fields and reads the Individual product strictly; version 2 shows three product objects; every record carries a context, its own or its parent's (step 55); the webhook carries the context and respects the scope; `path_id` returns only those paths' learners and records, in version 1 and in version 2.
    13. A future-dated Individual assignment hides the Library card while `?lp=P` still works; a future-dated step refuses `?lp=P` while the Library launch still works.
    14. A course update restarts open attempts in every context and re-issues grants in their own context; finished attempts are unchanged.
    15. A learner who presses Start on an "Everyone in this org" course, path or journey has a recorded enrolment before the first attempt and appears in that product's reports, the Overview and the CRM feed. A member who never starts is not counted.
    16. Editing a journey's deadline changes every enrolled learner's overdue status at once in journey admin and after the next refresh on the Overview.
    17. Feedback R gives for C in P does not change his Individual feedback for C; each product's report shows its own.
    18. Every before- and after-check of 0107, 0108 and 0109 is green on staging; the grants check is green; the nightly list is green two nights running.
    19. After a publish to all enrolled learners that removes a day, swaps a day's course, inserts a day before the learner's current day or reorders days (A27, in the owner's four sentences): no day the learner completed is required again; a day the learner started is resumed with its progress and its completion credits it, also when the publish removed it, swapped its course, moved it past the calendar ceiling or completed the enrolment over it, and a pass rule the publish made stricter does not fail it; a learner who has not completed a changed day gets the new version of it; every attempt and credit row that existed before the publish is unchanged after it. Every journey surface shows the same credited count and the same open day, and completing the remaining required days completes the enrolment once, with one badge, one claim and one email.
    20. A journey that an admin's publish completes shows as completed, with its date, on the learner's dashboard card, the journey page, the certificate, journey admin, Learner Analytics, the Report Card, the Overview and the CRM feeds, with day figures that agree, and sends no email (A20).
    21. A Preview or a test run by an admin, a super owner or an analyst writes nothing official anywhere: no points, badge, streak, day credit, path completion, enrolment, email, webhook, CRM row, report row or tenant LRS statement. Its scores and its path or journey progress show only in Preview Reports. An authorised user can delete it at any time, the nightly purge deletes it by age, and no official row changes by id or value when it is deleted (R7).
    22. A tester can walk a whole strict path and a whole calendar journey in a test run, meeting the same locks, pass rules and unlock dates a learner meets, without waiting for a date and without a Continue button in the launch bar (A21). Until phase G this covers course days, which are the only kind that exists; from phase G a run also passes an activity day by marking it done inside the run (decision A41), and the criterion then covers a journey with activity days.
    23. A language switch made in one product closes only that product's open attempt; the closed row, its data and its statements are still in the database; no completed result changes; an audit row names what was closed (A24).

### N. Security, scale and performance

66. **RLS and grants.** Every new relation has RLS on, a policy and literal grants in its file; service-only tables carry an explicit no-access policy, because `tests/rls-audit/audit.sql:247-248` fails any table with `organization_id`, RLS on and no policy, and that halts production deploys; a table with RLS off raises the cron audit's critical alert. Matviews stay revoked from `authenticated`. Journey progress is service role only. `anon` gets nothing. The context trigger binds every role.
67. **Forgery.** The context comes only from the launch link and is checked against recorded enrolment, activity, release and lock before anything is written; a failed read refuses. Without this, per-context official attempts could be minted by URL.
68. **Self-enrolment and the service role.** The enrolment write of step 24 and the route of step 48 run with the service role only after the session, active membership and visibility checks, and write only the caller's own row in the caller's organisation.
69. **Privacy.** New columns hold ids, day numbers, flags and scores. `completion_notices` holds ids and times, no address and no name. No name or email is added anywhere. Logs carry attempt ids only.
70. **Workers free plan (50 subrequests, 10 ms CPU, both environments).** Estimates, counted as ADR 0019 counts them (middleware 1, learner layout about 12, `requireOrgAccess` about 5); the launch page was not measured for this record.
    - Launch: the page has 47 `.from(` or `.rpc(` call sites today, of which one request runs a subset. This record did not count the worst branch. The independent check read today's call sites on a learner's first strict `?lp=` launch and came to about 31 page reads on top of the 18 fixed ones, about 49; that is an upper reading, because it does not remove branches that cannot run together, and it was not recounted here. Either way, nothing shows today's base is under 35, so a target of 40 cannot be met by adding reads to it. Planned work, not a contingency: `launch_context` (step 18) is built in phase 1 and wired in phase 2, and replaces the access read, the entitlement reads, the release read and the strict lock's reads (eight call sites today, `launch:597-725`) with one call. A self-enrolling learner adds the insert and one more call (2). Target: the worst path (a self-enrolling learner's first strict-path launch that consumes a grant) at or under 40 of 50. **A measured count on a local build, branch by branch as ADR 0019 does it, is a gate of phase 2** and is recorded under this record; if it is still over 40, the phase does not ship until further reads have moved into the function.
    - Course page chooser: one read of the learner's path and journey holdings of the course (2 at most), from rows the page mostly reads already. The course page's whole request is measured with the chooser in phase 6 and recorded the same way.
    - Publish with `apply_to=all`: one set-based call replaces today's loop of a count read, an update and an insert per enrolment (`app/api/journey/program/route.ts:152-171`), and the route sends no email (step 47), so its count no longer grows with the number of enrolments.
    - Completion routes: one writer call replaces two or three calls. One email costs 5 to 6 subrequests (`lib/notifications/send.ts:155-163`, `:203`, `:245`, `:270`, `:291`); a single attempt has one context, so at most one email per request. The statements route gains that cost on the completing request only. The commit route awaits its send inline today; that is unchanged and disclosed.
    - Overview: three reads per page view. `learner-summary`: the per-enrolment count becomes one batched read before per-day rows are added.
71. **Refresh cost and growth.** The 15-minute set and the nightly set are timed separately; a timed run on a seeded dataset of about 6 million attempts gates the 0109 paste. The repository has no script, seed or place for that run today (the rehearsal is PGlite on a small seed). It is built in phase 7 as `scripts/refresh-timing/` (a seed generator and a timer), and it runs on a throwaway Postgres on the executor's machine or a CI service container, never on the shared staging database, where it would slow every harness, and never on production. The hardware is recorded beside the figure, because local timing is not the hosted database's timing; the first refresh on staging after the 0109 paste is timed as well and recorded with it. Attempts grow by one row per product a learner actually launches. `product_metrics_daily` grows with organisations, days and instances; a journey counts once per program.

### O. Phases, effort and gates

72. The core has no feature flag: two decision rules on one table for the life of a flag would recreate the drift this record removes, and the schema change is one transaction. Safety comes from production being empty, the paste-first order on staging and the rehearsal. Flagged: the Overview (`PRODUCT_OVERVIEW`), the CRM version (per key), journey activities (`JOURNEY_ACTIVITIES`), Preview Reports and test runs (`PREVIEW_REPORTS`, step 84).

    **A departure from `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 6, which only the owner can allow (decision A19).** Principle 6 says code is deploy-safe in both orders and tolerates a missing column. This record keeps it for every reader, cron and feed: they tolerate the absent objects of 0107 and 0109 (the fallbacks of steps 31, 49 and 53, and the hold sweep's select, which falls back to today's select when `webhook_skip_reason` is missing). It does not keep it for these write paths from phase 2 on: (1) the launch insert, which names `context_kind`; (2) the two completion routes, which call `record_learning_activity` and read an error as "not official"; (3) the journey publish route with `apply_to=all`, which calls `journey_repin_enrollments` (steps 47, 94), and the curriculum save, which calls `journey_save_days` (step 92); (4) every other write from phase 2 on that names a 0107 column or calls a 0107 function, among them the self-enrolment insert of step 24 (`source`), the journey self-start (D15), the revision visit in its context (step 11), grant requests, approvals and `consume_grant` per context (phase 4) and the completion-notice stamp (step 15). On a database without 0107 a launch would fail, a completion would silently earn no points, credit, webhook or email, and a publish to all enrolled learners would re-pin and complete nobody. ADR 0014, the precedent, kept the principle with a narrower-select fallback. Recommended: allow the departure for these write paths, with this rule in its place: **on every environment 0107 is pasted before a commit that contains phase 2 serves a launch there.** On staging that is the order of step 73. On production it is the sitting of step 74; a tag cut from `main` between the phase 2 merge and that sitting is allowed only while the first-learner gate of step 74a is closed, because then nothing can be launched or completed there and no enrolment exists for a publish to complete, and the owner is told so before the tag. Two admin actions need no learner and fail on a database without 0107: saving a journey curriculum (`journey_save_days`) and pressing Preview (the insert names `preview_kind`). In that window nobody edits a journey curriculum or presses Preview on production; step 74a's condition (4) already holds back the first course, and the owner is told before the tag that these two actions fail until the sitting. The alternative keeps the principle: the launch insert retries without the context columns on 42703, the routes fall back to today's classify-then-call path when the function is missing (PGRST202), and on PGRST202 the publish route keeps today's count loop. It costs about 2 more days for (1) to (3), the publish route's fallback included; fallbacks for the writes under (4) would add more and are not priced here. It keeps alive, behind a schema test, the fail-open path this record removes. The owner allowed the departure on 2026-11-13 (A19: "**Allow** the required database migration (0107) to be applied before the new application code starts serving launches that depend on it"), with four preconditions "before the new code serves real learners": staging validation, a production backup, migration verification and safe deployment checks. They are gates in steps 73, 74 and 74a. The alternative's fallbacks are not built.

The plan said phases 1 to 4, about 25 days, this record's first draft said 32, and its first review 59 without a margin. This was the largest change of the review; the owner chose it on 2026-11-13 (R8, not A4). With round 4's scope and its review the release is 85 days by the table (plan for about 128), and after round 5 it is 87 (plan for about 131), as the totals above say.** The release and the first learner are bound by different things:
    - The release is bound by D3 together with a choice of this record. D3 puts 0109 in the same sitting as 0107. This record writes 0109 in phase 7, with the report code that reads it, and proves it on staging there. So the sitting cannot happen before phase 7 is proven, and the tag cut then carries phases 1 to 7. The owner's words fix the sitting; placing 0109 in phase 7 is the architect's plan, and it is what turns D3 into a later release.
    - The first learner is bound by D11: Yoddha reads version 2 before production's first learner. Version 2 is built in 9a, so 9a must be on production before the gate of step 74a opens. It is put in the same release.
    - The first learner is also bound by the owner's sentence of 2026-11-11, that "the Continue button, chapter lock/unlock logic, Learning Path progress, dashboards and reports must all follow the same path-specific completion rule". The dashboards are phase 6 and the reports are phase 7, so both must be on production before the first learner. This binds the first learner, not the release.

    Phases 1 to 4 are still built and proven first, on staging. Recorded enrolment on Start for courses and paths sits inside them (phases 1 and 2), because the views read only enrolment rows: without it an "Everyone" learner would be invisible or refused. The journey half of D15 sits in phase 6. The Overview (phase 8, behind its flag) and journey activities (phase G) do not block the release. The conversation with Yoddha opens at acceptance and runs alongside phases 1 to 4, because Yoddha's own move to version 2 is the part this team does not control.

    The alternative, if the owner prefers an earlier release: phases 1 to 4 and 9a in a first release (44.5 days by the table; plan for about 67), with 0109 pasted in a second sitting when phase 7 is ready. It departs from D3 as accepted. Until phases 6 and 7 follow, the product chooser is missing, and path reports and the leaderboards do not yet follow the path rule (step 59). It was not recommended, and the owner declined it on 2026-11-13: "I will go with your recommendation, **R8**, rather than the faster A4 option."

    Every pull request is independently reviewed and pushed under the owner's standing approval for non-production branches. The owner merges, deploys staging and pastes.

### P. Deploy and paste order

73. **Staging.** The owner runs the Q2 and Q8 counts and resets any orphaned journey rows. Before the paste, the fixtures pull request that gives specs 46 and 55 their `journey_days` rows is merged (step 64), and the lifecycle-namespace organisations made by earlier runs are deleted, or the nightly is paused for 48 hours so its cleanup removes them: a journey fixture built without `journey_days` rows would make Q8 refuse the paste. Pull request 1 (0107, 0108, the rehearsal, the pure modules, fallback-safe readers) merges and deploys to both staging deployments. The owner pastes 0107 in a window clear of everything else that uses the staging database: the pg_cron refresh at 03:30 UTC, the lifecycle nightly from 22:41 UTC (about 45 to 80 minutes), the nightly e2e at 04:00 UTC, any pull request's e2e job and any hand-run harness; the before- and after-checks are recorded under this record. (The round-3 text named the :03 and :07 offsets; those are production's crons, `.github/workflows/cron.yml`, and belong to step 74.) Staging has no database backup and needs none: it holds test data (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 5), so a failed paste is met by the re-paste rule of step 4 and the reverse script of step 61, not by a restore. Phases 2 to 4 follow. The owner pastes 0108 once the preconditions of step 20 hold (phase 2 on both staging deployments, the insert-sites guard green over the test folders, the nightly and the e2e job green on fixtures that name `context_kind`, and no firing in `context_derive_log` for 24 hours), and 0109 with phase 7. A re-paste of 0107 on staging stays possible until 0109 and changes no value the new code wrote (step 4).

    **Staging validation (the first of the owner's A19 preconditions; reading R19).** Before the production sitting: every phase the tag carries has passed its gate on staging; 0107, 0108 and 0109 are pasted there with every before- and after-check green and recorded; the rehearsal is green for every scenario; specs 68, 69, 70, 72 and 75, the cases of spec 73 that phases 6 and 7 build (its journey self-enrolment case, which is phase 6's gate, and its CSV and feedback cases, which phase 7 builds), and the changed specs have passed by hand and joined the nightly list; and the lifecycle nightly is green on the release candidate. Spec 71 needs the Overview page and stays phase 8's gate; the whole of spec 73 and the two green nights stay 9b's gate; neither phase is in the release (corrected by the independent review of round 4, which found that "specs 68 to 73" could not be met by the release's own phases). Specs are run "by hand on staging, never while another harness runs": that rule is this record's (step 64) and ADR 0014's and ADR 0019's, not `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 9, which states only the `qa-*` purge rule. The nightly job has a 75-minute timeout against about 40 to 50 minutes today (`.github/workflows/lifecycle-nightly.yml:60-61`); before the first new spec joins the list (in phase 2, where the change is priced) the timeout is raised or the list is split into two jobs that run one after the other, never side by side on one database, because a timeout is a red night and restarts the two-night count of step 74a.
74. **Production** (owner-run, explained beforehand, never the Supabase CLI). Production holds 0100, 0103 and 0104 on top of 0099 and neither 0105 nor 0106. D0 keeps 0105 out of any sitting before this one. An earlier sitting may paste 0106 and tag the code already on `main`; that code was planned to run before 0105.

    **Gates before the first paste (the owner's A19 preconditions, 2026-11-13).** The sitting does not start until each is recorded in the release runbook of phase 9a.
    - *Staging validation*: step 73's list, complete, on the release candidate. The tag is cut from that commit, or from one that differs from it only in documents.
    - *Production backup*: an on-demand run of the database backup workflow on `main`, green, started within the hour before the first paste, with its steps Dump database, Encrypt with GPG (required) and Upload to Backblaze B2 all successful and no artifact published. The scheduled nightly alone does not count: its 02:00 UTC schedule in fact starts between about 08:00 and 09:30 UTC. This is the practice of the v1.4.0 sitting (`docs/release/v1.4.0.md`). What the facts show today (run history read on 2026-11-12, ids and conclusions only): the production database backup is working; all 31 runs since 2026-10-18 10:55 UTC are green, the latest scheduled one on 2026-11-12; the five red runs of 2026-10-14 to 2026-10-18 came before the workflow was hardened. So the precondition can be met as practised and nothing needs repair first. "Verified" means what is proven: a green run, and the owner's restore drill (download, decrypt, completeness by counts), which last printed `RESULT OK` on 2026-10-18 and is repeated before this sitting. Its limit, stated: a restore of a dump into a database project has never been rehearsed; production is on the free tier with no point-in-time recovery and no vendor-side backups; the disaster path is a restore of the pre-sitting dump into a fresh project, which loses every write made after the dump. Production has no learners and no courses at the sitting, which is what makes that limit acceptable there.
    - *Restore rehearsal (added in round 5; the owner, 2026-11-13: "mandatory before the production sitting ... in an isolated environment, with the restore process and verification documented")*: the rehearsal of step 98 has passed, with the release candidate's migration files, and its record is in the release runbook. This closes the limit stated in the bullet above ("a restore of a dump into a database project has never been rehearsed") before the sitting, not after it. A rehearsal that failed, or one made before the candidate's files existed, does not open the gate.
    - *A freeze*: no push to any branch and no harness running on staging, so every parallel stream pauses for it. Workflows, as practised at the v1.4.0 sitting (rewritten by the independent review of round 4; "no workflow running" could not hold): production's scheduled crons keep firing, every 5 and every 15 minutes (`.github/workflows/cron.yml:22-31`), and the tag's own deploy is the one workflow the sitting starts; before each paste the owner checks that no other run is in progress.
    - *Paste minutes on production* (`docs/release/v1.4.0.md:92`): clear of 02:00, 03:00, 03:30 and 06:00 UTC, of the hour's :00, and of the quarter-hour offsets :03, :07, :18, :22, :33, :37, :48 and :52. Scheduled runs start late, so this lowers the chance of a lock wait and does not remove it: each file sets `lock_timeout = '5s'`, and on a timeout nothing changed, so the owner waits a minute and pastes the same file again.
    - *Counts*: Q8 to Q10 read 0 on production; a count above 0 goes to the owner before 0107 is pasted (step 4).

    **Migration verification (the third precondition).** Each file's before-checks are read before its paste and its after-checks after it, in the SQL editor, and each result is written into the runbook before the next file. A failed check stops the sitting at that file. Nothing is repaired by hand in the sitting.

    **Safe deployment checks (the fourth precondition).** Before the tag: the RLS audit and `check-migration-grants` are green on the tag's commit; no `cron.yml` line names a route this release adds (it adds none; principle 6); the notice addendum and Yoddha's agreement are in hand. After the tag: the production deploy workflow is green; the health check answers 200, and the only entries it reports as degraded are the stale-cron ones that were degraded before the sitting (production health is expected to read `degraded` while GitHub starves the short crons, `docs/release/v1.4.0.md:442`, `:498`; a new degraded entry stops the sitting's sign-off); the `refresh-report-views` heartbeat reads `ok` after its next 03:30 UTC run, with the refresh answer's `completion_sweep` and `preview_purge` entries present and their `error` null; a new CRM key reads version 2 and an old one version 1 (A17); launch-side checks are recorded as not performed while production has no courses. A code rollback is a re-deploy of the previous tag and needs no reverse migration, because readers tolerate the new columns (step 61). The first-learner gate of step 74a stays closed through all of this: the new code serves no real learner until that gate opens.

    Then, in one sitting, in the order the owner accepted under D3:
    1. 0105, if not yet pasted, with Q0 to Q8 before and A1 to A8 after (A8 recorded as not performed; ADR 0020 step 9).
    2. 0106, if not yet pasted, with Q0 to Q3 before and A1 and A3 to A5 after (ADR 0021 I.2).
    3. 0107, with Q1 to Q10 before and A1 to A24 after. Every count reads 0.
    4. The release tag. It carries phases 1 to 7, 9a and P (step 72 says why; P by reading R12), with the notice addendum and Yoddha's agreement in hand. The deploy must be green.
    5. 0108. Its `context_derive_log` check passes because nothing was launched.
    6. 0109, in the same sitting, with its before- and after-checks.

    The one order, stated the same way in the notes on ADR 0020 and ADR 0021: 0105; then 0106 if not yet pasted; then 0107. 0106 may instead have been pasted in an earlier sitting; it is never pasted before 0105 inside this one.

    0105 is pasted, not skipped: it carries the Custom Group helpers, the release gates and `v_path_enrollments_expanded`, which 0107 needs. 0107 follows it in the same sitting and removes its path arm, so the state "path learners in course reports" never serves a request on production.

    **74a. The first-learner gate.** Production has no learners and no courses today, so the release and the first learner are two separate events. After the sitting, production still gets no learner: no course, path or journey is assigned to anyone, and none is set to "Everyone in this org", until all of these are recorded as a dated note under this record: (1) the sitting of step 74 is complete and the deploy is green; (2) phase 9b's staging proof is done, with the nightly green two nights; (3) Yoddha reads contract version 2: their key is a version 2 key and their written confirmation is on record; (4) added on 2026-11-13 for R7, and made exact in round 5 by the owner's A34 answer: the owner has confirmed that the bucket whose setting was read is the database backup bucket; a lifecycle rule set by the owner bounds the life of every copy of a dump at R days, superseded versions included, and is read back (today's setting keeps every version, so the gate is closed); `preview_settings.live_days` and `backup_days` are set to L and R with L + R + 1 ≤ 90 and the values read back are recorded (an update that changed no row must not pass), in the release runbook with `purge_legacy`, where every later change of the row is added as a dated line, because a restore reads them from there (step 97; check of the round-5 fold); the retention of the log stores is read (step 83); under A33 option (b), `purge_legacy` is on, or its being off is recorded with the reason (step 82); the nightly refresh's `preview_purge` entry is green on production; (5) the content backup is green and its restore drill has passed from `main` (`docs/release/v1.4.0.md` already requires this before the first course; it has been green since 2026-11-11, and a drill passed on 2026-11-10 from a fix branch). Condition (4) also gates the first course on production, because a Preview of that course is the first production preview. A version 2 key is a key created in the settings page after the sitting; it reads 2 by the column default (step 8, decision A28), so Yoddha is issued a new key and nobody runs SQL on production for it. Their old key keeps reading version 1 until it is revoked. The owner opens the gate. If (3) cannot be met, R4 applies: option (c) goes back to the owner, and the gate stays closed until the owner decides. The gate is a procedure in the production runbook, not code. The runbook is written in phase 9a, because the sitting it describes comes before 9b; the dated note that records the five conditions and opens the gate is written in 9b.
75. **Standing never-paste rules, kept and extended.** Never paste 0098 after 0100. Never paste 0073, 0081, 0090, 0092, 0095 or 0099 after 0103. After 0107: the list of step 4. After 0109: the list of step 21. Never push a `v*` tag as a bookmark.

Principle 3 ("Learner history is never rewritten or deleted") is departed from for preview data only: rows written by a Preview launch or a test run are deleted on request and by age, as the owner asked under R7 (section S, decision A36); no other learner row is ever deleted or rewritten. Principle 5 ("No test or seed data on production") is read as not forbidding a Preview or a test run on production: they are the owner's R7 testing data, kept out of every official record by their context and deleted within the retention; since round 5 this is the owner's decision, not a reading ("I agree to allow Preview and test runs on production, as an exception to the no-test-data-on-production rule", 2026-11-13), with the owner's conditions: separate from official data and without any official effect, identifiable, open to authorised users only, deleted within the 90-day maximum; it is an exception for Preview and test runs only, and no test fixture, seed row or test-learner account is allowed on production by it. The principle 3 departure is likewise the owner's decision since that message (A36), for preview and test data only (added by the independent review of round 4; the round-4 text said no other principle was touched). Phase 9b writes all three into the file. No other principle is touched. Section 4 is not overridden.

### S. Preview data and Preview Reports (the owner's R7)

There is no section R, so that "R7" always names the owner's item. This section replaces the round-3 reading R7 (the open question O-1 of ADR 0006 note (r)): the owner's answer settles it. Preview results are kept, apart, for a limited time, and count nowhere.

78. **What a preview is, from phase 2.** A row is a preview only when its launch was started with Preview or from a test run; never by role (R6). `preview_kind` says which: `course` (Preview of one course), `path_run` or `journey_run` (a launch inside a test run, section T), or `legacy_role` (flagged by role before this record; step 82). Every preview row has `context_kind = 'preview'`, is excluded by the one official rule in SQL and TypeScript, and is skipped by the writer (step 12). **Launch mode (decision A32; answered by the owner on 2026-11-13: "I agree that Preview should run in normal course mode and store scores and results. However, all Preview data must remain completely isolated from official learner data, reports, analytics, progress, rewards and completion records." The recommendation below is therefore the rule, and the Browse alternative is not built).** The owner asks Preview Reports for "Preview scores and results" and for path and journey test progress. Today a preview launches in Browse mode (ADR 0006 step 17), and a KIVO 2.0 package in Browse sends only `initialized` and `terminated` (ADR 0006 note (r)): it stores no score and no completion, so its previews would show nothing and a strict test run could never unlock its second step. Recommended: a preview launches in `Normal` mode, so the module behaves exactly as it does for a learner and the result lands on the preview row; what keeps it from being official is the row's context, enforced by the database, not the module's behaviour. This changes ADR 0006 step 17 for previews and closes O-1 without a hold. Alternative: keep Browse; then Preview Reports show scores for SCORM and A.1 packages and none for KIVO 2.0, and a test run advances on "opened and closed", not on a result. What a KIVO 2.0 package sends in Normal mode on a preview row was not run for this record; it is the same launch a learner gets, and spec 75 proves it on staging.

    **How a preview launch behaves (the `canManage` short-circuit of `launch:318` is gone, step 23, so this is stated).** The one-attempt rule does not apply to previews: a preview has no official attempt, no revision visit, no grant and no window. A Preview launch resumes the open preview row of its own key and kind, if there is one, and otherwise inserts a new row; after a preview is completed, the next Preview of that course is a new row, never a revision row. `evaluate()` is not run for a preview launch, and `contextResult` is never asked whether a preview is "official". When a step or day is taken more than once in a test run, the run's state reads the latest completed preview row of that step or day in the run, and Preview Reports list the earlier ones under it. An open `legacy_role` row is never resumed by the new code, although it shares the plain key `preview`: the launch looks its row up by key and `preview_kind`, closes an open `legacy_role` row of that version it meets (status `abandoned`, conditional on in progress, as the launch's other closes do; a close is not a change of context; unlike those closes it sends no "abandoned" statement to a tenant LRS, step 80) and inserts the new row, so new preview data never lands on a row the purge cannot select.

79. **0107: the preview objects** (creation order: the head of section B).
    - `preview_runs` (new table, preview only; decision A31): `id uuid primary key`, `organization_id` (cascade), `user_id` (the tester; cascade), `kind text not null check (kind in ('path','journey'))`, `learning_path_id uuid`, `journey_program_id uuid`, `journey_version_id uuid` (each `on delete no action`), `simulated_date date` (the date the run's unlock rules read; null means today), `created_at timestamptz not null default now()`, and a CHECK that a path run names only the path and a journey run only the program and the version, and (added in round 5) that a path run's `done_activity_day_ids` is empty; `done_activity_day_ids uuid[] not null default '{}'` (added by the independent review of round 4, decision A41: the activity days a tester marked done inside a journey run; empty and unused until phase G; it is the run's own state, lives and is purged with the run row, and is the one piece of test-run progress that is not derived from an attempt, because an activity day has no attempt). It holds ids, a kind and dates: no result and, apart from that list, no progress. It is the one row a test run writes outside `course_attempts`; it is not an enrolment, and no official reader may read it (step 86). RLS on with an explicit no-access policy; `revoke all on public.preview_runs from public, anon, authenticated`; `grant select, insert, update on public.preview_runs to service_role`. No delete is granted: rows leave only through the purge function and the organisation and user cascades.
    - `course_attempts.preview_kind text check (preview_kind in ('legacy_role','course','path_run','journey_run'))`, with `(preview_kind is not null) = (context_kind = 'preview')`. Backfill (once, step 4): `legacy_role` on every row with `is_preview`. `course_attempts.preview_run_id uuid references preview_runs(id) on delete no action`. Both are in the shape CHECK, the key and the immutability trigger (steps 5 and 7).
    - `preview_settings`: one row (`id boolean primary key default true check (id)`, `live_days int not null default 30 check (live_days between 1 and 89)`, `updated_at`, `updated_by uuid`); RLS on, no-access policy, `grant select, update` to `service_role` only. It is the retention figure of step 83. **Changed in round 5 (finding 7 of its review, and decisions A33 and A34).** The row is one for the whole installation, because the backup bucket it must agree with is one for the whole installation. So it is not an organisation's setting: only a platform owner may change it (`platform_owners`, `is_platform_owner()`, `0021:14-39`; the route calls `requirePlatformOwner`, `lib/auth/require-platform-owner.ts:13`, and then writes with the service role, recording `updated_by`). An organisation's Preview Reports page shows the figure read only. As first written the row was edited from each organisation's page with no writer named, so one tenant's admin would have changed every tenant's retention. Two columns are added: `backup_days int check (backup_days between 1 and 88)`, the lifetime R of a backup copy that the owner's lifecycle rule guarantees (null until the owner has set the rule), with `check (backup_days is null or live_days + backup_days + 1 <= 90)`, so L cannot be raised past what the backups allow without R being changed knowingly in the same update; and `purge_legacy boolean not null default false`, the switch of the by-role arm (step 82). `backup_days` is a recorded figure, not a measurement: the bucket's rule is the fact, and gate (4) of step 74a reads both. No role is granted insert, so 0107 creates the row itself, in the run-once block that creates the table: `insert into public.preview_settings (id) values (true) on conflict (id) do nothing`. The purge raises when it reads no row or a null `live_days`; without that, a missing row would make the retention predicate null and the nightly purge would delete nothing and still report success (found by the independent review of round 4). After-check A23 asserts the one row.
    - `preview_purge_log` (append-only, in the `xp_events` trigger form; its trigger function is named in round 5: `preview_purge_log_append_only()`, plpgsql, `set search_path = pg_catalog, public`, modelled on `learning_path_completions_append_only()`, in the creation order's item 3, the reverse script's drop list and the `search_path` check of step 19): `id`, `organization_id` (cascade), `ran_at`, `reason text check (reason in ('manual','retention','restore','product_delete'))`, `actor_user_id uuid` (null for the scheduled run), `scope jsonb` (ids only), `counts jsonb` (one number per table), `attempt_ids uuid[]`, `run_ids uuid[]`, `excluded jsonb` (the attempts the call refused to delete, each with the table that points at it; step 81). Ids and counts only: no score, no name, no email. RLS on, no-access policy, `grant select` to `service_role`; inserted only by the purge function.
    - `purge_preview_data(p_org uuid, p_scope jsonb, p_reason text, p_actor uuid, p_dry_run boolean) returns jsonb`: plpgsql, `security definer`, `revoke execute ... from public, anon, authenticated`, `grant execute ... to service_role` (step 81).

80. **Isolation by construction: every store, how a preview is kept out of it, and how it is purged.** "Today" is the code at `main` 1aeb339, where nothing but two delete guards and one staging-only view reads the preview flag, so a completed preview can earn points and a streak, credit a journey day, send the SCORM course and path emails, fire the CRM webhook and be forwarded to a tenant LRS.

    | Store | Today | From this record | Purge |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `course_attempts` (with `cmi_data`, score, status) | An ordinary attempt with a flag | Its own context; excluded by the one rule; the only place a preview's result lives | Deleted by the purge function |
    | `xapi_statements`, `xapi_state`, `cmi5_launch_tokens`, `learning_sessions` | Written per attempt | Unchanged: they are the preview's own data, keyed by its attempt | Cascade from the attempt (`0004:11`, `:30`, `:46`; `0100:386`) |
    | `preview_runs` | Does not exist | Ids and dates of a test run | Deleted by the purge function `live_days` after the run's own `created_at`, with every attempt of the run; earlier when its last attempt goes in the same call, or by scope (step 81) |
    | `xp_events`, `user_gamification` (points, streak), badges, monthly recognitions | Written for a preview, even one that never completes (daily activity) | Never written: the writer skips previews (step 12) and the wrapper returns early (step 34) | Nothing to purge for new previews. The table is append-only for every role (`0053:129-146`); rows written for `legacy_role` previews cannot be deleted (step 82) |
    | `journey_day_progress`, `journey_enrollments`, `learning_path_completions`, `course_assignments`, `learning_path_assignments` | A staff preview can credit a day and complete an enrolment | Never written: `journey_record_completion` requires a journey context (step 13); a test run has no enrolment and writes no credit or completion; Preview never self-enrols (step 24 runs only without the mode) | Nothing to purge |
    | `completion_notices`, `notification_log`, sent emails | SCORM previews send the course email and can send the path email | No claim, no send, no log row (steps 12, 32) | Nothing to purge. Emails already sent are outside the system |
    | CRM webhook and the CRM feeds | The webhook fires; the feeds count the row | The routes take "official" from the writer, which skips previews; the feeds read non-preview contexts only | Nothing to purge. A message already sent is outside the system |
    | `attempt_requests`, `revision_visits` | An analyst's preview can consume a grant | A request in a preview context is refused (step 37); a preview records no visit and is never a revision row | Nothing to purge |
    | `lrs_forward_outbox` and the tenant LRS | Every statement and the sweeper's derived statements are forwarded, with no preview marker; the LMS cannot delete from a tenant LRS | Never forwarded (decision A35): `mirrorToExternalLrs` (`lib/lrs/enqueue.ts:69-102`) and every sweeper source that reads attempts (`lib/lrs/sweep.ts`) exclude `context_kind = 'preview'`, "Resend all history" included. So does the third path, `enqueueAbandonedStatements` (`lib/lrs/abandoned.ts:34-60`), which reads attempts by id with no context filter today and is called by every abandon site: the per-version restart (`.../restart/route.ts:233`), the course-update forced restart (`lib/attempts/force-restart.ts:731`), the launch's closes (`launch:789`, `:809`, `:942`, `:992`) and the language switch (`language-preference/route.ts:259`). Its select drops preview rows, `legacy_role` ones included, so a restart over an open preview and the close of an open `legacy_role` row (step 78) send no "abandoned" statement, which would carry the tester's identity to a store the LMS cannot delete from (added by the independent review of round 4) | The purge deletes outbox rows by `attempt_id` explicitly (the column has no foreign key, `0044:30`); expected 0 |
    | Report matviews, leaderboards, question analytics, `report_card_cache`, the Overview | Count the row | Built on the official rule or on enrolments; a preview is in neither | Nothing to purge |
    | Raw TypeScript attempt readers on official surfaces: Learner Analytics' tally and last activity, the Report Card's "last active" (added by the independent review of round 4) | Count every row that is not a revision visit, previews included (`analytics/page.tsx:1008-1022`; `lib/manager/insights.ts:507-515`) | They drop `context_kind = 'preview'` (step 49's row), and the source scan of step 62 fails on a new reader that does not | Nothing to purge |
    | Audit rows written through `auditLog` that name attempt ids (the per-version restart's row, the language switch's row of step 27) | The restart's row lists the attempts it closed, previews among them | Ids only, no result. The audit trail is not preview data (reading R18, as for the purge log); a preview's id in it outlives the preview | Not purged; stated |
    | Course file storage and its locked backup | Not written by a preview | Preview Reports export nothing into file storage; no preview data may be written to the `course-content` bucket, whose backup copy is locked for 90 days | None needed |
    | Logs (the hosting platform's request logs, browser error telemetry, the database platform's logs, CI logs) | Lines and URLs can carry attempt, user and course ids; no score, no name | Unchanged; ids only | Not deletable by the application; their retention is the owner's read (step 83) |
    | Database backups | Every nightly dump holds every row | Unchanged | Not purgeable row by row: a dump is one encrypted file. Copies expire by the bucket's lifecycle (step 83) |

81. **The purge: one SQL function, manual and automatic.** Deleting a learner-data row is new to this system, so the function is written to be unable to touch anything else.
    - *Selection.* Preview attempts of the three new kinds only: `context_kind = 'preview' and preview_kind in ('course','path_run','journey_run')`, in organisation `p_org` (null means every organisation, for the scheduled run and for reason `restore` only), narrowed by `p_scope`: `{"older_than_retention": true}` (attempts whose `started_at` is older than `preview_settings.live_days`, and every attempt of a test run whose own `created_at` is older than that; reading R11), or ids of one run, one tester, one course, one path or one journey program, or the whole organisation. The same predicate is repeated in the `delete` statement itself, so a wrong id list cannot widen it. A non-preview row can never be selected. A `legacy_role` row can never be selected by a manual scope or by a path or program delete; whether the retention run may select one is the owner's open A33 choice, designed in step 82 behind a switch that is off; a course delete's own call selects that course's by-role rows that nothing protects, switch or no switch, because the course's cascade removes them in any case and the call is what gives them a log row (step 82). It reads `live_days` first and raises when the settings row is missing or the value is null (step 79).
    - *The scope, made exact in round 5 (finding 3 of its review; the list above could not say "one preview" or "one tester's previews of one course", which the buttons of step 85 and reading R10 need).* `p_scope` is a JSON object that holds exactly one of these shapes, and the function raises 22023 and deletes nothing on anything else (an unknown key, an empty object, a null, a key with a null value, two product keys):
      - `{"older_than_retention": true}`, alone; reason `retention` only;
      - `{"attempt_id": <uuid>}`: one preview;
      - `{"run_id": <uuid>}`: one test run with its attempts;
      - `{"user_id": <uuid>}`: one tester's preview data;
      - one product key, `{"course_id": <uuid>}`, `{"learning_path_id": <uuid>}` or `{"journey_program_id": <uuid>}`: that product's preview data (for a path or program, its test runs and their attempts);
      - `{"organization": true}`: all of `p_org`'s preview data; with reason `restore` and a null `p_org`, every organisation's;
      - `user_id` together with one of `attempt_id`, `run_id` or one product key: the tester's own data inside that scope. This is how a tester who is not an admin deletes: the route always adds the caller's id, so an analyst's call can never name another person's data (reading R10).
      A null `p_org` raises unless the reason is `retention` or `restore`. Reason `product_delete` raises unless the scope is exactly one product key. Reason `manual` raises with `older_than_retention`. Every key is applied with `and`, inside the fixed predicate, so no scope can widen the selection beyond preview rows.
    - *A test run has its own clock (added by the independent review of round 4).* A `preview_runs` row is preview data too: it names the tester, the product and dates. The retention scope therefore also selects every run whose `created_at` is older than `live_days` and deletes it with all of its attempts, whatever their age, so a run lives at most `live_days` from its start and a tester who keeps launching in an old run cannot keep its header alive. An attempt is never older than its run, so nothing leaves early by this. A run with no attempt is deleted only when it is that old: another tester's run created minutes ago is never touched. A run whose last attempt this call deleted goes in the same call.
    - *Isolation check before deleting.* It finds rows that point at a selected attempt in `xp_events`, `journey_day_progress`, `completion_notices`, `learning_path_completions.steps`, `attempt_requests.used_attempt_id` and `revision_visits`. There must be none by step 80. An attempt that has one is never deleted: an official side effect on a preview is a defect to be looked at, not something to delete around. Changed by the independent review of round 4: the function no longer raises and deletes nothing for the whole organisation, because two of those tables are append-only, so one such row would have stopped that organisation's purge every night for good and kept all of its preview data past the limit. It sets the attempt (and its run) aside, deletes the rest, writes the excluded ids and the tables that point at them into the log row, and answers `excluded` above 0, which the caller treats as a failure (below). A dry run reports the same. An excluded attempt is an exception to the 90-day rule until it is cleared. This record does not approve that exception in advance and plans no way to delete around it: each case is reported to the owner the next morning with the ids, the defect that wrote the official row is fixed first, and what happens to the row is the owner's decision.
    - *Dry run.* With `p_dry_run = true` it returns the counts per table and writes nothing, no log row either.
    - *Delete.* Outbox rows by `attempt_id`; the attempts (statements, State, tokens and sessions go by cascade; the function owns the tables, so the missing delete grants of `learning_sessions` and the absent trigger on `course_attempts` are not in its way); then the runs selected by age, the runs whose last attempt this call deleted, and the runs named by the scope, never any other run. It asserts that the number of attempts deleted equals the number selected less the excluded ones, and raises otherwise, which rolls the whole call back.
    - *Audit.* One `preview_purge_log` row per organisation and call that deleted or excluded anything: who (or the schedule), why, the scope, the counts, the ids. Ids and counts only.
    - *Manual, at any time.* `POST app/api/preview/purge/route.ts` (phase P): the caller's session and organisation; the role check of reading R10; a first call with the dry run returns the counts, which the page shows in a confirmation; the second call deletes. It calls the function with the service role and passes the caller as `p_actor`. No TypeScript code deletes an attempt row: rule (iii) of `tests/unit/revision-listing.test.ts` (no `.delete()` on `course_attempts` and three other tables in app code) stays as it is.
    - *Automatic.* `refresh_report_views()`, which already runs nightly at 03:30 UTC on both environments (production by `cron.yml`, staging by pg_cron), calls the function with the retention scope, once per organisation that has anything to purge, each in its own exception block as the completion sweep does, so an error in one organisation stops that organisation's purge and no other; it reports a `preview_purge` entry in its answer: counts, the number of organisations that raised, the number of attempts excluded, and an `error` that is non-null when either number is above 0. **Making a failed purge visible (rewritten by the independent review of round 4; the round-4 text relied on a route behaviour that does not exist and cited the wrong step).** Today `app/api/cron/refresh-report-views/route.ts:39-55` answers `ok: true` whenever the function returns, the heartbeat is recorded with that (`:61-64`), the health route reads only `last_status` (`app/api/ops/health/route.ts:126`), and the public cron log prints only top-level numbers and booleans and fails only on a status other than 200 (`.github/workflows/cron.yml:80-89`): a purge that raised inside the function would pass unseen. So the route changes, in phase 1's pull request and priced there: when the `preview_purge` or the `completion_sweep` entry carries an error, it sets `ok` false, answers 500 and records an errored heartbeat, and it adds a top-level number `preview_purge_failed` (organisations that raised plus attempts excluded), which the cron log prints. Other entries keep today's behaviour, so no existing condition turns the cron red. A unit test covers the route's mapping. Preview data that could not be purged is then seen the next morning on production, well inside the limit. Staging runs the function by pg_cron with no route and no heartbeat: there a failure is read from the function's answer by spec 75 and from `preview_purge_log`, which is enough for test data. No new `/api/cron/*` route or schedule line is added, so nothing can fire on production before its route is deployed. The call exists from the 0107 paste; until phase 2 deploys there is no row of a new kind for it to select.
    - *After a restore.* A database restored from a dump brings purged preview rows back. `docs/ops/backup-restore.md` gains the step: run the purge with reason `restore` on the restored database before it serves. Its scope, stated by the independent review of round 4: every organisation (a null `p_org` is allowed for this reason) and every preview row and test run of the new kinds, not only the aged ones. With the retention scope alone, a preview that was deleted by hand after the dump would come back and stay until its age limit, against "delete ... at any time"; the dump cannot say which rows were deleted later, so all go. Testers lose recent previews in a restore, which is stated in the runbook step; no official row is selected, as in every run. The owner's requirement of 2026-11-13 is met ("restoring a backup does not permanently reintroduce Preview records that have already crossed their retention period. The expiry cleanup should be applied after a restore"), and the design goes further than those words: it removes every new-kind preview, those still inside the limit included, because the dump cannot show which were deleted by hand after it. The wider scope is this record's reading, not the owner's sentence (reading R23; corrected by the check of the fold, which found the text calling it "exactly" what the owner asked). The alternative, the retention scope alone, is the owner's literal words and lets a hand-deleted preview return until its age limit. It is step 97, with its runbook line and its place in the restore rehearsal. Under A33 option (b) with the switch on, the same call also removes the aged by-role rows that nothing protects, which the dump brought back, and no other by-role row (step 82).
    - *Rehearsal.* Step 63's purge rehearsal is the proof that no non-preview row is touched; spec 75 repeats it on staging.

82. **Rows flagged as previews by role before this record (`legacy_role`; decision A33).** **The owner answered A33 on 2026-11-13 in two sentences that this record cannot both apply to every such row: "Please preserve the old staff records that have been classified as Preview based on the user's role." and "They must be automatically deleted under the new 90-day cleanup rule." One clarification is asked, with three options; the design for the recommended one, (b), follows this paragraph's text and is marked.** The text from here to the mark is round 4's, kept: under it they are not swept, automatically or by the manual delete. The flag on them does not prove a test: an analyst's first launch used the official slot and could consume a grant (Context row 9), and the 0100 backfill flagged every earlier attempt of anyone who held a staff role when it was pasted, real learner work before a promotion included (`0100:456-467`). No role-change history exists to tell them apart. So: 0107 labels them `preview` and `legacy_role` (they leave every official count, as round 3 already had it), Q10 counts them, and they are kept. On production every count is expected to read 0, because production has no courses; a count above 0 goes to the owner before the paste. On staging they are test data, and the owner may reset them under the standing direction of 2026-10-18. They are shown in Preview Reports as a count only. Three things about them cannot be undone by any purge and are stated as exceptions to "nowhere over 90 days" for rows that predate the rule: points rows they earned (`xp_events` is append-only for every role), statements already forwarded to a tenant LRS, and emails and webhook messages already sent. A course whose only attempts are `legacy_role` rows can still be deleted with them, as today (`0100:546-575`). Such a row can also still hold a used grant; the grant stays "used". Two effects of these rows reach the same people now that they learn for real under R6, and both are kept as they are and stated: (1) a journey day credit that a by-role preview earned is labelled `legacy_cross_context` (step 8) and still counts as credited, so that person is not asked for the day again; (2) points a by-role preview earned stay, and their per-course key means the person's later real completion of that course pays nothing more (it still gets its 0-point event row and its result). Both were that person's real standing under the rule of the time, when the launch itself treated those rows as their attempts.
