Two more effects, added by the independent review of round 4, reach real learner work and are stated so the owner decides A33 with them: (3) a person whose earlier work the 0100 backfill flagged holds, after 0107, no official attempt in the Individual or path context of that course; the earlier result is kept but hidden from every official surface, and their next Start is a first official attempt, with no grant; (4) an open by-role row is never resumed by Start, whose key is `individual`, a path or a day and never `preview`, and it is closed by that person's next Preview of the version (step 78), so work in progress on it is not continued. Q10 counts all four. If the owner wants them undone instead, the ledger's own means is a compensating `adjustment` row and a label on the credit; this record does not plan it, and on production the counts are expected to be 0.

    **The clarification asked of the owner, and the design that is ready (round 5, 2026-11-13).** Why the two sentences cannot both hold for every row: some of these rows are tests, and some are real learner work, the rows of anyone who held a staff role when 0100 was pasted (`0100:456-467` flagged every earlier attempt of theirs), among them rows that hold a result, a used grant, points or a day credit. The owner's A36 answer of the same message says "Official learner records and completion history must remain protected." The options:
    - **(a) Keep every by-role row for good.** No automatic deletion; the first A33 sentence and A36 are met for every row, the second A33 sentence for none. Rows that are plainly old tests stay as a stated exception to the 90-day rule.
    - **(b) Recommended: delete automatically, at the retention limit, only the by-role rows that nothing official rests on; keep the others and list them for a decision.** Both A33 sentences are met as far as A36 allows: tests go under the 90-day rule; real work is preserved.
    - **(c) Delete every by-role row at the limit, real results included.** It meets the second A33 sentence in full and breaks A36's protection and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` principle 3 for rows that are learner history. Not recommended, and not designed here: if the owner chooses it, the kept test below is removed, and the points rows, which no role can delete, stay behind without their attempts.

    *Design of (b).* The purge gains one arm, used by the retention run, by the run after a restore and, for one course's unprotected rows, by the course delete (the last bullet but two); by nothing else.
    - *The switch.* `preview_settings.purge_legacy` (step 79), false after the paste. Nearly every by-role row is already older than any L on the day 0107 is pasted (the last ones are written until phase 2 deploys, so a few are younger), so without a switch the first nightly run after the paste would delete most of them before anyone had seen a count. The platform owner turns it on per environment, after the owner has seen Q10 and the arm's dry-run counts for that environment. With it off, the retention run and the run after a restore select no by-role row; the course delete's call is the one exception and does not read the switch (below). Turning it on is recorded (`updated_by`, `updated_at`) and, for production, under gate (4) of step 74a.
    - *Scope.* `context_kind = 'preview' and preview_kind = 'legacy_role'` and `started_at` older than `live_days`, in the retention run of each organisation and in the `restore` run. Never a manual scope: no button deletes a by-role row. Never a path or program delete. The predicate is repeated in the `delete` statement, as for the new kinds.
    - *The kept test (this is what A36 protects).* A by-role row is kept, never deleted by this arm, when any of these holds: (1) it carries a result, whatever its launch mode: the row is completed, passed or failed, or has a score. **Changed by the check of the round-5 fold.** As first written, part (1) asked for `launch_mode = 'Normal'` as well, on the argument that from 0100 on a by-role launch ran in Browse and so was no ordinary attempt. That argument was wrong. With ALDS_P4 on, every launch by an admin, a super owner or an analyst is written as Browse (`lib/alds/launch.ts:58-60`), the one by an analyst who was assigned the course and used the official slot included (Context row 9); and a SCORM or A.1 package stores a score and a completion in Browse, because the launch sets a review mode only for revision rows (`launch:1298-1305`; step 78). The mode therefore does not tell a test from a real result, and such a row is protected by parts (2) to (6) only when the organisation's settings happened to write a points row or send a webhook. A completed analyst attempt with a score would have been deleted once aged, against the owner's A36 sentence. So a result keeps the row. Every attempt older than 0100 reads `Normal` (the column's default, `0100:419`); (2) an `attempt_requests` row names it, as the used attempt or otherwise; (3) an `xp_events` row points at it; (4) a `journey_day_progress` row credits a day from it; (5) a completion record names it: `completion_notices`, `learning_path_completions.steps`, a `revision_visits` row; (6) its `completion_webhook_at` is set, so a completion was reported to the CRM from it. The test errs on the side of keeping: a row with any of (2) to (6) stays, and so does a row with a result that nothing else names, which is step 82's effect (3), a person's hidden earlier result. Its price, stated: a staff member's test of a SCORM or A.1 course that ran to a result between 0100 and phase 2 is kept and listed too, past the 90-day limit, because nothing on the row tells it from an analyst's real attempt.
    - *What is deleted.* An aged by-role row for which no part of the test holds: a launch in either mode that never produced a result (opened, perhaps partly answered, never completed, passed, failed or scored). By ADR 0006 note (r), a KIVO 2.0 package in Browse sends only `initialized` and `terminated` (step 78), so a by-role launch of one is of this kind; that was not run for this record. Its statements, State, tokens and sessions go by cascade, its outbox rows by id.
    - *Kept is not a failure.* A kept row is not an "excluded" attempt of the isolation check: it does not turn the nightly heartbeat red. The answer carries `legacy_deleted`, `legacy_kept`, the second split by the first part of the test that held, and `legacy_not_aged` (by-role rows younger than L, which wait for their age). The log row lists the deleted ids with the others; kept ids are not logged each night.
    - *Dry run.* The same counts with nothing written, for any organisation or all; it works with the switch off, which is how the counts are shown before the switch is turned on. After-check A22 is unchanged: on the first paste the new kinds count 0; it now also prints the three by-role counts (would be deleted, kept, not yet aged), which must add up to Q10's total.
    - *Preview Reports.* The housekeeping part (step 84) lists the kept rows under "Kept earlier staff attempts": per row the course, the person, the date and which part of the test keeps it; no score beyond what Preview Reports show for any preview. There is no delete button on that list. What happens to a kept row is the owner's decision, row by row or by count; this record builds no way to delete one, and on production the list is expected to be empty.
    - *The exception that stays.* A kept row lives past the 90-day limit. That rests on the owner's A36 sentence and is stated in the list's heading. The three things no purge can undo, named above, are unchanged.
    - *Product deletes (finding 4 of the round 5 review).* As round 4 wrote it, a course whose only attempts are by-role rows could be deleted with them and no log row, a kept row included, while the language delete refuses on the same rows. Under (b) the course delete refuses, with 409 "This course holds earlier staff attempts that are kept. Archive it instead", while the purge's dry run for the course, or the real call after it (step 85, check of the fold), reports any kept by-role row; the by-role rows of that course that nothing protects are deleted by the `product_delete` call for a course scope, so they are in its log row, whatever the switch says, because the admin is deleting the course and today's cascade would remove them unseen. Under (a) the course delete refuses on any by-role row. The language delete keeps refusing on any by-role row (step 85). 0100's guard is not changed: it still lets previews through, so these refusals are the routes' (step 85's source scan covers them).
    - *After a restore.* A dump brings deleted by-role rows back; the `restore` run removes the aged, unprotected ones again when the switch is on in the restored database. The settings row comes back as the dump held it, which may predate the switch or a change of L, so the runbook sets the row to the values last recorded outside the database before the purge runs (step 97; added by the check of the fold).
    - *What waits.* Phase 1 waits for the owner's choice: the arm, the switch column and the kept test are 0107's SQL, 0107 cannot be re-pasted on staging once 0109 is there, and under (a) the arm and the column are left out, under (c) the test is.

83. **"No Preview data should be retained anywhere in the system for more than 90 days": the arithmetic and the owner's choice (decision A34).**
    - *The owner's answer and reading (2026-11-13, second message), and what they mean.* The owner agreed with option (a) below, "subject to verification", gave L = 60 as an example ("Live Preview data can be deleted earlier, for example after 60 days, while Preview data in B2 backups must not be retained for more than 90 days") and read the bucket's lifecycle setting: "The currently selected option is "Keep all versions of the file (default)." As per the setting shown, all file versions are retained, with no automatic version deletion configured through this option." Taken as the owner states it, no version of any backup object is ever deleted. So R has no bound today and **the 90-day ceiling is not met for backup copies today**, whatever L is. It breaks nothing yet: production has no course, so no production dump holds a preview row. What closes it is a lifecycle rule that the owner sets on the database backup bucket, before production's first course (gate (4) of step 74a). R is counted from a dump's upload to the day no copy of it can be fetched or restored any more: the dated `daily/` object, and every superseded version of the `latest/` object, which the workflow overwrites each night (`backup.yml:185-194`), so under "keep all versions" each night's overwrite leaves the earlier night's file behind as an old version. The rule must cover both prefixes and old versions; how the vendor's rule fields express that is read from the vendor's own page when the rule is set and is not stated here from memory. The rule is then checked on the bucket, not assumed: after R days the oldest dated object and the oldest version of `latest/` are listed and must be gone.
    - *Three things are still the owner's (A34 stays open for these only).* (1) Confirm which bucket was read. There are two (ADR 0003): the database backup bucket, which holds the dumps, and the content bucket, whose 90-day object lock holds course files and no learner rows. The reading must be of the first; if it was of the second, the first is read too. (2) Choose L. (3) Choose R. **L + R + 1 ≤ 90.** The owner's example, L = 60, gives R ≤ 29: recommended then R = 28, four weeks of backups (60 + 28 + 1 = 89). L = 30 gives R ≤ 59: recommended then R = 56, eight weeks of backups (30 + 56 + 1 = 87, three days of margin for a late nightly). **Recommended: L = 30 and R = 56.** The trade is between two different things. L is how long a tester can still see a preview or continue a test run. R is how far back the whole database can be restored, official learner records included, because the rule deletes whole dumps and cannot spare the official rows in them. A test run rarely needs more than a month; a backup history of four weeks means that damage noticed in week five cannot be undone from a dump. If the owner prefers L = 60, it is built the same way with R = 28, and the shorter backup history is the stated price. Either pair goes into `preview_settings` (`live_days`, `backup_days`), whose CHECK refuses a pair that breaks the inequality (step 79). 0107's default stays `live_days = 30`.
    - *Live rows.* The purge removes them `live_days` after the row started. Call that L.
    - *Database backups.* Every nightly dump is a full copy, previews included, encrypted as one file, so one row cannot be removed from it; deleting a dump removes every learner's backup of that night. A preview row deleted on day L is still in the dumps taken up to that night. Let R be the days a dump lives in the backup bucket from upload until it is truly gone. The owner's sentence holds when **L + R + 1 ≤ 90**. The repository states no R: the runbook says dumps are "kept until you delete (or until a B2 Lifecycle Rule expires it)", and 30 days is only its suggestion. The 90-day object lock is on the content bucket, which holds course files and no learner rows; the dump bucket has no lock on record. So today R has no known bound, and no L meets the sentence until the owner reads the bucket and a lifecycle rule exists. The read must return: the rule for `daily/`; the rule for `latest/`; whether superseded versions of the nightly-overwritten `latest/` object are kept and for how long; that Object Lock is off on that bucket; whether any older unencrypted dumps remain.
    - *Logs.* Lines that can carry a preview's attempt id live as long as each store keeps them: the hosting platform's request logs, browser error telemetry (production falls back to the staging project when it has no address of its own, `deploy-prod.yml:41`), the database platform's logs. None of these retentions is in the repository; each is the owner's read, and each must satisfy L + its retention ≤ 90, or be recorded as an exception for ids without content.
    - *If production moves to a paid database plan with point-in-time recovery*, the vendor's backup window joins R.
    - *Options.* (a) Recommended: shorten live retention so the last backup copy is gone by day 90. Set a lifecycle rule on the dump bucket and set `live_days` so that L + R + 1 ≤ 90. The owner chose this option on 2026-11-13, subject to verification. Round 4's example here (R ≤ 45, an assumption) is replaced by the two pairs above: L = 30 with R = 56 (recommended), or the owner's example L = 60 with R = 28. The rule is the inequality; the figures are the owner's to fix. (b) Keep previews live for the full 90 days and record backups as an exception: copies inside encrypted dumps then live up to 90 + R days. (c) Exclude preview data from the dump: not possible row by row with the present full dump, and a second, filtered dump would no longer be a faithful backup; not recommended.
    - *Manual delete "at any time"* removes the live rows and outbox rows at once. It cannot reach a dump; those copies expire on the same rule, so they too are gone within 90 days of the row's start under option (a).
    - *Outside the system*, and not reachable by any purge: a tenant's LRS (hence decision A35, never forward), mail already delivered, a CRM message already sent. New previews send none of these.
    - *Gate.* The read, the lifecycle rule and `live_days` are condition (4) of step 74a and hold back production's first course. Production has no courses, so no production dump holds a preview row today, and the rule can be in place before one exists.

84. **Preview Reports** (`app/[org]/(admin)/preview-reports/`, flag `PREVIEW_REPORTS`; phase P). A separate section of the admin area with its own nav item, "Preview Reports", with the line "Not official records" under its title (the round-4 label "(test data)" is dropped: on production it read as a breach of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` principle 5; reading R21). Nothing in it links into an official report and no official report links into it.
    - *Courses.* Per course and tester: each Preview's start, last activity, status, score, pass or fail under the course's rule, time spent, the count of statements and interactions stored, and a drill-down to the question-level answers of that preview attempt, so a tester can see that "data is being captured as expected".
    - *Path test runs.* Per run: the path, the tester, the steps in order with locked, open, done or failed, score, `passed_ever` and the latest result, the run's simulated date, and whether the run reached the end; the same columns the path report shows a learner, computed by `pathProgress()` over the run's preview rows.
    - *Journey test runs.* Per run: the program and pinned version, the simulated date, each day with locked, open, credited-in-this-run, failed or rest, score and late, and whether the run finished; computed by `computeJourneyState()` over the run's rows.
    - *What an official completion would have done.* For each completed item, labels only: the email template that a learner's completion would send, whether a webhook would be sent under the organisation's scope, and that points would be considered. Nothing is sent or written.
    - *Housekeeping.* The retention figure, read only (it is the platform owner's setting, step 79), and each row's delete-by date; the purge log (counts); the count of `legacy_role` rows and, under A33 option (b), the list "Kept earlier staff attempts" (step 82); the delete buttons of step 85. Every page of the section carries the "Preview" mark on each row, which with the separate section is how preview data is "identifiable", and the permission check of reading R9 is how it is "accessible only to authorised users" (the owner's R21 conditions).
    - *Reads.* The `data_analyst` model: every read uses the service role after the permission check of reading R9, and names `organization_id = org.id`, because RLS does not back it up. The reads are plain selects of `course_attempts` with `context_kind = 'preview'` and of `preview_runs`, through `lib/preview/runs.ts`. There is no SQL view and no matview for previews, so nothing preview-shaped can be joined into an official object by mistake.
    - *Scoring.* `contextResult` accepts a preview key and returns that key's rows only; it is called with one only from `lib/preview/` (step 86). The official kinds are unchanged.

85. **Manual delete and product deletes.** Buttons on Preview Reports: delete one preview; one test run; all my preview data; all preview data of one course, path or journey; all preview data of this organisation. Each shows the dry-run counts first. The delete routes of step 40 call the purge with reason `product_delete` for the path or program before their own delete, so test runs never block removing a path or program that has no learner attempts; without it, `preview_runs`' `no action` reference would answer the misleading 409 "has learner attempts".

    **Product deletes, made exact by the independent review of round 4 (built in phase 4, half a day; its spec 75 cases in phase P).** Three routes: the path delete, the journey program delete and the course delete (`app/api/courses/[courseId]/route.ts:167`). The course delete is added because 0100's guard lets a course whose only attempts are previews be deleted (`0100:546-575`), and that cascade would remove new-kind preview attempts with no log row and leave their test runs' steps gone without a trace. Each route, in this order: (1) counts the attempts that would block the delete and that the purge cannot remove: for a course, its attempts that are not previews; for a path or a journey program, every attempt that carries the product's tag (`learning_path_id`, or an enrolment of the program), which includes a `legacy_role` row with an old tag, because its `no action` key refuses the delete (step 6) and the purge never selects it. Above 0 the route answers 409 and deletes nothing, preview data included; (2) calls `purge_preview_data` with reason `product_delete` and the product's scope, which writes the log row and removes the outbox rows, which have no foreign key (`0044:30`); (3) deletes the product. If a learner attempt appears between (1) and (3), the delete is refused and the route answers 409: for a path or a program by the attempt's `no action` key (23503); for a course by 0100's guard trigger, which raises `learner_history_exists` (below). The preview data is then already gone, with its log row. That is accepted: preview data may be deleted at any time.

    **Three rules added by the check of the fold (2026-11-13).** *A preview set aside is never deleted by a product delete.* Step 81 keeps a preview attempt that an official row points at, for the owner to decide. So in all three routes, between (1) and (2), the route calls the purge as a dry run with the same scope, and when it answers `excluded` above 0 the route answers 409 "This item holds preview data that is kept for review and cannot be deleted yet" and deletes nothing, preview data included. The real call in (2) is read the same way: `excluded` above 0 there (a breach that appeared in between) answers the same 409 and step (3) is not run; the clean previews are then gone, with their log row. Without this, a course delete would cascade the set-aside attempt away with no log row and no decision, and a path or program delete would stop on the kept run's `no action` key with the wrong message. *The course delete stays a session delete.* 0100's guard returns at once for the service role (`0100:558-560`) and otherwise raises `learner_history_exists` when the course holds an attempt that is not a preview (`:561-573`); the route deletes with the caller's session client (`app/api/courses/[courseId]/route.ts:222-225`) and maps that error to 409 (`:227`; `lib/courses/learner-history.ts:64-66`). Step (3) keeps exactly that client. Only the count and the two purge calls use the service role, and they sit inside the route's admin branch (`:200-218`): a caller who is not an admin reaches no purge and, as today, an RLS delete that removes nothing. A course delete made with the service client would skip the guard and cascade real learner attempts away, which cannot be undone; `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` (step 62) gains the case that the course route's `courses` delete is never called on a service client and that its purge calls are inside the admin branch. In the path and program routes the purge calls come after the admin check that step 40 adds. *One window stays, and is named.* A Preview launched between (2) and (3): on a course it is cascaded with no log row (the guard does not count previews); on a path or a program its new run row refuses the delete with 23503; step 40's 409 would then say "has learner attempts", so on a 23503 the route re-counts as in (1) and, when no blocking attempt exists, answers 409 "Preview data was added while deleting. Try again", and the admin presses delete again. Both need a tester to start a preview in the same moment an admin deletes the product; neither touches an official row.

    **Three more rules, from the review of round 5 (2026-11-13).** *A purge call that fails stops the delete (finding 2).* If the dry run or the real purge call returns an error of any kind, a raise, a timeout or a network failure, the route answers 503 "Preview data could not be checked. Nothing was deleted. Try again", and step (3) is not run. Only a clean answer with `excluded` 0 lets the product delete go on. Without this rule a route that read an error as "nothing to purge" would cascade a course's previews away with no log row, a set-aside one included. `tests/unit/product-delete-order.test.ts` (new) covers, for each of the three routes, an error from each call, `excluded` above 0 from each call, and the order count, dry run, purge, delete. *The course delete and by-role rows (finding 4).* Step (1) for a course also refuses while the course holds a kept by-role row under A33 option (b), or any by-role row under option (a); the rule and its message are in step 82. The count comes from the purge's dry run for the course scope (`legacy_kept`), so the kept test exists in one place, the SQL function. **Read twice (check of the fold, 2026-11-13).** A kept row is not an excluded one, and 0100's guard does not count previews (`0100:561-566`), so `excluded` 0 alone did not cover a by-role row that becomes kept between the dry run and the real call, for example by a late commit on an open row: the purge would skip it and the course's cascade would then remove it with no log row. The course route therefore reads `legacy_kept` from the real call's answer as well and, when it is above 0, answers the same "kept" 409 and does not run step (3); the unprotected rows are then already gone, with their log row. For a course, "a clean answer" means `excluded` 0 and `legacy_kept` 0, from both calls. `product-delete-order.test.ts` gains the case. *One re-count, never a loop (finding 5).* On a 23503 from step (3) of a path or program delete the route re-counts once and answers, with no second delete in the same request: 409 "has learner attempts" when a blocking attempt exists; else 409 "This item has learner records (requests or completions). Archive it instead" when an `attempt_requests` or `learning_path_completions` row names it, since both hold `no action` keys to it and the attempt count cannot see them; else 409 "Preview data was added while deleting. Try again".

    **The language delete (a gap found by the check of the fold; not a product delete; corrected in round 5).** What the check of the fold wrote about this route's delete was wrong for the code at `main` 1aeb339, and the review of round 5 found it (finding 1): the route counted attempts with the service client (`:288-291`) and then deleted the `course_packages` row with the service client (`:326`). That delete cascades to the language's versions and from them to their attempts, and 0100's version guard returns at once for the service role (`0100:525-527`), so a learner attempt inserted between the count and the delete was deleted with the language. This was a defect in live code, not in this record's plan, and it is fixed there: pull request 245, merged to `main` on 2026-11-13 after the commit this record cites, makes the route delete `course_packages` on the caller's session, so the version guard runs inside the cascade and refuses with `learner_history_exists` when any version holds an attempt that is not a preview (`0100:528-539`). That pull request was not opened for this record (the checkout is at 1aeb339); the executor re-anchors the lines and the 409 mapping when phase 4 opens. What this record adds to the route is unchanged in kind. `DELETE app/api/courses/[courseId]/packages/[packageId]/route.ts` counts every attempt on the language's versions, previews included, and answers 409 "learner attempts exist on this language" (`:281-300` at 1aeb339): the same misleading refusal for preview data. It keeps refusing, because the purge has no language scope and this record adds none to 0107, but it says why. It counts through a helper in `lib/preview/` (the only place that may name `preview_kind`, step 62): attempts that are not previews answer today's 409; otherwise any `legacy_role` row answers 409 "Earlier staff attempts are kept on this language. Deactivate it instead" (decision A33: no button purges those rows, and the retention run removes only the aged ones that nothing protects, and only under option (b) with the switch on, so this is tested before the next case and the message never promises a delete the admin cannot make; qualified by the check of the fold, the text said "never purged"); otherwise new-kind previews answer 409 "Only preview data exists on this language. Delete this course's preview data in Preview Reports, then delete the language". The route purges nothing. It does delete attempts in one case, by cascade, which round 4's text denied: the version guard counts only attempts that are not previews (`0100:528-531`), so a preview attempt on the language, one started between the route's count and its delete included, goes with the language and leaves no log row. The route's own count is what stops that outside the window; `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` gains the case that this route's `course_packages` delete is never called on a service client, as for the course route. A spec 75 case covers the three answers and the delete that succeeds after the manual purge; the race with a learner attempt cannot be staged in a spec, so it is a rehearsal case (step 63: the guard refuses through the cascade for a session claim and lets the service role through). It is inside phase 4's days, as an estimate. The cascades that still delete attempt rows without the purge function are named, not hidden: an organisation's delete; a user's delete; a course's delete, for by-role rows that nothing protects (logged by the route's purge call, step 82) and for a Preview started in the window between the purge and the delete; and a language's delete, for a Preview started in the window between its count and its delete. The first two remove the whole tenant or person, their purge log included. None can be reached by a learner; the course's and the language's cascades remove no attempt that is not a preview and no kept by-role row. **These four are exceptions to the owner's "an audit record for every deletion", and are put to the owner as such (reading R22; check of the fold, 2026-11-13).** After an organisation's or a user's delete no audit row of their preview data survives, because the log goes with the tenant or names a person who no longer exists. In the two windows, a Preview started in the same moment an admin deletes the course or the language is removed with no log row. The windows are not closed by a log row written from the route: the log is inserted only by the purge function (step 79), and a route that wrote it would be a second writer of the audit. Nothing is stored about a row removed in a window: a dry run writes nothing, and the routes' counts are not kept.

86. **Guards.** `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` and `preview-run-state.test.ts` (step 62); the purge rehearsal (step 63); spec 75 (step 64); after-checks A21 to A23 (step 19). `attempt-insert-sites.test.ts` asserts that a preview insert names `preview_kind` and no official tag. The tier guard's one amendment is stated in step 62: check (ii) is unchanged, a new check (ii-b) limits `preview_kind` and `preview_run_id` to the launch page, `lib/alds/launch.ts`, `lib/preview/`, the two preview routes and the Preview Reports pages, and `context_kind` is not guarded by name. `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` asserts positively that `lib/lrs/` (with `lib/lrs/abandoned.ts`), `lib/integrations/` and the raw attempt readers of step 49 leave the preview context out.

    **The owner allowed the departure below on 2026-11-13 (A36):** "I agree to allow deletion of Preview data, with a dry run before deletion and an audit record for every deletion. This permission must apply only to Preview/test data. Official learner records and completion history must remain protected." The dry run is step 81's and every button's first call; the audit record is the `preview_purge_log` row; the limit to preview and test data is the selection predicate and, for by-role rows, the kept test of step 82. **Where each condition holds, and where it does not (check of the fold, 2026-11-13; reading R22).** A dry run comes before every delete a person starts: each button and each product delete. The two automatic runs, the nightly retention run and the run after a restore, have no dry run before them; they report their counts in the function's answer and in the log row, and the same function can be called as a dry run at any time to see what the next run would remove. An audit row is written for every deletion the purge function makes. The deletions it does not make are the four cascades named above in this step; they leave no audit row and are stated to the owner as exceptions.

    **The principle this section departs from.** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 3: "Learner history is never rewritten or deleted: attempts, statements, stored package bytes." The purge deletes attempt rows and their statements. It is the first operation in this system that cannot be undone. The departure is the owner's own requirement (R7), it is limited to preview data of the three new kinds, and it is fenced by the selection predicate, the isolation check, the dry run, the count assertion, the audit row and the rehearsal. Accepting this record with decision A36 allows it; nothing else in principle 3 changes.

    **Placement.** Phase 1 (0107): the columns, `preview_runs`, the settings, the log, the purge function and its call from the nightly refresh, the wrapper's early return, Q10, the purge rehearsal. Phase 2: Preview by mode with `preview_kind = 'course'` and the launch mode of A32; the tenant LRS and CRM filters, the abandon path and the raw attempt readers; the guard amendments. Phase 1's pull request also carries the refresh route's failure reporting (step 81). Phase 4: the three product-delete routes (step 85). Phase P: Preview Reports, test runs, the manual delete route, the retention setting, spec 75, the runbook step. Under R12's alternative only the path and journey report pages and the test runs leave the release; the manual delete with its page, the retention setting, spec 75's course, isolation and purge cases and the runbook step stay in it (step 72). No new migration file: every database object of this section is in 0107, so D3's order for the sitting is unchanged. 0109 recreates `refresh_report_views()` again and keeps the purge call.

### T. Testing a whole Learning Path or Journey without official records (the owner's A21 question)

87. **The question.** The owner agreed that Preview has no Continue button and asked how an admin or authorised tester then validates a complete path or journey "without creating official learner progress or results". Today there is no such way. A path can be previewed one step at a time, and on staging a preview from a path shows a Continue that skips the wait. A journey cannot be previewed at all: the only way to walk one is a real self-enrolment, which writes real day credits, a real completion and a badge, and shows in every journey report and the CRM feed. A later calendar day can be reached only by back-dating the start date.

88. **The options.**

    | | 1. A guided test run in Preview Reports (recommended) | 2. A test-learner account | 3. A scripted harness |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | What it is | A staff member starts a "Test run" of a path or journey. Each launch is a preview in the run's own context; the run page shows every step or day as a learner would see it | A real account with the learner role, enrolled like any learner, in an organisation kept for testing | The lifecycle specs (63, 68, 69, 73) drive a browser as a learner on staging |
    | Official records written | None | Yes, real ones for that account: attempts, credits, completions, points, emails, CRM rows | Yes, in throwaway organisations on staging |
    | Locks, pass rules, step and day order | Yes: the same pure rules, read over the run's rows | Yes | Yes |
    | Calendar unlock of a later day, release dates, without waiting | Yes: the run has a simulated date the tester moves forward | Only by back-dating the journey's start date; a step's release date cannot be skipped | Yes, by fixture dates |
    | The Continue button itself and its wait | No. The run page offers the next step; the launch bar has no Continue | Yes | Yes |
    | Real emails | No. It names the email a learner would get | Yes, to the test mailbox | Captured, not delivered |
    | CRM and the webhook | No. It says whether one would be sent | Yes, and Yoddha sees the test account unless the organisation is separate | Yes, against a test receiver |
    | Points, badges, leaderboards | No | Yes | Yes |
    | Grants, requests, revision visits, self-enrolment, reminders, reports, the Overview | No: these are official flows a preview is kept out of | Yes | Yes |
    | Where it can be used | Staging and production | Staging only. On production it is test data (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 5), and keeping it out of reports would need a test flag that every official reader must honour: a second exclusion rule beside the preview one, which this record exists to avoid | Staging only |

    A fourth option was considered and is not offered: a preview that carries the path and shows a no-wait Continue (the alternative of the round-3 decision A21). The owner decided on 2026-11-13 that Preview has no Continue button, and this record does not bring one back under another name. If the owner later wants a "next step" control inside the launch bar of a test run, that is a new decision for the owner.

89. **The recommendation: option 1, the guided test run, with the staging practice of options 2 and 3 for what it cannot reach.**
    - *Start.* "Test run" on the path editor, the journey program editor and Preview Reports, for the roles of reading R9. `POST app/api/preview/runs/route.ts` inserts a `preview_runs` row with the service role after the session, organisation and role checks; a journey run pins the program's current version.
    - *The run page* (`app/[org]/(admin)/preview-reports/runs/[runId]/`) lists the steps or days with the state a learner would see, from `pathProgress()` or `computeJourneyState()` fed with the run's preview rows, the run's pinned version and its simulated date. The real rules are what is tested, because the same functions decide. A journey run's days are "credited in this run" when the run's preview attempt of that day satisfies the day's pass rule; no credit row exists.
    - *Launch.* Each open step or day has "Open (test run)": `launch?mode=preview&run=<id>`, with `&day=<day id>` for a journey. The resolver's test-run row (step 22) checks the run and that the step or day is open in it; a locked step is refused as it is for a learner. The launch bar says "Preview: not an official record" and has no Continue. The exit returns to the run page, which now shows the next step open and offers it.
    - *Simulated date.* "Next day" and "Jump to date" on the run page move `preview_runs.simulated_date`. Calendar days, release dates and the deadline are read against it, so a 30-day journey can be walked in one sitting and "behind" and "overdue" can be seen.
    - *Result.* The run, its scores and its progress show in Preview Reports and nowhere else, and go with the purge.
    - *What it does not test, and where that is tested.* The Continue button and its wait, real email delivery, the CRM feed and webhook, points and badges, grants and requests: on staging, with learner accounts by hand and by specs 63, 68, 69, 70, 72 and 73 every night. The run page says so in one line, so a tester does not take a green test run as proof of those.
    - *Activity days (added by the independent review of round 4; decision A41).* D13's activity days, built in phase G, are credited with no attempt (`credit_kind` `self` or `admin`), so a run that reads only its preview attempts could never pass one and would stop at the first such day. From phase G the run page shows "Mark done (test run)" on an open activity day; it adds the day's id to `preview_runs.done_activity_day_ids` (step 79), and the run's state counts that day as credited in the run. No `journey_day_progress` row, approval or email is written; the mark is purged with the run. It is an exception to "a test run's progress is derived from preview attempts only", limited to activity days, and it is stored on the run's own row and in no official table, so the rule of section A, principle 7, and step 80, that nothing of a preview enters a progress table, holds. What it does not test: the approval flow of an admin-credited activity, which stays a staging check with a learner account. Until phase G no activity day exists and the list stays empty.
    - *Cost and placement.* 3 days in phase P; half a day in phase G for the activity mark; the schema is in 0107 (step 79).

### U. Journey republish and day identity (the owner's A27 answer)

90. **What identifies a day today: nothing but its number.** A version's frozen days hold `{day, course_id, mission_title}` and no identity (`app/api/journey/program/route.ts:82-86`). Credits and attempt tags hold an integer day and no version (`0058:169`, `:173`, `:192`). An enrolment's earlier pins are overwritten with no history (`route.ts:135-140`). The draft table has a row id, `journey_days.id`, but it is a slot id: the editor never loads it, "move up" swaps the content of two numbered rows and leaves the rows where they are (`journey-client.tsx:472-485`), and save upserts on the number (`route.ts:663-665`). Used as it is, that id would be the per-number rule again. Today an in-progress attempt survives a publish to all only because the launch finds it by course version and re-tags it to the new day number (`launch:834-884`); 0107's immutability trigger and the per-context lookup remove exactly that. So the round-3 text would have repeated completed days and orphaned started ones, which is what the owner's answer forbids.

91. **The design: a day keeps one identity for life, and credits and contexts name it.** In 0107:
    - `journey_days.id` becomes the day's identity: it travels with the day when the admin moves it (step 92). `journey_days.removed_at timestamptz` (new): a removed day is kept, without a number, so attempts and credits that name it keep a parent. `day_number` becomes nullable, with `check (removed_at is null or day_number is null)`; `unique (program_id, day_number)` (`0058:73`) is unchanged and ignores the nulls.
    - `journey_versions.day_ids jsonb` (new): `{"<day number>": "<day id>"}` for every day of the version, and `day_ids_source text check (day_ids_source in ('slot_backfill','editor'))`. Backfill (once): from `journey_days` by (program, day number), source `slot_backfill`. It is exact as a reading of history by slot, because until the new editor ships a row's id and its number are the same thing (step 90). `days` itself is not rewritten (principle 5). A paste-window `before insert` trigger fills `day_ids` the same way, marked `slot_backfill`, for a version published by the old code; 0108 drops it and sets the column `not null`.
    - `course_attempts.journey_day_id` in the key, the CHECK and the immutability trigger (steps 5, 7); `attempt_requests.journey_day_id` (step 8).
    - `journey_day_progress.day_id uuid references journey_days(id) on delete no action`, backfilled once from (the enrolment's program, `day_number`), then `not null`; `unique (enrollment_id, day_id)` is created and `unique (enrollment_id, day_number)` (`0058:173`) is dropped, because after a move two credits of one enrolment can carry the same number at credit time. `day_number` stays as the number when credited.
    - No existing column value changes: every identity is written into a new column, derived from the row's own program and number.

92. **The editor and the save (phase 2; an API change, recorded here).** The journey admin page loads each row's id; `DayRow` carries it. "Move up" and "Move down" swap the two rows' numbers, not their contents. "Insert a day here" creates a row and shifts later numbers; "Remove day" marks the row removed and shifts later numbers up. Clearing a day's module keeps the day (it becomes a rest day), as today. When one save clears module C on one day and sets C on another, the editor asks "Move instead?", because otherwise the day's identity stays behind and learners who completed C would meet it again as a new day (reading R17). The curriculum PUT saves through `journey_save_days(p_program_id uuid, p_days jsonb) returns jsonb` (plpgsql, `security invoker`, so the table's admin-write RLS still decides; `revoke execute ... from public, anon`, `grant execute ... to authenticated, service_role`): in one transaction it clears the numbers of the rows that move, writes the new numbers, inserts new rows, marks removed rows, and raises if an active row is left without a number. The publish writes `day_ids` from the rows with source `editor`, in the same pull request as the editor, so that a version is marked `editor` only when every move in it was a move of identity. The two never ship apart.

93. **Credit, launch and resume by identity.**
    - The context of a journey attempt is `journey:<enrolment id>:<day id>`. It never changes, and nothing needs to change it: when the day moves to another number or gets another course, the same context still names the same day.
    - `journey_record_completion` credits the attempt's own day (step 13). `journey_credit_state` and the twin decide completion and the open day by day id (steps 13, 45).
    - The launch (step 22) admits a day that is open, or credited, or **resumable**: its context holds an open official attempt. Without the third, a started day that moved behind the open day, or beyond the calendar, could not be reopened. While that attempt is open the launch resumes it; it does not open the day's current course beside it. The journey page lists resumable days under "Started earlier", a removed one included (the list is built in phase 6; from phase 2 a started day that is still in the pinned version is resumed from its day on the journey page, and a removed day, which has no place on the page until the list exists, by its launch link, which is how phase 2's gate exercises it). The access check that runs before the resolver admits these launches too (step 23): without that change it would refuse a started day that was removed, swapped or moved past the ceiling before the resolver was ever asked. A completed enrolment admits the resume of an attempt that was open when a publish completed it, and nothing else new (step 22).
    - Links carry the day id. A link with a number (an old bookmark, a CRM target issued before the change) is resolved through the pinned version's current numbering and then checked like any other; the check that the day holds the launched course stops it from opening a different day's module.
    - Readers that show days by number are fed through `creditedDaysFor` (step 45). The day funnel groups by day id and labels each with its current number. The CRM `days[]` looks credits up by day id and gains an additive `day_id`. The tenant LRS day activity keeps the number at credit time, so no statement already sent is renumbered and the profile needs no further change.

94. **The re-pin: one function, one transaction.** `journey_repin_enrollments(p_program_id uuid, p_version_id uuid, p_draft jsonb, p_dry_run boolean) returns jsonb` (plpgsql, `security definer`, service role only; no argument has a default) replaces the route's update and loop (step 47). The signature was changed by the independent review of round 4: the round-4 form named no target, and before a publish the program's current version is still the old one, so its dry run would have compared old with old and shown 0 everywhere. A real run names the version to pin (`p_version_id`, with `p_draft` null and `p_dry_run` false); the function raises unless that version belongs to the program and is its current version. A dry run passes `p_version_id` null and the would-be version as `p_draft` (`days`, `day_ids`, `days_total`, as the publish will freeze them; step 47) and changes nothing. Both compare each enrolment's pinned version with that target by the same code. It locks the program's active enrolments in id order; applies the gate below; sets `version_id` to the target on those it re-pins; writes the credits of a relaxed pass rule (step 95); calls the completion core with the origin `publish` (step 13); and returns the counts of step 47. With `p_dry_run` it returns the counts and changes nothing. Completed and reset enrolments are not selected. Today these are separate requests with no transaction.

    **The gate for versions published before the new editor.** The backfill reads history by slot. It cannot recover a move an admin made with the old editor: such a move swapped content between numbered rows, and reads now as two course swaps in place. For an enrolment whose pinned version is `slot_backfill`, identity alone would therefore still repeat or skip a module across such a move. So for those enrolments, and only those, the function re-pins only when the first day at which the old and new versions differ (course, required or not, the per-day pass rule, or a cut of `days_total`) comes after the learner's furthest day that holds a credit or any attempt. Otherwise the enrolment stays on its version; it is counted (`kept_on_version`), named in the publish confirmation, and offered the admin's existing reset-and-re-enrol. For those learners the owner's third sentence is not met by that publish: they keep everything they did and do not get the update. This is the one limit of the design. It concerns only enrolments that exist before the new editor ships. Production has none and is expected never to have one, because its first learner comes after the release; on staging they are test data. Q9 sizes it before the paste.

95. **The outcome in every edge the owner's four sentences cover.** Edits are made with the new editor and published to all enrolled learners. "Done" means the learner holds a credit for the day; "started" means an open official attempt; "ahead" means neither.

    | Edge | Done | Started | Ahead |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | A day removed (the day removed, its module cleared, or `days_total` cut below it) | The credit is kept and counts towards nothing. Nothing is retaken | The attempt stays resumable and can be finished. Its result is kept on the attempt and a credit row is written as history; neither counts towards the journey (reading R13). This holds also when the same publish completes the enrolment (every remaining required day is credited): the enrolment completes at the publish, the open attempt stays resumable in the completed enrolment (step 22), and its history credit is still written (step 13) | The day is skipped |
    | A day's course swapped | The credit stands. The update does not reach this learner (the owner's third sentence): the day keeps showing the course they completed, with its score, on every surface, its review opens that course, and the new course is not offered inside the journey | The learner finishes the course they started and that credits the day; the new course is not opened beside it (reading R13) | The new course |
    | A day inserted before the learner's current day | Completed days keep their credits under their new numbers | The started day, one number later, stays resumable; its completion credits it even though an earlier day is open | The inserted day has no credit and is the open day: the learner goes back to it. In calendar mode its date has passed, so it counts as behind at once and reminders follow (reading R15; the publish confirmation shows `sent_back`) |
    | Days reordered | Credits follow their days; a done day moved later stays done | Resumable wherever it moved, beyond the calendar ceiling too; credit has no ceiling (A30) | An uncredited day moved before the learner's furthest day becomes the open day |
    | A required flag changed | A rest day that gains a module is a new required day; nobody holds a credit for it. A mission day that becomes a rest day is a removed day (row 1). A per-day pass rule (step 46) made stricter or looser: an existing credit stands | A publish never makes a started day harder (reading R14, default changed by the independent review of round 4): the attempt is credited when it satisfies the rule of the version it was launched under or the pinned version's (step 13). A rule made stricter does not fail work started before it; a rule relaxed helps at once. A result that satisfies neither blocks the day until a grant, as any fail does | The new rule. A rule relaxed on a day the learner already finished without passing: the re-pin writes the credit, because the day's official attempt now satisfies the rule (`credited_by_rule_change`); it never removes one |
    | A completed enrolment and a new day | Not re-pinned (step 47): it stays completed with its badge and certificate | Not applicable | Not applicable |
    | An attempt started before the republish and finished after it | Not applicable | Credited to its own day, whatever number or course the day has now; history if the day was removed. The round-3 day-course check, which would have refused it, is withdrawn | Not applicable |

    One thing no publish controls: a course's own `pass_required` is not versioned. Changing it reaches every started and future journey day that inherits it at once, with no publish (`0089:46-51`, kept as the fallback of step 9). The course form says so.

    Against the owner's four sentences: (1) a completed day is never required again, in every row; (2) a started day loses nothing and is not restarted, in every row; (3) the update reaches every learner who has not completed the changed day, with the stated limit of step 94 and the two readings where (2) and (3) pull against each other for a learner who is mid-way through a swapped or removed day (R13) or a day whose pass rule the publish made stricter (R14), each decided for the started work; (4) no attempt row and no credit row is updated or deleted by a publish, proven by the before-and-after comparison of the rehearsal and spec 69.

96. **What this removes from round 3.** The per-day-number rule of decision A27 and its amend list; `day_course_mismatch`; `days_reopened` and `failed_days_moved`; the second consequence of A11 (a moved day must be retaken) and its first (a moved failed day gives a new official attempt without a grant: the day keeps its context, so the failed result stays with it). What stays: one rule in SQL and its TypeScript twin, the enrolment lock, the one-function completion, A30. Alternatives weighed for this answer are in "Alternatives considered".

### V. Restore: the purge after a restore, and the restore rehearsal (the owner's second message of 2026-11-13)

97. **A restored database is purged before it serves.** The owner: "Please also ensure that restoring a backup does not permanently reintroduce Preview records that have already crossed their retention period. The expiry cleanup should be applied after a restore." A dump is a copy of a past day, so it holds preview rows that have since been deleted by age or by hand.
    - *The rule.* After any restore of a database dump, into a new project or over an existing one, and before the application is pointed at it or re-opened, `purge_preview_data` is run once with reason `restore`, a null organisation and the scope `{"organization": true}` (step 81). It removes every preview row and test run of the new kinds in every organisation, not only the aged ones, because the dump cannot say which rows were deleted by hand after it was taken. Under A33 option (b), with the switch on in the restored settings row, it also removes the aged by-role rows that nothing protects. It selects no official row and no kept by-role row. It writes its log rows with reason `restore`. The every-preview scope is this record's reading of the owner's sentence, which names only the records past their retention (reading R23, step 81).
    - *The settings are set before the purge (check of the fold, 2026-11-13).* The purge reads `preview_settings` as the dump held it. A dump taken before the by-role switch was turned on, or before L was changed, would make the restore run keep by-role rows that have since been deleted, or judge age by an old L. So the current values must exist outside the database: gate (4) of step 74a records `live_days`, `backup_days` and `purge_legacy` in the release runbook, and from then on every change of the row is recorded there by the platform owner as a dated line (the three values, nothing else); the settings page's confirmation says so (phase P, with the retention setting). A change that is not written down is the gap this cannot close: it is a procedure, not code, and the gate's note says so. Before the restore purge the operator updates the restored row to the last recorded values and reads them back; an update that changed no row does not pass. If no line exists, the dump's values stay and the owner is told before the database serves.
    - *Set-aside previews in the dump (check of the fold, 2026-11-13).* Step 81 lets a preview that an official row points at stay, set aside, until the owner decides, so a dump can hold one and the restore purge then answers `excluded` above 0. That alone must not stop a disaster restore. The operator compares the excluded attempt ids of the answer with the ids listed under `excluded` in the restored `preview_purge_log`, which holds every set-aside case reported up to the dump's date. When every id is listed there, the step passes and the ids are reported to the owner as still waiting. An id that is not listed is treated as a new breach: the database is not served, and it goes to the owner, who decides. It can also be a preview younger than one night, which no nightly run had yet seen; the operator cannot tell the two apart, so the answer is the same.
    - *The runbook line (executor, phase P, with the restore step already priced there).* `docs/ops/backup-restore.md`, Scenario 1, gains a step between "Verify" and "Point the prod Worker at the new project": "Run the preview purge with reason `restore` and read its answer: `excluded` must be 0. Do not point the Worker at this database before this step has passed. Testers lose their recent previews in a restore." **Replaced by the check of the fold (2026-11-13) with three lines, in this order, all before "Point the prod Worker":** (i) "Restore the privileges. The dump carries no grant and no revoke. Run the grant and revoke statements collected from the migrations (the statements only; never paste again a migration file that the dump already holds), then run the privilege matrix that ends that file. It must pass before anything else is done." (step 98 says why and where the collected statements come from); (ii) "Set `preview_settings` (`live_days`, `backup_days`, `purge_legacy`) to the values of the last dated line in the release runbook and read them back."; (iii) "Run the preview purge with reason `restore` and read its answer. Every excluded attempt id must already be listed under `excluded` in this database's `preview_purge_log`; an id that is not listed stops the restore and goes to the owner. Do not point the Worker at this database before these three lines have passed. Testers lose their recent previews in a restore." The single-table Scenario 2 gains the line that `course_attempts`, `xapi_statements`, `xapi_state` and `preview_runs` are never restored alone without the same purge.
    - *A dump older than 0107.* It holds no row of a new kind and no purge function. Restoring it means pasting the later migrations again in their order; the purge is run after 0107 is there and answers 0. The runbook says so, because the first dumps after the release are of this kind.
    - *Proof.* Purge rehearsal case (11) of step 63 proves the delete on seeded rows, a manually deleted one that the seed "restored" included; the restore rehearsal of step 98 proves the step on a real restored copy.
    - *What it cannot do.* It runs in the restored database only. It does not reach the dump itself: copies inside dumps end by the bucket's rule (step 83).

98. **The restore rehearsal: a gate of the production sitting.** The owner: "Please make a successful backup restore rehearsal mandatory before the production sitting. It should be carried out in an isolated environment, with the restore process and verification documented." Until now the proof of the backup was a green run and the restore drill (download, decrypt, completeness by counts of the file); a restore into a database has never been rehearsed (step 74).
    - *Isolated environment.* Not production and not staging. Staging is one shared database that every harness and the pull-request e2e job write to and purge, and production's rows must not be loaded into the test environment. Two environments qualify: (i) recommended, a separate throwaway database project, created empty by the owner in the vendor's dashboard for this rehearsal only and deleted at its end; this is Scenario 1 of the runbook, the same path a real disaster would take, and it has the platform's roles, `auth` schema and API layer, so the application can be run against it; (ii) a local Postgres of the dump's major version on the owner's machine. Option (ii) proves that the file decrypts, loads and counts, and nothing more: it has none of the platform's roles or API, so the application cannot be checked against it, and statements that name those roles may fail. The gate needs (i). If a throwaway project cannot be created, (ii) is recorded with "application health: not performed", and whether that opens the gate is the owner's decision at the sitting, stated beforehand. The Supabase CLI is not used in either (it is linked to production), and no local Supabase stack is planned.
    - *When, and with which files.* After the release candidate is fixed and before the sitting, with a production dump taken for the rehearsal by an on-demand run of the backup workflow, and with the candidate's migration files. An earlier rehearsal, for example now (proposal P12), is useful to find problems early and does not open the gate, because it cannot include the candidate's files.
    - *The process, in order, each step recorded.* (1) On-demand backup run, green. (2) Download, decrypt, decompress, as in the drill. (3) Load into the empty isolated project with `psql`; the number of errors is recorded and each is explained or the rehearsal fails. (4) Verification A, completeness: the row count of every table of `public`, and of `auth.users`, on the restored copy against the same counts read on production within the hour (production has no learners, so they are expected to be equal; any difference is explained); the counts of policies, triggers, functions, indexes and constraints against production's. (5) Verification B, privileges: the dump carries no owner and no grant statements (`--no-owner --no-privileges`, the runbook's operational notes), so the literal grants that migrations have carried since 2026-10-30 are not in it. The rehearsal checks the privilege matrix on the restored copy (a learner's and an admin's read of the tables the application reads, and a real 42501 for `anon`); what is missing is restored by the grant statements of the migrations, and the runbook gains that step with the list the rehearsal found. This is the most likely place for the rehearsal to find a real gap, and it is the reason a count of rows alone is not enough. **Widened by the check of the fold (2026-11-13): lost revokes, not only missing grants.** `--no-privileges` drops every `revoke` as well as every `grant` (`.github/workflows/backup.yml:120-122`). A function that a dump re-creates gets PostgreSQL's own default, execute for `public`, so without its `revoke execute ... from public, anon, authenticated` every `security definer` function that the API can call by name would be open to `anon` and `authenticated` on the restored copy: `purge_preview_data`, both forms of `consume_grant`, `record_learning_activity`, `launch_context`, `journey_repin_enrollments` and the others of the matrix (0100 shows the dependence at `0100:578-581`). The same holds for table revokes and for the column-level revoke on `org_api_keys` (step 8, after-check A17). A check of table reads alone would pass while that exposure exists. So verification B is not a sample of reads. The dump of the rehearsal is taken before the sitting, so at this point the copy holds the objects up to production's last migration and none of 0107's: the functions a bare restore exposes here are the older ones, 0103's `consume_grant` and `record_revision_visit` and the others that carry a revoke today, and step 19's matrix, which lists 0107's objects, cannot run yet. Verification B is therefore in two parts. Part one, here: the privileges of `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` on every function, relation and column of `public` are read on the copy as loaded (`has_function_privilege`, `has_table_privilege`, `has_column_privilege`) and compared with the same read on production, as verification A compares counts; the differences are recorded (what the vendor's defaults give a new project is read here, not assumed); the fix below is applied; the comparison is read again and must show no difference, and one real call as `anon` and one as `authenticated` to `consume_grant` must each be refused with 42501. Part two, after (7) and before (8): step 19's privilege matrix for the objects of 0107 to 0109, with a call as `anon` and as `authenticated` refused with 42501 on every service-role-only function, `purge_preview_data` and `consume_grant` named first, and the `org_api_keys` column case. The fix is the grant and revoke statements of the migrations, collected in their order into one file by the executor in phase 9a and kept beside the runbook; statements only, never a migration file that the dump already holds, because step 4's never-paste-again list forbids re-pasting those files (a dump older than 0107 still gets the later migrations pasted once, in their order, as step 97 says). The file ends with its own check: for every revoke it holds, the privilege reads false for that role, and for every grant, true; that check is what the runbook means by "the privilege matrix" in a real restore, where production is gone and there is nothing to compare with. The proof that the collection is complete is the comparison of part one showing no difference on the restored copy, not the collection's own text. It is priced inside phase 9a's half day for the rehearsal, as an estimate: the first text already had the executor list the grants, and the revokes come from the same statements; if the collection proves larger, the executor reports it before the rehearsal. Dumping with privileges instead would change the backup workflow and its portability note, and is not planned here. The runbook's line (i) of step 97 is this fix followed by the matrix. (7a), after (7) and before (8), because `preview_settings` arrives with 0107: part two of verification B, and then the settings step of step 97, in which `preview_settings` is set to the values the release runbook records and read back; on a copy of production before the gate of step 74a these are the defaults, and the step is still run so the procedure is proven. (6) Verification C, the application's health: a production build of the code that runs on production, started on the owner's machine with the restored project's address and keys in its environment, never deployed anywhere: the health route answers 200, one owner account signs in, one admin page and one learner page render. It settles whether the dump carries the `auth` schema, which the runbook leaves open. (7) Verification D, the sitting's pastes on a copy of production's own data: 0105 and 0106 if production does not hold them, then 0107, then 0108 and 0109, each with its before- and after-checks, as step 74 orders them. This is added value the owner did not ask for and costs minutes: the sitting's files are proven against production's real rows before the sitting. (8) Verification E, the purge after the restore: `purge_preview_data` with reason `restore` runs on the copy and answers with every count 0 and `excluded` 0 (production has no preview), and the row counts of every table are the same before and after it. That it deletes what it should is proven on seeded rows by case (11) of step 63, not here: no test row is seeded into a copy of production. (9) The copy is destroyed: the throwaway project is deleted, the plaintext dump and the decrypted file are removed from the machine, and both are recorded.
    - *What passes.* Every step done, every difference explained, the application healthy, the migrations' checks green, the purge answer clean, the copy destroyed. A failed step fails the rehearsal; it is repeated after the cause is fixed.
    - *Where the record lives.* The procedure is a new scenario in `docs/ops/backup-restore.md` ("Restore rehearsal"). The result is a dated section of the release runbook under `docs/release/`, where the v1.4.0 runbook already records the restore drill: dates, counts, pass or fail per step, the count of grant and revoke statements replayed and the matrix result before and after them, and the time the restore took. The repository is public, so the record holds counts and outcomes only: no project reference, no address, no key, no name, no row content.
    - *Price.* Half a day of executor work in phase 9a (the procedure, the count queries, the checklist, the record's form), added in step 72's round 5 table. The owner's time is not executor work: an estimate of two to three hours for the rehearsal itself, and a throwaway project for its length. Whether the vendor's plan allows another project at no cost was not read; the owner checks it when creating the project.
    - *After the release.* The gate is for this sitting. The rehearsal becomes the runbook's standing practice before any sitting that pastes a migration on a production with learners; a later record may fix its frequency.

### Risks and protections

| Risk | Protection |
|---|---|
| Learners redo the same module in several products | The owner's requirement; the course page shows one line per product; the editors warn on repeats |
| Two scored results for one module inside one journey | D1; per-day scope is the only alternative to a stuck day |
| A second product earns no points for a module already paid | D8(c); every product still shows its completion and score |
| A forged link yields a free official attempt | Step 22 before any write; a failed read refuses; unit matrix and spec 68 |
| Grading: an official attempt in one product changes another | The official rule filters by context first; `consume_grant` checks the grant's context; the rehearsal and spec 68 |
| Grading: a preview becomes a result | `preview` is its own context, excluded by the one rule in SQL and TypeScript; the CHECK ties it to `is_preview` |
| Grading: a staff member's or analyst's real work is lost as a preview | The owner's answers D7 and R6 (all three roles): Start is a real attempt; `preview-mode-sites.test.ts` |
| Grading: an admin's test clicks become official results on KIVO 2.0 courses | The owner chose this on 2026-11-13 as "an intentional change to my earlier KIVO 2.0 rule dated 6 November 2026". Admins who only test press Preview or start a test run; the launch bar says which mode is running |
| Grading: an admin's republish gives a learner a fresh official attempt on a failed day | Removed by the day identity: a moved day keeps its context and its failed result (section U). Only a course swap on a failed day opens a first attempt, of a different course |
| A journey enrolment that can never complete after a re-pin, with no badge, claim or email | One rule by day identity in the SQL completion, the SQL state and `computeJourneyState`: the open day is always one without a credit (steps 13, 45; section U); the rehearsal's re-pin fixtures, the parity check and spec 69 |
| Learners: an admin's republish repeats a completed day or loses a started one | The owner's A27 answer. Credits and contexts name the day's identity, which moves with the day; a started day stays resumable and credits on completion; the re-pin is one transaction with a dry run and counts in the confirmation; the rehearsal and spec 69 compare every attempt and credit row before and after (steps 93 to 95) |
| Learners enrolled before the new editor ships are kept on their version by a later publish | Step 94's gate, counted and named in the confirmation; expected 0 on production; Q9 sizes it on staging |
| A learner is sent back to an inserted day and shows as behind | Reading R15 for the owner; `sent_back` in the confirmation before the publish |
| Existing records: the purge deletes an official row | The selection predicate is repeated in the delete; a non-preview row cannot be selected, and a by-role row only as step 82 allows; the isolation check sets aside and reports any attempt with an official side effect; the count assertion; the dry run; the audit row; the purge rehearsal compares every other table before and after; spec 75 (step 81) |
| Existing records: real learner work flagged as a preview by role is deleted | The owner's A33 answer is put back with one clarification. Under the recommended option (b) a by-role row is deleted only when it is aged and nothing protects it; the kept test errs towards keeping (a result in any launch mode, a grant, points, a day credit, a completion record, a reported completion); the arm is behind a switch that is off until the owner has seen Q10 and the dry-run counts; kept rows are listed; the rehearsal seeds each protected kind (step 82). Under option (a) no by-role row is ever deleted. Production expected 0 |
| Existing records: the lifecycle rule that bounds preview copies in backups also shortens the backup history of official learner data | The owner chooses L and R knowing this: L = 30 leaves eight weeks of dumps, L = 60 four (step 83). The on-demand backup before every sitting and the restore rehearsal are unchanged by it |
| A restored database brings back deleted preview data, or is served with missing grants or with lost revokes (every service-role-only function callable by `anon` and `authenticated`) | The purge runs on a restored database before it serves, after the settings row is set to the recorded values (step 97); the dump carries no grant and no revoke, so the runbook replays the migrations' grant and revoke statements and runs the whole privilege matrix before the database serves, with a 42501 required for `anon` and `authenticated` on every service-role-only function; the restore rehearsal proves both on a restored copy (step 98) |
| A product delete goes on after a purge call that failed | Any error from either purge call answers 503 and nothing is deleted (step 85); `product-delete-order.test.ts` |
| One tenant's admin changes every tenant's retention, or sets a figure the backups cannot honour | The setting is the platform owner's; the CHECK ties `live_days` to `backup_days` (step 79) |
| A purge called with a scope it does not know deletes too much or silently nothing | Exact scope shapes; 22023 on anything else; a tester's call always carries their own id (step 81) |
| Privacy: preview data outlives 90 days in a backup or a log | L + R + 1 ≤ 90 with the owner's read of the backup bucket, a lifecycle rule and `live_days`; gate (4) of step 74a before production's first course; the purge after any restore (step 83) |
| Privacy: preview statements reach a tenant's LRS, where the LMS cannot delete them | Never forwarded, "Resend all history" included (decision A35); the source scan and spec 75 with forwarding on |
| Grading: a preview launched in Normal mode is read as a result | The database ties it to the `preview` context (CHECK and trigger); the one rule excludes it; the writer skips it; spec 75 asserts no official trace for all three roles (decision A32) |
| A tester takes a green test run as proof of emails, CRM or the Continue button | The run page says what it does not test; those are proven on staging by learner accounts and the nightly specs (step 89) |
| Privacy: a test run's header table is read by an official report | No-access RLS, service role only, no view over it, and the source scan of step 86 |
| The production sitting starts without a restorable backup | The on-demand green run within the hour, the repeated restore drill and, since round 5, a successful restore rehearsal in an isolated environment are gates (steps 74, 98) |
| Two last-day credits at the same moment both miss the completion | The enrolment row is locked before the credit insert (step 13); spec 69 runs two parallel requests |
| A new CRM key is version 1, so the first-learner gate would need SQL on production | The column default is 2 after the existing keys are labelled 1 (step 8, A28); after-check A17; spec 72 |
| A re-paste of 0107 marks failed writer rows as handled, or held SCORM rows as never sending | Backfills run only when their column is first added (step 4); the rehearsal re-pastes over rows the new code could have written |
| 0108 turns the nightly and every pull request's e2e red | The fixtures name `context_kind` first; the insert-sites guard scans the test folders; 0108 refuses within 24 hours of a derive firing (steps 20, 64) |
| A deploy on a database without 0107 fails launches, loses completion side effects and completes no journey at a publish to all | Decision A19: the paste-first rule of step 72; readers, crons and feeds keep their fallbacks |
| Privacy: a new view exposes every organisation's assignment dates | The terms and path views are `security_invoker` with literal grants; a cross-organisation read as `authenticated` returns 0 rows (steps 9, 18, 19) |
| Privacy: a super owner's session sets a CRM key's contract version | Table-level insert and update revoked from `authenticated`, the written columns granted back (step 8) |
| Grading: a pass that arrives after `completed` loses its rewards or credit | The settled-state marker re-runs the branch (step 12) |
| Points, credit or a webhook lost on an error | Fail-closed writer, the sweep scheduled from 0107, the Attention item. Emails are not retried (disclosed) |
| Duplicate emails | One claim per learner, product and instance, taken before the send (step 15) |
| Privacy: an analyst reads another organisation, or learner feedback | The service role only after `canViewReports`, with the organisation on every read; feedback stays `canManage` only (step 51) |
| Privacy: self-enrolment writes with the service role | Step 68 |
| Existing records | No row moved, no existing value changed; new columns only; before-counts equal after-counts; deletes refused |
| Yoddha reads "not started" for path-only learners in version 1 | The versioned feed, built in 9a and in the first release; the first-learner gate of step 74a keeps production without learners until Yoddha reads version 2; the agreement gates the tag; R4 if Yoddha cannot move |
| Reports on staging between 0107 and 0109 | The old-name views stay one row per learner (step 9); the remaining differences are listed in step 59 |
| A journey learner resumes an old attempt in the staging window | Staging only, days; the next journey launch repairs it (step 60) |
| The 0107 paste is refused | Q2 runs first; the owner's reset on staging; production reads 0 |
| A re-paste of an old file strips the new bodies | The never-paste lists (steps 4, 21, 75) |
| Workers limits on the heavier launch | `launch_context` is planned work, and the measured count gates phase 2 (step 70) |
| Workers limits on a publish to all enrolled learners | One set-based call and no email in the request (steps 47, 70) |
| Stale reads within one render | Step 25 and its test |
| Refresh time at scale | Separate budgets; the timed gate before 0109 |
