| A preview's "abandoned" statement, or a staff member's preview in an official report, through a reader that never met the official rule (`lib/lrs/abandoned.ts`, Learner Analytics, the Report Card's engagement) | Each named in steps 49 and 80 with its filter; the widened source scan of step 62; spec 75's cases |
| A purge that fails silently, or is blocked for good by one official row on a preview | The settings row is created by 0107 and the purge raises without it; a breaching attempt is set aside and reported while the rest is deleted; the refresh route answers 500 and an errored heartbeat (step 81) |
| A test run's header, or a preview that a restore or a course delete brought back or removed unseen, outside the 90-day rule | The run's own clock; the `restore` scope; the three product-delete routes purge first with a log row (steps 81, 85) |
| A started journey day refused before the resolver is asked, ended by a publish that completes the enrolment, or failed by a rule the publish made stricter | The access check admits it (step 23); a completed enrolment admits the resume and the history credit (steps 13, 22); the lenient pass rule (step 13, reading R14) |
| About 145 planned days (96.5 by the table) with the scope of 2026-11-13, its reviews and round 5, of which about 131 (87 by the table) come before production's first release; round 4 after its review said about 142 and 128 (94.5 and 85); round 3 agreed about 110 and 96 (73.5 and 64) | The owner sees the agreed figures, the added 21 days item by item and the dependencies before accepting (step 72); the owner chose R8 over A4; ways to shorten the elapsed time are proposals with their risks (section "Opportunities to finish sooner"); phases 1 to 4 are proven on staging first; the Overview and journey activities trail behind flags; the earlier-release alternative is named with what it gives up |

## Consequences

- A learner's result on a course is a result in a product. The Library, each Learning Path and each journey day keep their own official attempt, revision visits, grants, bookmark, score and status. Finishing a module in one place completes or unlocks nothing elsewhere.
- Every binding policy keeps its content and is scoped to the context. ADR 0017's guard is untouched: it is per attempt row.
- Course reports show the Individual product. ADR 0020's path arm and hold-back, on staging since 2026-11-11, never reach production as a served state.
- Admins, super owners and analysts learn for real when they press Start and test on purpose when they press Preview (D7; the owner's R6 answer of 2026-11-13, which replaces the owner's rule of 2026-11-06). A launch is never a preview by role. Preview has no Continue (the owner's A21 answer); a whole path or journey is tested in a test run (section T).
- Preview data is kept apart, shown only in Preview Reports, never counted, never sent anywhere, and deleted on request and by age. This is the first deletion of learner-data rows in the system and a named departure from `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 3, limited to preview data (section S).
- Completion side effects have one writer that fails closed. A passing error no longer pays points or fires a webhook; it delays them until the sweep.
- Each product sends one completion email. cmi5 learners gain the course email; a granted retake no longer re-sends anything. One exception to D16, which the owner accepted on 2026-11-13 (A20): a journey that an admin's publish completes sends no email; it shows as completed everywhere, the dashboard included.
- Paths and journey programs with learner attempts cannot be deleted; they are archived.
- "Everyone in this org" learners have recorded enrolments, so reports, the Overview and the CRM can see them. Members who never start are not counted.
- The CRM contract has a version per key. Version 1's meaning changes on release; the agreement is a release gate, and Yoddha reading version 2 is a gate on the first production learner.
- The effort agreed in round 3 was about 74 executor-days plus journey activities, against the plan's 50, with a planning figure of about 110; the scope of 2026-11-13, as priced after its independent review, adds 21 days, so 94.5 by the table and about 142 to plan for; round 5 adds 2 more (96.5 and about 145). Production's first release carries phases 1 to 7, 9a and P: 87 days by the table and about 131 to plan for after round 5 (round 4: 85 and about 128; round 3: 64 and about 96; the plan: 25 for phases 1 to 4); the first learner follows the gate of step 74a.
- A journey day has one identity for life. A journey is complete when every required day of its pinned version holds a credit for that identity, checked in SQL and TypeScript alike. After an admin's publish to all enrolled learners, nobody repeats a completed day or loses a started one, and learners who have not completed a changed day get its new version (the owner's A27 answer). The journey editor moves, inserts and removes days as operations of their own, and the curriculum save and the publish go through two database functions.
- A language switch closes one product's open attempt and deletes nothing; an audit row records it (A24).
- Preview and test runs exist on production, as the owner's named exception to "No test or seed data on production" (R21, 2026-11-13): identifiable, open to authorised users only, without any official effect, and gone within 90 days.
- The database backup bucket gets a lifecycle rule, set by the owner, which it has never had: backups of everything, official learner data included, then reach back R days and no further (step 83). The preview retention is one figure for the whole installation, set by the platform owner.
- A restore is followed by the preview purge before the database serves, and a successful restore rehearsal in an isolated environment is a gate of the production sitting (steps 97, 98).
- A CRM key created after 0107 reads contract version 2; older keys stay on version 1 (A28).
- Between the phase 2 merge and the production sitting, a production tag of `main` is allowed only while production has no learner (decision A19).
- Binding on later work: a new reader of "done", "official", "in progress" or "revision" calls `contextResult`, `readContextResult` or a product's progress rule, never `computeScoring` or a raw completed-or-passed test; a new write of an attempt, request or visit names its context; a new column that carries a context references its parent `on delete no action`; a new completion side effect is added inside `record_learning_activity`; a new server-render re-read carries its own abort signal; a journey day is named by its id, never matched by its number; a new store that a launch can write is added to the table of step 80 with how a preview is kept out of it and how it is purged; no code deletes an attempt row directly except `purge_preview_data` (the cascades of an organisation, a user, a preview-only course and, for previews only, a language remain; a route that deletes a course or a language deletes on the caller's session, never the service client, so 0100's guards refuse through the cascade; and a route that deletes a product purges its preview data first and refuses while the purge sets a preview aside, and the language delete refuses while any preview exists, step 85); a new feed, export or forwarder excludes the `preview` context, and so does a new reader of raw `course_attempts` rows on an official surface; a new function carries a pinned `search_path`.

## Alternatives considered

- **The ADR 0023 draft as written (paths only).** Individual plus each path as contexts, keyed on `learning_path_id`, journeys reading the Individual result. Not chosen: a Library completion would still credit a journey day and rewrite the row; a course on two journey days stays stuck; previews share the Individual slot and stay previews by role; journey membership keeps cancelling points; classification still fails open; the SCORM webhook stays unlatched; 0105's path arm stays in course reports. Kept from it: the diagnosis, the per-context scope of ADR 0014, `contextResult` with the guard test, the index split, `no action` foreign keys, the paste-first order with an either-state check, its reader sweep as the build checklist. Changed from it: journeys become contexts (D1); its decision (h), "the standing result relocks", is replaced by D14; its decision (d), course reports in the context of enrolment, by D2; its decision (c), webhook for the Individual context only, by the per-organisation scope; its decision (g) by D7.
- **Three attempt tables, one per product.** Triples the runtime, the LRS feed and the CRM logic. Rejected.
- **Filtering only in reports.** One product would still complete another. Rejected.
- **A stored `learning_records` table with a cached official pointer.** A writer on the launch hot path, a circular foreign key and a cache that can drift, for nothing a view over enrolment and attempts does not give. Rejected.
- **A generated key without an explicit kind.** A careless insert would silently become an Individual attempt. Rejected in favour of `context_kind` with the CHECK.
- **Nulling the tags on old preview rows.** An update of existing attempt columns. Rejected (step 6).
- **A one-time `side_effects_at` stamp.** Loses a pass that arrives after `completed`. Rejected (step 12).
- **Changing the grain of `v_course_attempt_summary` in 0107.** Six LIVE matviews would count a learner in two statuses until 0109. Rejected for new-name context views (step 9).
- **`notification_log` or `xp_events` as the email latch.** Neither can serve (step 15).
- **Points per product instance (D8 a).** Farmable by repeating a module in open or recreated paths; needs a ledger rule change. Declined by the owner's choice of (c).
- **Freezing the journey deadline per enrolment (D17 a).** Declined by the owner.
- **A feature flag on the core.** Two rules on one table. Rejected (step 72).
- **A webhook scope per API key.** The webhook has no key when it fires. Rejected for a per-organisation setting.
- **Run instances for yearly re-assignment now.** Deferred (D5); `path_run:<id>` stays an additive key later.
- **Journey republish, weighed against the owner's four A27 sentences (2026-11-13).** (C0) Credit per day number, round 3's rule: repeats a completed module after an insert or reorder and orphans a started day. Rejected by the owner. (C2) Keep number-keyed rows and record a day map at each re-pin: to be exact the map needs the same day identity as the chosen design; every one of about 13 readers must then translate through the chain of maps; one day has different context keys before and after a publish; a moved failed day still opens a fresh official attempt without a grant. Rejected. (C3) No identity; re-pin only the enrolments whose first changed day lies after everything they did: protects completed and started work with almost no schema, but a learner past an earlier change never gets that publish or any later one, which fails the third sentence for most learners of a running journey. Kept only as the gate for versions published before the new editor (step 94). (C4) Match a credit to any day that holds the same course: fails when a day's course is swapped (the completed day would be asked for again) and is ambiguous when a course repeats. Rejected. (C5) No re-pin of enrolled learners at all: fails the third sentence for everyone. Rejected. Chosen: the day identity of section U, the only candidate that meets all four sentences in every edge.
- **A stable day id read from today's `journey_days.id` with no editor change.** That id is a slot id tied to the number (step 90); it would be the per-number rule again. Rejected.
- **A deferrable unique constraint on (program, day number) for the save.** It makes one-statement swaps possible, but `ON CONFLICT` cannot use a deferrable constraint, so the save would need a function anyway; the function with nullable numbers needs no constraint change. Not chosen.
- **Previews with no stored result (Browse everywhere, nothing kept).** The round-3 position. Replaced by the owner's R7: results are wanted, apart, for testing.
- **A preview-only copy of the progress tables (preview credits, preview completions).** A second set of writers and tables to keep in step, and rows a wrong join could count. Rejected for progress derived from preview attempts by the same pure functions (section S).
- **A test-learner flag on real accounts.** Every official reader would have to honour a second exclusion rule. Rejected (section T).
- **The purge as TypeScript deletes, or as a new cron route.** The first breaks the rule that app code never deletes attempts and cannot reach two tables without new grants; the second adds a schedule line that fires on production before its route is deployed. Rejected for one SQL function called from the existing nightly refresh (step 81).
- **A separate migration for the preview objects.** It would add a file to the sitting whose order the owner fixed under D3. Not chosen: they are in 0107.
- **Sweeping today's by-role preview rows with the rest.** Some are real results (step 82). Rejected in round 4; since the owner's A33 answer of 2026-11-13 it is option (c) of the clarification, still not recommended.
- **A retention setting per organisation, capped by a global figure** (weighed for finding 7 of round 5). It would let a tenant keep previews for less time than the installation's limit. Not chosen: a second table and a second rule for a need nobody has stated; the one row is made the platform owner's. It can be added later without changing the purge's predicate.
- **The restore rehearsal on staging, or on a local database alone.** Staging is shared and purged by harnesses, and must not hold production's rows; a local database cannot run the application. Not chosen for the gate (step 98).

"Resolved" means this record states how; "the owner chooses" means it is listed in "Decisions requested".

Four conflicts are the owner's to choose, each an explicit item of "Decisions requested": rows 1 (A36), 3 (A32), 8 (A34) and rows 6 and 7 together (A33). Row 10 (R16) needs the owner's confirmation. R13, the sixth explicit item, weighs two of the owner's own sentences and is not a conflict with a record. Row 14 is a reading the owner may object to (R21).

**Round 5 (the owner's second message of 2026-11-13): how the rows above now stand.** The table is kept as round 4 wrote it; this paragraph is its current state. Row 1 is resolved by the owner (A36: deletion allowed for preview and test data only, with a dry run and an audit record). Row 3 is resolved by the owner (A32: Normal mode, completely isolated). Row 10 is resolved by the owner (R16: a stable day identity, in the owner's words). Row 14 is resolved by the owner, as a named exception to principle 5 with four conditions (R21); it is no longer a reading. Row 8 is agreed in its approach and open in its figures: the owner's own reading of the bucket shows every version kept, so the conflict is real today and ends when the owner sets the lifecycle rule and L and R (A34). Rows 6 and 7 are answered in part: the owner's two A33 sentences meet the owner's A36 sentence on rows that are real learner work, and one clarification is asked (step 82). One conflict is new, and it is between two of the owner's answers of the same message, not with an older record: A33's "must be automatically deleted" against A36's "Official learner records and completion history must remain protected". It is the A33 clarification. Two things about option (b) are stated here so the owner chooses knowing them. First, it widens the principle 3 departure of row 1: the purge would delete not only preview data of the three new kinds but also aged by-role rows that nothing protects, among them an attempt in Normal mode that was opened and never produced a result, with the answers stored on it; sections S and Q and the Consequences say "the three new kinds" and are read with this addition if (b) is chosen. Second, row 5's sentence that `launch_mode` stays unread no longer holds for one reader: the kept test inside `purge_preview_data` reads it, in SQL. That is not a grading, LRS, CRM or integration read, which is what ADR 0005 D1 forbids: it decides only whether an old by-role row may be deleted, and it errs towards keeping. The guard test scans `app`, `lib` and `components` (`tests/unit/alds-tier-read-sites.test.ts:42`), not migrations, so it is unaffected; the note on ADR 0005 gains a line for this reader when A33 is answered with (b). The restore rehearsal conflicts with nothing: it adds a gate.

Changed by this round but not conflicts with an accepted record: this record's own round-3 text on A27, A11 and the day-course check (never accepted; replaced by section U); the column list in the note this branch wrote on ADR 0009 (D6's rule is unchanged and wider; the note is updated); a test-learner account on production, which would meet principle 5 ("No test or seed data on production") and is therefore offered for staging only (section T).

Checked and found not to conflict: ADR 0009 decision 6 (it declined a service-role write-guard trigger; this record adds none for deletes); ADR 0017 (the fail-to-pass guard is per attempt row and previews are outside the official rule); ADR 0021 (feedback stays `canManage` only; Preview Reports show a tester's own answers, not learner feedback); D3 (no migration file is added to the sitting); the content bucket's object lock (no preview data is written there); A30 (no floor or ceiling at credit time; the day-identity rule relies on it). Not checked: ADR 0003 beyond its lines on the two backup buckets.

## Opportunities to finish sooner (proposals, not applied)

The owner asked for ways to finish sooner "without compromising any approved requirement, functionality, data accuracy, security, testing, performance, deployment safety or quality of work", shared with their impact and risks "before changing the agreed scope". None of these is applied: the phase table, the gates and the scope stand as written until the owner says otherwise. No proposal removes scope, a test, a review, the rehearsal or a gate.

**First, what the figures are.** The table's days are effort. The planning figure is effort times one and a half. Neither is elapsed time: both add the phases one after another, as if one executor did everything. The dependencies of step 72 allow more: phases 1 and 2 must come one after the other (38.25 days with round 4's scope and its review), and after phase 2, phases 3, 4, 6, 7 and P need only phase 2, while phase 5 needs phase 3 (the chain 1, 2, 3, 5 is 49.25 days). The longest chain is 1, 2, 7, 9a: 54.75 days by the table (about 82 to plan for), against 85 (about 128) end to end. The other 30 days or so are work that can run beside the chain if people and the shared resources allow. Those shared resources set the real limit: one staging database on which only one harness may run at a time; the owner's merges, staging deploys and pastes; independent review of every pull request; one nightly run a day for "two green nights"; and Yoddha's own pace. After round 5's 2 days the figures of this paragraph read: 39 for phases 1 and 2; 50 for the chain 1, 2, 3, 5; 56 for the longest chain (about 84 to plan for), against 87 (about 131) end to end; the figures in the table and the combinations below move up by about 1 day and are not rewritten. Round 5 also adds two things to the serial path that no executor can shorten: the owner's restore rehearsal before the sitting, and the owner's lifecycle rule before the first course.

Savings are in table days unless marked elapsed, and they do not add up item by item.

| # | Proposal | Saves | Needs | Risk | Owner's word needed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Run phases 3, 4, 6, 7 and P side by side once phase 2 is merged; 5 after 3; 9a after 3, 6 and 7 | Up to about 30 days of elapsed effort on the release (85 down to about 55 to 61) | Three or four executors; one owner per shared file with a merge order (the launch page, the journey page and program route, `attempt-kind.ts`, the CRM feeds are each touched by several phases); two independent reviewers; a staging-run lock; merges batched daily | Integration mistakes between streams in shared files, which could mis-state a product's result: held down by daily rebases and by re-running the guard and parity tests on every merge. More pushes mean more `qa-*` purges on staging. Review quality under load | No for the method; yes if it changes who works on what |
| P2 | Phase 1 in two streams, SQL and TypeScript, joined by the parity test | 3 to 5 days elapsed | The answer shapes of `contextResult`, `launch_context` and the path views frozen first (the record fixes most of them) | The SQL rule and its twin drift until the parity test runs; the parity test gates the second merge. 0107 stays one file and one transaction | No |
| P3 | Write phase 2 and phase 3 code on stacked branches while 0107 is in review, and merge only after the staging paste | 2 to 4 days elapsed | 0107's interfaces frozen after its review; the A32 answer for the preview row | Rework if the review changes an interface; those branches' checks stay red until the paste. Nothing merges before the paste | Yes: it starts code before 0107 is pasted, though not before acceptance |
| P4 | Build 9a in two parts: the version 2 shape (contract version, strict version 1, `steps[]`, `days[]`, `path_id`, `context`, `released`) right after phase 2; the fields that phases 6 and 7 fill (journey status and score, revisits per product) after them | 2.5 to 3.5 days elapsed on the release, and much more on the first learner: Yoddha can build against version 2 on staging weeks earlier. As written, version 2 exists on staging only when 9a deploys, and 9a is last, so all of Yoddha's build time falls after the release | The version 2 field names frozen in the addendum first; a staging key created after 0107 | Yoddha builds against fields whose values arrive later; the addendum must list them. Spec 72 asserts the shape both times | Yes: it splits a phase of the agreed table |

Production is unaffected | Yes: it rewords a gate |

Since round 5 a restore rehearsal is a gate (step 98); one made now finds problems early, the missing grants above all, and does not replace the gate's, which needs the candidate's files | Yes: the owner acts |
| P13 | Smaller pull requests along boundaries the record already draws, reviewed in parallel (the journey twin, its readers and the publish call stay one pull request) | 1 to 3 days of review time | Two reviewers and a merge order | More merges and more e2e purges on staging; every intermediate state of `main` must be consistent | No |
| P14 | Read the bucket's retention and set the lifecycle rule now (step 83), not at the first-learner gate | Removes a late surprise from the first learner's path | The owner's read: done in part on 2026-11-13 (every version is kept; step 83). What is left is to confirm the bucket, choose L and R and set the rule | A rule set now starts deleting old dumps now: the owner chooses R with that in view | Yes: the owner acts |

What the combinations come to, as arithmetic on the table's days under the staffing named, not as measurements: P1 alone, a release chain of about 55 days (plan for about 82) with three executors; P1 with P4, about 51 to 52; P1 with P2, P3, P4 and P11, about 40 to 45 (plan for about 60 to 68) with four. These moved up by about 3 days with the work the independent review of round 4 priced. The elapsed savings of P5 to P10 and P12 to P14 come on top and are calendar days, not table days.

Risks the parallel streams add, whichever proposals are taken: a branch cut before the fixtures commit restarts 0108's 24 hours (now a precondition, step 20); the production sitting needs a push freeze across every stream (step 74); concurrent e2e runs purge each other's `qa-*` data.

Not proposed, because each would compromise something the owner listed: a first release split in two (declined, A4); a flag on the core or 0107 in several files; specs or two nightly jobs at once on one staging database; dispatched runs in place of the two nights; pasting 0109 early on staging; skipping the rehearsal or the independent review; leaving the purge, the filters or the retention gate out of the first release.

## Decisions requested from the owner

D0 to D17 are answered and are not asked again. Accepting this record accepts the items below as written unless the owner objects to one; steps may be excluded individually (ADR 0001 decision 2).

**The eight items of round 3 are answered (2026-11-13).** R6, R8 (A4 declined), A19, A20, A21 and A24 were answered as recommended and are applied in the steps; their pending marks, amend lists and gates are removed. R7 and A27 were answered with new requirements, designed in sections S, T and U. The owner's words are in the Context and in each row below.

**The seven items of round 4 are answered or narrowed (the owner's second message of 2026-11-13).** A36, A32, R16, R13 with R14, and R21 are answered; their pending marks and the phase gates they held are removed, and wherever the body still says "reading R13", "reading R14", "reading R16" or "reading R21" it is now the owner's rule. **What still needs the owner's explicit word is three things: (1) the acceptance of this record; (2) the A33 clarification, one choice among options (a), (b) and (c) of step 82, recommended (b); it holds phase 1; (3) A34's three open points: that the bucket read was the database backup bucket, and the values of L and R, recommended L = 30 and R = 56; they hold production's first course, with the lifecycle rule the owner sets.** Added by the check of the round-5 fold: the A33 question now names a third thing the owner should know about (b) (results stored in Browse mode, the A33 row), and three readings are new and open to an objection, R22 (where the A36 conditions hold), R23 (the purge after a restore removes every preview, not only the aged ones) and R24 (what the build retry treats as a network failure). The paragraph below is round 4's and is kept for the record of what was asked; its list of six is replaced by this one.

**What needed the owner's explicit word after round 4 (superseded by the paragraph above).** The acceptance of this record, and with it six new items, each of which changes an accepted rule, something the owner said, or how the owner's own sentences are weighed against each other: **A36** (the deletion of preview data as a departure from `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` principle 3, with its fences), **A32** (previews launch in Normal mode; ADR 0006 step 17), **A34** (how the 90-day rule meets the backups: a shorter live retention with a lifecycle rule, or an exception), **A33** (today's by-role preview rows are kept and never swept), **R16** (D1's "day" is the day's identity) and **R13** (a learner mid-way through a changed day, including a day whose pass rule the publish made stricter, keeps the work they started; R14 is part of this question since the check of the fold, because it weighs the same two sentences of the owner the same way). The other readings, R9 to R12, R15 and R17 to R21, are recorded for the owner to object to; they need no answer, and a phase does not wait for one. What each holds (step 72, made exact by the independent review of round 4): silence on A36, A33, R13 or R16 holds phase 1; on A32, the preview row of phase 2; on A34, production's first course. An objection to a reading must come before the phase that builds it: R11, R15, R20 and the scope part of R18 before phase 1 (they are in 0107); R17, R21 and the wording part of R18 before phase 2; R9, R10 and R12 before phase P; R19 before the production sitting.

**Readings recorded for the owner (object if wrong).** Rows R6, R7, R8, R13, R14, R16 and R21 are no longer readings: the owner answered them, and each row says so. The rows still open to an objection are R1 to R5, R9 to R12, R15, R17 to R20 and, added by the check of the round-5 fold, R22 to R24. An objection to R22 or R23 must come before phase 1 (the purge and its restore scope are 0107's); R24 belongs to the build-retry pull request.

Preview data on production is bounded by its context and the retention. The owner's R7 does not name production, so this is a reading and not the owner's decision. Alternative: Preview Reports and test runs on staging only (the flag stays off on production); a course Preview on production still exists and is still purged |
| R22 | **"With a dry run before deletion and an audit record for every deletion" (the owner's A36 conditions; added by the check of the round-5 fold, 2026-11-13).** Read as met in this way. Dry run: before every delete a person starts (each button, each product delete); the nightly retention run and the run after a restore are automatic, have no dry run before them, and report their counts in the answer and the log. Audit record: one log row for every deletion the purge function makes. Four deletions happen outside the function, by cascade, and leave no audit row: an organisation's delete and a user's delete (the whole tenant or person goes, the log with it or without a person to name), and a Preview started in the same moment an admin deletes its course or its language (step 85). None touches an official row. Alternatives: a nightly dry run stored the evening before each retention run, which adds a second scheduled call and a stored result for a figure the log row already gives; for the two windows, a route-written log row, which would make the route a second writer of the audit log |
| R23 | **"The expiry cleanup should be applied after a restore" (the owner's A34 sentence; added by the check of the round-5 fold).** Read more widely than the words: after a restore the purge removes every preview and test run of the new kinds, those still inside the retention limit included, because the dump cannot show which were deleted by hand after it was taken, and "delete at any time" would otherwise be undone by a restore. Testers lose their recent previews in a restore. Alternative, the owner's literal words: only the records past their retention are removed, and a preview deleted by hand after the dump returns until its age limit (steps 81, 97) |
| R24 | **"Temporary Google Fonts/network-related build failures" (the build retry; added by the check of the round-5 fold).** Read as: the font download failing, or another network error in the build log (a timeout, a DNS failure, a reset or refused connection). The patterns are chosen by the executor in the retry's own pull request; a compile, type or module error is never one, and a log that shows both fails without a retry. It is outside this record's phases |

| A27 | Journey republish and a learner's existing progress. **Answered by the owner on 2026-11-13, against the round-3 recommendation** | The round-3 rule (credit per day number, with its consequence that a moved day may have to be retaken) is withdrawn with its amend list. The owner's words: "I would prefer the alternative approach that protects learners' existing progress when an Admin republishes a Journey", with four expected behaviours (quoted in the Context) and the priority "that an Admin's Journey updates must not unexpectedly reset or repeat work that learners have already completed or started". **The owner asked whether the architecture supports this. Yes, with a day identity added in 0107** (new columns on `journey_days`, `journey_versions`, `journey_day_progress`, `course_attempts` and `attempt_requests`, a relaxed `journey_days` key, three new functions (`journey_save_days`, `journey_repin_enrollments`, `journey_attempt_satisfies`) and a second form of `consume_grant`, an editor and API change), one limit (step 94's gate for learners enrolled before the new editor ships) and readings R13 to R17. In short: a journey day gets an identity that it keeps when the admin moves it; credits and attempt contexts name that identity; a started day stays resumable; no row is re-tagged (section U). The outcome in every edge is step 95. It is applied in steps 4, 5, 7, 8, 10, 13, 18, 19, 22, 45 to 47, 60, 61, the tests and rehearsal of steps 62 and 63, spec 69, criterion 19, section L, the risks and the Consequences. What the owner is asked about it: readings R13 to R17 (R16 explicitly, and R13 with R14 as one explicit question), and the one limit, step 94's gate for learners enrolled before the new editor ships. Changed by the independent review of round 4: a learner who completed a day whose course was later swapped is not offered the new course (the third sentence, taken as written; step 22); a started day survives a publish that completes the enrolment (R13); a stricter pass rule does not fail started work (R14); the access check admits a started or credited day (step 23); the re-pin's dry run reads the draft (step 94). Cost: 6.25 days beyond the 2.5 priced in round 3 (step 72). The alternatives weighed are in "Alternatives considered" |
| A31 | A test run's header row, `preview_runs` (round 4) | Preview progress is derived from preview attempts only. One preview-only table holds each test run's ids and dates: which path, or which journey program and version, whose run, and its simulated date. It holds no result and no progress, apart from the activity days marked done inside a journey run (decision A41, from phase G), is not an enrolment, is service role only, and is purged with the run (step 79). It is the one exception to "a test run writes attempt rows only"; without it two test runs of one path could not be told apart and a journey run would have no version or date to read. Alternative: carry the path or program on every preview attempt and have no run row; then a tester can hold only one test run per path or journey at a time and must delete it to start again |
| A32 | The launch mode of a preview (round 4). **Answered by the owner on 2026-11-13: yes.** "I agree that Preview should run in normal course mode and store scores and results. However, all Preview data must remain completely isolated from official learner data, reports, analytics, progress, rewards and completion records." Phase 2 waits for nothing | The condition, complete isolation, is step 80's table, the database's CHECK and trigger, and spec 75. The Browse alternative is not built. Round 4's text follows. Recommended: a Preview and a test run launch in Normal mode, so every package, KIVO 2.0 included, records a score and a completion on the preview row, which is what Preview Reports and a test run need; the row is kept unofficial by its context, which the database enforces. This changes ADR 0006 step 17 for previews and closes that record's question O-1 with no hold. Alternative: previews stay in Browse; KIVO 2.0 previews then show no score or completion in Preview Reports, and a test run through KIVO 2.0 steps advances on "opened and closed" (step 78) |
| A33 | Rows flagged as previews by role before this record (round 4). **Answered in part by the owner on 2026-11-13; one clarification is asked, and phase 1 waits for it** | The owner: "Please preserve the old staff records that have been classified as Preview based on the user's role. They must be automatically deleted under the new 90-day cleanup rule." Some of these rows are real learner work (the rows of anyone who held a staff role when 0100 was pasted; some hold a result, a used grant, a points row or a day credit), and the owner's A36 answer of the same message says "Official learner records and completion history must remain protected." **The question: which of these is meant?** (a) Keep every by-role row for good, with no automatic deletion. (b) **Recommended:** by-role rows older than the retention limit are deleted automatically only when nothing official rests on them (no result in any launch mode, no grant, no points row, no day credit, no completion record, no completion reported to the CRM; "in any launch mode" since the check of the round-5 fold, the text said "made in Normal mode"); rows that have any of these are kept and listed in Preview Reports for the owner's decision. (c) Delete every by-role row at the limit, real results included, which goes against the A36 answer. Option (b) is designed in step 82, behind a switch that stays off until the owner has seen the counts, so the record is ready for (a) or (b); (c) would need the kept test removed and is not designed. Two things the owner should know about (b) before choosing it: it also deletes a by-role attempt that was started as an ordinary attempt and never produced a result (opened, perhaps partly answered, never completed, passed, failed or scored), with what was stored on it; and it widens the permission of A36 from the new preview data to these older rows. If either is unwanted, (a) is the answer, or (b) with every Normal-mode row kept, which the owner may ask for in one sentence. **A third thing, added by the check of the round-5 fold (2026-11-13).** Since 0100 every launch by an admin, a super owner or an analyst is stored in Browse mode, and a SCORM or A.1 course still records a score and a completion in that mode. Such a row can be a staff member's test, or an analyst's real attempt at a course they were assigned; nothing on the row tells the two apart. As first written, (b) would have deleted these rows once aged, completed analyst attempts with scores among them. The design now keeps every by-role row that has a result, in either mode, and deletes only rows that never produced one. The price: staff tests that ran to a result are kept and listed past 90 days as well. If the owner wants those deleted too, one sentence is enough ("also delete by-role results made in Browse mode"), knowing it can remove an analyst's real result; Q10 shows the count by mode and result before the choice. On production the count is expected to be 0. Phase 1 waits for this one answer because the purge function, the switch and the test are in 0107. Round 4's text follows; its "never deleted by the purge" holds under (a) only. Recommended: kept, labelled `legacy_role`, counted nowhere official, never deleted by the purge or the manual delete, because some are real results (an analyst's official attempt, work done before a promotion). Counted before the paste; expected 0 on production; on staging the owner may reset them as test data. Two effects on the people who own such rows, stated so the owner answers with them (step 82; Q10 counts both): a person whose real earlier work was flagged holds no official result on that course after 0107, so their next Start is a first official attempt beside a result that is now hidden; and an open by-role row is not resumed by Start and is closed by that person's next Preview. Stated exceptions to the 90-day rule for these older rows: their points rows cannot be deleted by anyone, and what was already forwarded or emailed cannot be recalled; a day credit or points such a row earned stay with the person, so the day is not asked for again and the course pays no second time (step 82). Alternative: the owner reviews the counts and orders a one-off, logged deletion of those that are plainly tests; this record does not plan it |
| A34 | The 90-day rule against backups and other copies (round 4). **Answered by the owner on 2026-11-13: "Yes, subject to verification". Open for three points only; production's first course waits for them and for the lifecycle rule** | The owner: "I agree with the proposed retention approach. Live Preview data can be deleted earlier, for example after 60 days, while Preview data in B2 backups must not be retained for more than 90 days." The owner's reading of the bucket: "The currently selected option is "Keep all versions of the file (default)." As per the setting shown, all file versions are retained, with no automatic version deletion configured through this option." So no backup copy is ever deleted today and the 90-day ceiling is **not** met for backups until the owner sets a lifecycle rule (step 83). Also the owner's: "restoring a backup does not permanently reintroduce Preview records that have already crossed their retention period. The expiry cleanup should be applied after a restore" (step 97). **Still asked:** (1) confirm that the bucket read is the database backup bucket and not the content bucket; (2) L; (3) R, with L + R + 1 ≤ 90. Recommended L = 30 and R = 56 (eight weeks of backups); with the owner's example L = 60, R = 28 (four weeks). The shorter R is, the shorter the backup history of official data too. Round 4's text follows. Recommended, option (a) of step 83: a lifecycle rule on the database backup bucket and a live retention short enough that the last backup copy is gone by day 90 (L + R + 1 ≤ 90; default L = 30 days, to be fixed when the owner has read the bucket). Alternative (b): keep previews live for 90 days and record backups as an exception. Either way the owner reads the bucket's rules and the log stores' retention first; the repository states none of them |
| A35 | Previews are never forwarded to a tenant LRS, and never reach the CRM (round 4) | The LMS cannot delete from a tenant's system, so the only way to meet "nowhere over 90 days" there is not to send. The forwarder, every sweeper source, "Resend all history" and the abandon path (`lib/lrs/abandoned.ts`, which every restart, close and language switch calls; added by the independent review of round 4) exclude the preview context; the CRM feeds and the webhook already do through the official rule. The source scan asserts it for every reader under `lib/lrs/` (steps 62, 80). This is the architect's decision, not the owner's words: the owner said "CRM/Yoddha updates" and "anywhere in the system"; the tenant LRS follows from the second. Alternative: forward with a preview marker and record the tenant LRS as an exception |
| A36 | Deleting preview data: the mechanism, and the departure from principle 3 (round 4). **Answered by the owner on 2026-11-13: allowed.** "I agree to allow deletion of Preview data, with a dry run before deletion and an audit record for every deletion. This permission must apply only to Preview/test data. Official learner records and completion history must remain protected." | The conditions, and where each holds (reworded by the check of the round-5 fold, 2026-11-13; the text said "a dry run before every delete, one audit row per deletion", which overstated both). A dry run comes before every delete a person starts, each button and each product delete. The nightly retention run and the run after a restore are automatic: they have no dry run before them and report their counts in the answer and the log. One audit row is written for every deletion the purge function makes; four cascades delete preview rows outside it and leave none: an organisation's delete, a user's delete, and a Preview started in the same moment an admin deletes its course or its language (step 85). Whether the two conditions are met by this is reading R22, for the owner to object to. Preview and test data only: the selection predicate. The last sentence is why A33 is put back with a clarification (step 82). Round 4's text follows. One SQL function, `purge_preview_data`, is the only code that deletes an attempt row directly; the cascades of an organisation, a user and a preview-only course remain as today, and the three product-delete routes purge first so the log row exists (step 85). It can select only preview rows of the three new kinds; it never deletes an attempt that an official side effect points at, sets it aside, reports it and deletes the rest (step 81; the round-4 text refused the whole organisation, which one append-only row would have blocked for good); a test run has its own clock; a failed or partial purge turns the nightly refresh's heartbeat red; it has a dry run that returns counts; it writes an audit row with ids and counts only; a rehearsal proves no other row is touched. The manual delete calls it from one admin route; the automatic delete is the same function called by the existing nightly refresh; no new cron route (step 81). This departs from `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 3, for preview data only, because the owner's R7 asks for it. Alternative: no deletion, with previews hidden after 90 days; it does not meet the owner's words |
| A37 | A record of a language switch, and a switch inside a path or journey (round 4) | An audit row per switch naming the attempts it closed; the redirect and a "Change language" entry that keep the product context; honest confirm copy (step 27). Alternative: a `closed_reason` column on the attempt, a schema change for the same information |
| A38 | A completed journey on the learner dashboard (round 4) | A "Journey completed" card for the latest completed enrolment, so a completion, an admin's publish included, is "reflected correctly in the learner dashboard" (the owner, A20). Today the dashboard only stops showing the journey. Alternative: record that the dashboard shows nothing and the journey page and certificate carry the completed state |
| A39 | The journey editor's operations and two new database functions (round 4) | Move, insert and remove are operations on days with ids; the curriculum save goes through `journey_save_days` and the publish to all through `journey_repin_enrollments`, each one transaction (steps 92, 94). Both change how an existing route writes. A removed day is kept without a number, so nothing that names it loses its parent |
| A40 | Corrections from the schedule read (round 4) | Where the timed refresh runs, and its price (step 71); the staging paste windows (step 73); one more precondition for 0108, every open branch up to date (step 20); the nightly's timeout before new specs join (step 73); the attribution of "never while another harness runs". The 0108 paste stays in phase 2's gate as agreed; moving it is proposal P6 |
| A41 | An activity day inside a journey test run (independent review of round 4; phase G) | D13's activity days are credited with no attempt, so a test run built on preview attempts alone could not pass one. From phase G the tester marks such a day done inside the run; the mark is a day id in `preview_runs.done_activity_day_ids`, a column 0107 creates empty, purged with the run, written to no official table (step 89). It is an exception to "a test run's progress is derived from preview attempts only", limited to activity days. Half a day in phase G. Alternative: a test run skips activity days and says so, and they are tested on staging with a learner account; the column is then dropped from 0107, so an objection must come before phase 1 |

Rows A28 to A30 below were made in the final review of round 3 and keep their place after the round-4 rows; the numbers, not the order, identify them.

| # | Decision | Recommendation and consequence |
|---|---|---|
| A28 | New CRM keys are version 2 (final review) | In 0107's run-once block the column is added with default 1, labelling existing keys, and the default is then set to 2; the key route is unchanged and writes with the user's session, which cannot name the column (steps 8, 53, 74a). Chosen over creating keys with the service client: no route change, no new service-role write, and the label cannot be chosen by the caller. Consequence: every key created after the paste is version 2, on staging from the phase 1 paste, where it takes effect when 9a deploys; a consumer that must stay on version 1 keeps its existing key |
| A29 | Who the CRM feeds list, and unreleased path steps (final review) | Both versions keep today's contract (ADR 0020 decision 2, `docs/CRM_INTEGRATION.md`): the feeds expand the three enrolment tables themselves and list what is assigned, released or not; `steps[]` lists every step; each step, and each course row in version 2, gains `released` and `release_at` (step 55). Alternative: version 2 lists only what the learner can open, as the reports count; it would make version 2 drop rows version 1 returns and needs its own paragraph in the notice |
| A30 | No start-date floor or calendar ceiling when a journey attempt is credited (final review) | The tagged branch has neither today. The day is checked when the attempt is launched, and the attempt's context cannot change, so the row itself is the proof; a second check at completion would refuse an earned credit after a late save, a late `passed` or a sweep (step 13). The day's course, the active enrolment, the owner and the pass rule are checked on every call, the sweep's included. Alternative: re-check the floor at completion and leave refused rows for an admin |

## Review

The independent review of 2026-11-12 read this record, the filed ADR 0023 draft and the dated notes against the owner's answers, the plan, the technical detail and `main` 1aeb339. It made 50 findings in three passes: fidelity to the owner's answers (10), accuracy against the code and migrations (17), and whether an executor can build from the text (23). Nine matters were found more than once (eight by two findings, one by three); each is folded once and every id is given, so the table has 40 rows for 50 ids. The fold was made on 2026-11-13.

How the evidence was checked. The architect's session has no shell, so nothing was run: no `git` command, no test, no rehearsal, no database. Each finding's evidence was re-read in the checkout at `main` 1aeb339 or in the named source. As before, the architect's session has no shell: no `git` command (so the list of files changed on this branch was taken from the notes this record names in step 77, not from a diff), no test, no rehearsal, no database.
